him as the liberator from the power of sin.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["Behold, the Lamb of God, who takes
away the sin of the world!"]{.small}

[Jn 1:29]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[In many and various ways God spoke of
old to our fathers by the prophets; but in these last days he has spoken
to us by a Son.]{.small}

[Heb 1:1-2]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["The Holy Spirit will come upon you,
and the power of the Most High will overshadow you."]{.small}

[Lk 1:35]{.small}

[117 ]{.bold1}[How could the Holy Spirit work in, with, and through
Mary]{.italic}?

[Mary was totally responsive and open to God (Lk 1:38). Thus she was
able to become the "Mother of God" through the working of the Holy
Spirit---and as Christ's Mother to become also the Mother of Christians,
indeed, the Mother of all mankind. \[721-726\]]{.bold1}

Mary made it possible for the Holy Spirit to work the miracle of all
miracles: the Incarnation of God. She gave God her Yes: "Behold, I am
the handmaid of the Lord; let it be to me according to your word" (Lk
1:38). Strengthened by the Holy Spirit, she went with Jesus through
thick and thin, even to the foot of the Cross. There Jesus gave her to
us all as our Mother (Jn 19:25-27).
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[80-85](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos156288),
[479](#YOUCAT_split_021.html_filepos782368)

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[PENTECOST]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Greek [pentecoste]{.italic} = "the fiftieth" day after Easter):
originally a feast on which Israel celebrated the establishment of the
covenant with God on Mount Sinai. Through the Pentecost event in
Jerusalem, it became for Christians the feast of the Holy
Spirit.]{.small}

[118]{.bold1} [What happened on Pentecost]{.italic}?

[Fifty days after his Resurrection, the Lord sent the Holy Spirit down
from heaven upon his disciples. The age of the →
[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) began.
\[731-733\]]{.bold1}

On Pentecost the Holy Spirit transformed fearful apostles into
courageous witnesses to Christ. In a very short time, thousands had
themselves baptized: it was the birthday of the Church. The miracle of
the languages on
→[P[ENTECOST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos212687) shows that
the Church is there for all peoples from the very beginning: She is
universal (= the Latin term for the Greek [kat' holon]{.italic},
catholic) and missionary. She speaks to all men, overcomes ethnic and
linguistic barriers, and can be understood by all. To this day the Holy
Spirit is the "soul" of the Church, the essential principle of her life.

[119]{.bold1} [What does the Holy Spirit do in the Church]{.italic}?

[The Holy Spirit builds up the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) and impels
her. He reminds her of her
→[[MISSION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos38075). He calls
people into her service and sends them the necessary gifts. He leads us
ever deeper into communion with the Triune God. \[733-741,
747\]]{.bold1}

Even though the Church during her long history has often seemed
"abandoned by all good spirits", the Holy Spirit has been at work in her
despite all the human failings and inadequacies. The mere fact of her
two-thousand-year existence and the many saints of all eras and cultures
are the visible proof of his presence. The Holy Spirit is the one who
maintains the Church as a whole in the truth and leads her ever deeper
into the knowledge of God. It is the Holy Spirit who works in the
→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) and brings
Sacred Scripture to life for us. Even today he gives his gifts of grace
(→[[CHARISMS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos205015)) to those
who are completely receptive to him.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[203-206](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos356041)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[And they were all filled with the Holy
Spirit and began to speak in other tongues, as the Spirit gave them
utterance. . . . Each one heard them speaking in his own
language.]{.small}

[Acts 2:4b, 6]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["I have yet many things to say to you,
but you cannot bear them now. When the Spirit of truth comes, he will
guide you into all the truth."]{.small}

[Jn 16:12-13a]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Our understanding is limited: thus the
Spirit's mission is to introduce the Church, in an ever new way from
generation to generation, into the greatness of Christ's
mystery.]{.small}

[POPE BENEDICT XVI,\
May 7, 2005]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[FRUITS OF THE HOLY
SPIRIT]{.bold1}]{.small}

[Love, joy, peace, patience, kindness, goodness, faithfulness,
gentleness, self-control (Gal 5:22-23).]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[THE WORKS OF THE
FLESH]{.bold1}]{.small}

According to Gal 5:19ff., these include: immorality, impurity,
licentiousness, idolatry, sorcery, enmity, strife, jealousy, anger,
selfishness, dissension, division, envy, drunkenness, carousing, and the
like.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The working of the Holy Spirit deepens
our interior life. Connected with this is a search for silence, an
aversion to inopportune speech.]{.small}

[HEINRICH SPAEMANN\
(1903-2001, Catholic priest and spiritual writer)]{.small}

[120 ]{.bold1}[What does the Holy Spirit do in my life]{.italic}?

[The Holy Spirit makes me receptive to God; he teaches me to pray and
helps me to be there for others. \[738-741\]]{.bold1}

Augustine calls the Holy Spirit "The quiet guest of our soul". Anyone
who wants to sense his presence must be quiet. Often this Guest speaks
very softly within us and with us, for instance, in the voice of our
conscience or through other interior and exterior promptings. Being a
"temple of the Holy Spirit" means being there, body and soul, for this
Guest, for [God in us]{.italic}. Our body is therefore God's living
room, so to speak. The more receptive we are to the Holy Spirit in us,
the more he becomes the master of our life, the sooner he will bestow on
us even today his →
[[CHARISMS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos205015) for the
upbuilding of the Church. And so, instead of the → [[WORKS OF THE
FLESH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos216791), the → [FRUITS OF
THE S[PIRIT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos216517) grow in us.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[290-291](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos501312),
[295-297](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos508050),
[310-311](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos525959)

"I Believe in . . . the Holy Catholic Church"

[121 ]{.bold1}[What does "Church" mean]{.italic}?

[The Greek word for
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) is
"[ekklesia]{.italic}" = those who are called forth. All of us who are
baptized and believe in God are called forth by the Lord. Together we
are the Church. Christ is, as Paul says, the Head of the Church. We are
his body. \[748-757\]]{.bold1}

When we receive the sacraments and hear God's Word, Christ is in us and
we are in him---that is the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459). The intimate
communion of life with Jesus that is shared personally by all the
baptized is described in Sacred Scripture by a wealth of images: Here it
speaks about the People of God and in another passage about the Bride of
Christ; now the Church is called Mother, and again she is God's family,
or she is compared with a wedding feast. Never is the Church a mere
institution, never just the "official Church" that we could do without.
We will be upset by the mistakes and defects in the → C[HURCH]{.small},
but we can never distance ourselves from her, because God has made an
irrevocable decision to love her and does not forsake her despite all
the sins of her members. The Church is God's presence among us men. That
is why we must love her.

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[CHURCH]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from the Greek [kyriake]{.italic} = belonging to the Lord): consists
of those called together from all nations (from Greek [ex kaleo,
ekklesia]{.italic}) who through Baptism belong to the Body of
Christ.]{.small}

![](images/00030.jpg){.calibre4}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[He \[Christ\] is the head of the body,
the Church.]{.small}

[Col 1:18a]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The Church is an old woman with many
wrinkles and furrows. But she is my mother. And no one strikes my
mother.]{.small}

[The theologian FR. KARL RAHNER, S.J. (1904-1984), when he heard
unseemly criticism of the Church.]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[So God created man in his own image, in
the image of God he created him.]{.small}

[Gen 1:27]{.small}

[122]{.bold1} [Why does God want there to be a Church]{.italic}?

[God wills the →[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459)
because he wants to redeem us, not individually, but together. He wants
to make all mankind his people. \[758-781, 802-804\]]{.bold1}

No one gets to heaven by the asocial route. Someone who thinks only
about himself and the salvation of his own soul is living a-socially.
That is impossible both in heaven and on earth. God himself is not
a-social; he is not a solitary, self-sufficient being. The Triune God in
himself is "social", a communion, an eternal exchange of love. Patterned
after God, man also is designed for relationship, exchange, sharing, and
love. We are responsible for one another.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Then the Lord said to Cain, "Where is
Abel your brother?" He said, "I do not know; am I my brother's
keeper?"]{.small}

[Gen 4:9]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["As the Father has sent me, even so I
send you."]{.small}

[Jn 20:21b]{.small}

[123]{.bold1} [What is the task of the Church]{.italic}?

[The →[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459)'s task is
to make the kingdom of God, which has already begun with Jesus,
germinate and grow in all nations. \[763-769, 774-776, 780\]]{.bold1}

Wherever Jesus went, heaven touched earth: the kingdom of God was
inaugurated, a kingdom of peace and justice. The
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) serves this
kingdom of God. She is not an end in herself. She must carry on what
Jesus started. She should act as Jesus would act. She continues the
sacred signs of Jesus (the
→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200)). She
hands on Jesus' words. That is why the Church, for all her weakness, is
a formidable bit of heaven on earth.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[We must be saved together. We must come
to God together. Together, we must present ourselves before him. . . .
What would God say to us if some of us were to return without the
others?]{.small}

[CHARLES PÉGUY\
(1873-1914, French poet)]{.small}

[124 ]{.bold1}[Why is the Church more than an institution]{.italic}?

[The Church is more than an institution because she is a
→[M[YSTERY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos155806) that is
simultaneously human and divine. \[770-773, 779\]]{.bold1}

True love does not blind a person but rather makes him see. With regard
to the →[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459), this
is precisely the case: Viewed from outside, the Church is only a
historical institution with historical achievements, but also mistakes
and even crimes---a Church of sinners. But that is not looking deep
enough. After all, Christ became so involved with us sinners that he
never abandons his Church, even if we were to betray him daily. This
inseparable union of the human and the divine, of sin and grace, is the
mystery of the Church. Seen with the eyes of faith, the Church is
therefore indestructibly holy.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[132](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos238814)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[But we have this treasure in earthen
vessels, to show that the transcendent power belongs to God and not to
us.]{.small}

[2 Cor 4:7]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Do all things without grumbling or
questioning, that you may be blameless and innocent, children of God
without blemish in the midst of a crooked and perverse generation, among
whom you shine as lights in the world.]{.small}

[Phil 2:14-15]{.small}

[125 ]{.bold1}[What is unique about the People of God]{.italic}?

[The founder of this people is God the Father. Its leader is Jesus
Christ. Its source of strength is the Holy Spirit. The entryway to the
People of God is Baptism. Its dignity is the freedom of the children of
God. Its law is love. If this people remains faithful to God and seeks
first the kingdom of God, it changes the world. \[781-786\]]{.bold1}

In the midst of all the peoples on earth, there is one people that is
like no other. It is subject to no one but God alone. It is supposed to
be like salt, which adds flavor; like yeast, which permeates everything;
like light, which drives away the darkness. Anyone who belongs to the
People of God must count on coming into conflict with people who deny
God's existence and disregard his commandments. In the freedom of the
children of God, however, we have nothing to fear, not even death.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The Church cannot behave like a
business that changes its product when the demand for it
decreases.]{.small}

[KARL CARDINAL LEHMANN\
(b. 1936)]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["But seek first his kingdom and his
righteousness, and all these things shall be yours as well."]{.small}

[Mt 6:33]{.small}

[126]{.bold1} [What does it mean to say that the Church is the "Body of
Christ"]{.italic}?

[Above all through the
→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) of Baptism
and Holy →[E[UCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475),
an inseparable union comes about between Jesus Christ and Christians.
The union is so strong that it joins him and us like the head and
members of a human body and makes us one. \[787-795\]
]{.bold1}![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[146](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos265962),
[175](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos313358),
[179](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos319934),
[200](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos352589),
[208](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos364532),
[217](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos391628)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[He is the head of the body, the
Church.]{.small}

[Col 1:18]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Loving Christ is the same as loving the
Church.]{.small}

[BROTHER ROGER SCHUTZ]{.small}

[(1915-2005)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[I would create the whole universe
again, just to hear you say that you love me.]{.small}

[Jesus in a vision to\
ST. TERESA OF AVILA]{.small}

[(1515-1582)]{.small}

[127 ]{.bold1}[What does it mean to say that the Church is the "Bride of
Christ"]{.italic}?

[Jesus Christ loves the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) as a
bridegroom loves his bride. He binds himself to her forever and gives
his life for her. \[796\]]{.bold1}

Anyone who has ever been in love has some idea of what love is. Jesus
knows it and calls himself a bridegroom who lovingly and longingly
courts his bride and desires to celebrate the feast of love with her. We
are his Bride, the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459). In the
→[O[LD]{.small}
T[ESTAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos45534) God's love for
his people is compared to the love between husband and wife. If Jesus
seeks the love of each one of us, how often is he then [unhappily in
love]{.italic}---that is to say, with all those who want nothing to do
with his love and do not reciprocate it?!

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[My beloved speaks and says to me:
"Arise, my love, my fair one, and come away."]{.small}

[Song 2:10]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[The Spirit and the Bride say, "Come."
And let him who hears say, "Come." . . . Come, Lord Jesus!]{.small}

[Rev 22:17, 20]{.small}

[128 ]{.bold1}[What does it mean to say that the Church is the "Temple
of the Holy Spirit"]{.italic}?

[The →[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) is the
place in the world where the Holy Spirit is completely present.
\[797-801, 809\]]{.bold1}

The people of Israel worshipped God in the Temple of Jerusalem. This
temple no longer exists. It has been replaced by the Church, which is
not limited to a particular place. "Where two or three are gathered in
my name, there am I in the midst of them" (Mt 18:20). What makes her
alive is the Spirit of Christ: He lives in the Word of Sacred Scripture
and is present in the sacred signs of the
→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200). He loves
in the hearts of believers and speaks in their prayers. He leads them
and bestows charisms on them---simple gifts as well as extraordinary
ones. Anyone who enters into a relationship with the Holy Spirit can
experience true miracles even today.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[113-120](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos205878),
[203-205](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos356041),
[310-311](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos525959)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[For we are the temple of the living
God; as God said, "I will live in them and move among them, and I will
be their God, and they shall be my people."]{.small}

[2 Cor 6:16]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Most people have no idea what God would
make of them if they would only place themselves at his
disposal.]{.small}

[ST. IGNATIUS LOYOLA\
(1491-1556, founder of the Jesuits)]{.small}

I Believe in One, Holy, Catholic, and Apostolic Church

[129 ]{.bold1}[Why can there be only one Church]{.italic}?

[Just as there is only one Christ, there can be only one Body of Christ,
only one Bride of Christ, and therefore only one
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) of Jesus
Christ. He is the Head, the Church is the Body. Together they form the
"whole Christ" (St. Augustine). Just as the body has many members yet is
one, so too the one Church consists of and is made up of many particular
churches (dioceses). Together they form the whole Christ. \[811-816,
866, 870\]]{.bold1}

Jesus built his Church on the foundation of the
→[[APOSTLES]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos38443). This
foundation supports her to this day. The faith of the apostles was
handed down from generation to generation under the leadership of the
Pope, the Petrine ministry, "which presides in charity" (St. Ignatius of
Antioch). The
→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200), too,
which Jesus entrusted to the apostolic college, still work with their
original power.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[There is one body and one Spirit, just
as you were called to the one hope that belongs to your call, one Lord,
one faith, one baptism, one God and Father of us all, who is above all
and through all and in all.]{.small}

[Eph 4:4-6]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[For with this Church \[the Christian
community in Rome\], by reason of its preeminence, the whole Church,
that is the faithful everywhere, must necessarily be in accord, because
in it the tradition of the apostles has always been preserved.]{.small}

[ST. IRENAEUS OF LYONS]{.small}

[(ca. 135-202)]{.small}

[130 ]{.bold1}[Are non-Catholic Christians our sisters and brothers
also]{.italic}?

[All baptized persons belong to the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) of Jesus
Christ. That is why also those Christians who find themselves separated
from the full communion of the Catholic Church are rightly called
Christians and are therefore our sisters and brothers.
\[817-819\]]{.bold1}

Instances of separation from the one Church of Christ came about through
falsifications of Christ's teaching, human failings, and a lack of
willingness to be reconciled---usually on the part of representatives on
both sides. Christians today are in no way guilty for the historical
divisions of the Church. The Holy Spirit also works for the salvation of
mankind in the →[C[HURCHES AND ECCLESIAL
COMMUNITIES]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos236322) that are
separated from the Catholic Church. All of the gifts present there, for
example, Sacred Scripture,
→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200), faith,
hope, love, and other charisms, come originally from Christ. Where the
Spirit of Christ lives, there is an inner dynamic leading toward
"reunion", because what belongs together wants to grow together.

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[CHURCHES AND ECCLESIAL
COMMUNITIES]{.bold1}]{.small}

[Many Christian communities on earth call themselves churches. According
to the Catholic understanding, only those in which the sacraments of
Jesus Christ have been preserved in their entirety have remained
"Church". This is true especially of the Orthodox and Eastern Churches.
In the "ecclesial communities" that resulted from the Protestant
Reformation, all the sacraments have not been preserved.]{.small}

[131 ]{.bold1}[What must we do for the unity of Christians]{.italic}?

[In word and deed we must obey Christ, who expressly wills "that they
may all be one" (Jn 17:21). \[820-822\]]{.bold1}

Christian unity is the business of all Christians, regardless of how
young or old they are. Unity was one of Jesus' most important concerns.
He prayed to the Father, "that they may all be one . . . so that the
world may believe that you have sent me" (Jn 17:21). Divisions are like
wounds on the Body of Christ; they hurt and fester. Divisions lead to
enmities and weaken the faith and credibility of Christians. Overcoming
the scandal of separation requires the conversion of all concerned but
also knowledge of one's own faith convictions, dialogues with others,
and especially prayer in common, and collaboration among Christians in
serving mankind. Those in authority in the Church must not let the
theological dialogue be interrupted.

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[ECUMENISM]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Greek [oikumene]{.italic} = the inhabited earth, the globe):
efforts to unify divided Christians.]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["Say to all the congregation of the
sons of Israel, You shall be holy, for I the Lord your God am
holy."]{.small}

[Lev 19:2]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[There is only one tragedy in the end,
not to have been a saint.]{.small}

[LÉON BLOY\
(1846-1917)]{.small}

[132 ]{.bold1}[Why is the Church holy]{.italic}?

[The Church is holy, not because all her members are supposedly holy,
but rather because God is holy and is at work in her. All the members of
the Church are sanctified by Baptism. \[823-829\]]{.bold1}

Whenever we allow ourselves to be touched by the Triune God, we grow in
love and become [holy]{.italic} and whole. The saints are lovers---not
because they are able to love so well, but because God has touched them.
They pass on the love they have received from God to other people in
their own, often original way. Once God takes them home, they also
sanctify the Church, because they "spend their heaven" supporting us on
our path to →[[HOLINESS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos239756).
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[124](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos224378)

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[HOLINESS]{.bold1}]{.small}

[The most essential attribute of God. In Latin there is a word,
[fanum]{.italic}, for what is divine, pure, and set apart from profane,
everyday things. God is the "totally Other", the Holy One of Israel (Is
30:15); Jesus comes into the world as the "Holy One of God" (Jn 6:69).
In him we can see what "holiness" is: to love unconditionally and
mercifully, in a helping and healing way, up to perfection in the Cross
and Resurrection.]{.small}

[133 ]{.bold1}[Why is the Church called catholic]{.italic}?

["Catholic" (Greek [kat' holon]{.italic}) means related to the whole.
The →[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) is
catholic because Christ called her to profess the [whole]{.italic}
faith, to preserve [all]{.italic} the
→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200), to
administer them and proclaim the Good News to [all]{.italic}; and he
sent her to [all]{.italic} nations. \[830-831, 849-856\]]{.bold1}

[134 ]{.bold1}[Who belongs to the Catholic Church]{.italic}?

[Anyone who, in union with the
→[P[OPE]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254325) and the bishops,
is united to Jesus Christ through profession of the Catholic faith and
reception of the
→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) is in full
communion with the Catholic Church. \[836-838\]]{.bold1}

God willed [one]{.italic}
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) for all.
Unfortunately we Christians have been unfaithful to this wish of Christ.
Nevertheless, even today we are still deeply united with one another by
our faith and common Baptism.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Just as there are also uncatholic
things in the Catholic Church, so too we can find something catholic
even outside the Catholic Church. Many who seem to be outside are
inside; many who seem to be inside are outside.]{.small}

[ST. AUGUSTINE]{.small}

[(354-430)]{.small}

[135 ]{.bold1}[What is the relation between the Church and the
Jews]{.italic}?

[Jews are the "older brethren" of Christians, because God loved them
first and spoke to them first. Jesus Christ as man is a Jew, and this
fact unites us. The Church recognizes in him the Son of the living God,
and this fact separates us. In awaiting the final coming of the Messiah
we are one. \[839-840\]]{.bold1}

The Jewish faith is the root of our faith. The Sacred Scripture of the
Jews, which we call the Old Testament, is the first part of our Sacred
Scripture. The Judeo-Christian concept of man and morality, which is
informed by the Ten Commandments, is the foundation of Western
democracies. It is shameful that for hundreds of years Christians were
unwilling to admit this close relation to Judaism and for
pseudo-theological reasons helped foment an anti-Semitism that all too
often had lethal effects. During the Holy Year 2000, Pope John Paul II
expressly asked forgiveness for this. The Second Vatican Council clearly
states that the Jews as a people cannot be charged with any collective
guilt for the crucifixion of Christ.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[96-97](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos179117),
[335](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos559360)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["Do not think that I have come to
abolish the law and the prophets; I have come not to abolish them but to
fulfil them."]{.small}

[Mt 5:17]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The rulers of the Third Reich wanted to
crush the entire Jewish people. . . . Deep down, those vicious
criminals, by wiping out this people, wanted to kill the God who called
Abraham, who spoke on Sinai, and laid down principles to serve as a
guide for mankind, principles that are eternally valid.]{.small}

[POPE BENEDICT XVI,\
May 28, 2006, in Auschwitz-Birkenau]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[For us the Jewish religion is not
something external but in a certain way intrinsically belongs to our
religion. Consequently we have relations with it unlike those with any
other religion. You are our privileged brothers and, one might say, our
older brothers.]{.small}

[POPE JOHN PAUL II\
(1920-2005), during his visit to the Great Synagogue in Rome,
1986]{.small}

![](images/00031.jpg){.calibre4}

[136 ]{.bold1}[How does the Church view other religions]{.italic}?

[The →[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) respects
everything in other religions that is good and true. She respects and
promotes freedom of religion as a human right. Yet she knows that Jesus
Christ is the sole redeemer of all mankind. He alone is "the way, and
the truth, and the life" (Jn 14:6). \[841-848\]]{.bold1}

Whoever seeks God is close to us Christians. There is a special degree
of "affinity" to the Muslims. Like Judaism and Christianity, Islam is
one of the monotheistic religions
(→[[MONOTHEISM]{.small})](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos76826). The
Muslims, too, revere God the Creator and Abraham as their father in
faith. Jesus is considered a great prophet in the Qur'an; Mary, his
Mother, as the mother of a prophet. The Church teaches that all men who
by no fault of their own do not know Christ and his Church but sincerely
seek God and follow the voice of their conscience can attain eternal
salvation. However, anyone who has recognized that Jesus Christ is "the
way, and the truth, and the life" but is unwilling to follow him cannot
find salvation by other paths. This is what is meant by the saying,
[Extra ecclesiam nulla salus]{.italic} (outside of the Church there is
no salvation).
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[199](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos350800)

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[RELIGIOUS FREEDOM]{.bold1}]{.small}

[The right of every man to follow his conscience in choosing and
practicing his religion. Acknowledgment of religious freedom is not
saying that all religions are equal or equally true.]{.small}

[[TWELVE APOSTLES]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(Greek [apostolos]{.italic} = someone sent, messenger): "The names of
the twelve apostles are these: first, Simon, who is called Peter, and
Andrew his brother; James the son of Zebedee, and John his brother;
Philip and Bartholomew; Thomas and Matthew the tax collector; James the
son of Alphaeus, and Thaddaeus; Simon the Cananaean, and Judas Iscariot,
who betrayed him" (Mt 10:2-4).]{.small}

![](images/00032.jpg){.calibre4}

[137]{.bold1} [Why is the Church called apostolic]{.italic}?

[The →[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) is called
apostolic because she was founded by the apostles, holds fast to their
Tradition, and is governed by their successors. \[857-860, 869,
877\]]{.bold1}

Jesus called the
→[[APOSTLES]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos38443) to be his
closest collaborators. They were his eyewitnesses. After his
Resurrection, he appeared to them repeatedly. He bestowed on them the
Holy Spirit and sent them as his authoritative messengers to all the
world. They assured unity in the early Church. They conferred their
mission and authority upon their successors, the bishops, through the
laying on of hands. This process is called →[[APOSTOLIC
SUCCESSION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos250088).
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[92](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos173422)

[138]{.bold1}[How is the one, holy, catholic, and apostolic Church
structured]{.italic}?

[In the Church there are the
→[[LAITY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos250591) and clerics
(→[[CLERGY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos251934)). As children
of God, they are of equal dignity. They have equally important but
different tasks. The mission of the laity is to direct the whole world
toward the kingdom of God. In addition, there are the ordained ministers
(clerics), who have the duties of ecclesiastical governance, teaching,
and sanctification. In both states of life, there are Christians who
place themselves at God's disposal in a special way through celibacy,
poverty, and obedience (for example, consecrated religious). \[871-876,
934, 935\]]{.bold1}

Every Christian has the duty to bear witness to the Gospel by his own
life. But God walks a special path with each person. Some he sends as
laymen, so that they might build up the kingdom of God by their family
and occupation in the midst of the world. For this purpose, he bestows
on them in Baptism and
→[C[ONFIRMATION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos353709) all the
necessary gifts of the Holy Spirit. Others he entrusts with the pastoral
ministry; they are to govern, teach, and sanctify his people. No one can
take this duty upon himself; the Lord himself must send him on his way
with his divine power through Holy Orders, so that he can act in the
place of Christ and administer the
→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200).
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[259](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos451543)

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[APOSTOLIC SUCCESSION]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(Latin [successio]{.italic} = sequence, appointment of a replacement):
the unbroken series of bishops since the apostles, their successors in
the episcopal ministry. As Jesus conferred his authority on his
apostles, so it is passed on since then from bishop to bishop through
the imposition of hands and prayer, until the Lord comes again.]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[LAITY]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Greek [laos]{.italic} = people): the common state of life in the
Church: baptized, non-ordained Christians who belong to the People of
God.]{.small}

[139]{.bold1} [What is the lay vocation]{.italic}?

[The → [LAITY]{.small} are sent to engage in society so that the kingdom
of God can grow among men. \[897-913, 940-943\]]{.bold1}

A lay person is not a second-class Christian, for he shares in the
priestly ministry of Christ (the universal priesthood). He sees to it
that the people in his walk of life (in school, family and work) come to
know the Gospel and learn to love Christ. Through his faith he leaves a
mark on society, business, and politics. He supports the life of the
Church, for instance, by becoming a lector or an extraordinary minister,
by volunteering as a group leader, or by serving on church committees
and councils (for example, the parish council or the board of directors
of an institution). Young people especially should give serious thought
to the question of what place God might want them to have in the Church.

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[CLERGY]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Greek [kleroi]{.italic} = share or inheritance): the state of
ordained men in the Church.]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["For I know the plans I have for you,"
says the Lord, "plans for welfare and not for evil, to give you a future
and a hope."]{.small}

[Jer 29:11]{.small}

![](images/00033.jpg){.calibre4}

[140]{.bold1} [Why is the Church not a democratic
organization]{.italic}?

[Democracy operates on the principle that all power comes from the
people. In the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459), however, all
power comes from Christ. That is why the Church has a hierarchical
structure. At the same time, however, Christ gave her a collegial
structure as well. \[874-879\]]{.bold1}

The [hierarchical]{.italic} element in the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) consists in
the fact that Christ himself is the one who acts in the Church when
ordained ministers, by God's grace, do or give something that they could
not do or give by themselves, in other words, when they administer the
→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) in
Christ's place and teach with his authority. The [collegial]{.italic}
element in the Church consists in the fact that Christ entrusted the
entire faith to a group of twelve apostles, whose successors govern the
Church, with the Pope, the Petrine ministry presiding. Given this
collegial approach, councils are an indispensable part of the Church.
Yet even in other administrative bodies of the Church, in synods and
councils, the manifold gifts of the Spirit and the universality of the
Church throughout the world can be fruitful.

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[HIERARCHY]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Greek [hieros]{.italic} and [arché]{.italic} = holy origin): the
gradated structure of the Church under Christ, from whom all power and
authority proceeds.]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[POPE]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Greek [pappas]{.italic} = father): successor of the apostle
Peter, Bishop of Rome. Because Peter was the first among the apostles,
the Pope, as his successor, presides over the college of bishops. As
Christ's Vicar or representative, he is the supreme pastor, priest and
teacher of the Church.]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[BISHOP]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Greek [episkopein]{.italic} = to supervise): successor of the
apostles; leader of a diocese (local Church); as a member of the college
of bishops, under the leadership of the Pope, the bishop has a share in
the responsibility for the universal Church.]{.small}

[141]{.bold1} [What is the Pope's responsibility]{.italic}?

[As successor of St. Peter and head of the college of bishops, the
→[P[OPE]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254325) is the source
and guarantor of the
→[C[HURCH'S]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) unity. He
has the supreme pastoral authority and the final authority in doctrinal
and disciplinary decisions. \[880-882, 936-937\]]{.bold1}

Jesus gave Peter a unique position of preeminence among the apostles.
This made him the supreme authority in the early Church.
→[R[OME]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos258822)---the Local
Church that Peter Led and the place of his martyrdom---became after his
death the internal reference point of the young Church. Every Christian
community had to agree with Rome; that was the standard for the true,
complete, and unadulterated apostolic faith. To this day every
→[B[ISHOP]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254778) of Rome has
been, like Peter, the supreme shepherd of the Church, whose real Head is
Christ. Only in this capacity is the Pope "Christ's Vicar on earth". As
the highest pastoral and doctrinal authority, he watches over the
transmission of the true faith. If need be, he must revoke commissions
to teach doctrine or relieve ordained ministers of their office in cases
of serious failures in matters of faith and morals. Unity in matters of
faith and morals, which is guaranteed by the Church's
→[M[AGISTERIUM]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos40776), or
teaching authority, with the Pope at the head, is one reason for the
remarkable resilience and influence of the Catholic Church.

![](images/00034.jpg){.calibre4}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[DEACON]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(Greek [diakonos]{.italic} = servant, helper): He is ordained for the
ministry (diakonía) of the Word, the liturgy, and charitable works. His
ordination includes the authority to baptize, to preach at Mass, and to
preside at the sacrament of Matrimony.]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[PRIEST]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Greek [presbyteros]{.italic} = elder): co-worker with the bishop
in proclaiming the Gospel and administering the sacraments. He carries
out his ministry in communion with the other priests, under the
leadership of the bishop.]{.small}

[142 ]{.bold1}[Can bishops act and teach against the Pope, or the Pope
against the bishops]{.italic}?

[Bishops cannot act and teach against the
→[P[OPE]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254325), but only with
him. In contrast, the Pope can make decisions in clearly defined cases
even without the approval of the bishops. \[883-885, 880-890\]]{.bold1}

Of course the →[P[OPE]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254325) in
all his decisions is bound by the Church's faith. There is something
like a general sense of the faith in the Church, a fundamental
conviction in matters of faith that is brought about by the Holy Spirit
and present throughout the Church, the Church's "common sense", so to
speak, which recognizes "what has always and everywhere been believed by
all" (Vincent of Lerins).

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[ROME]{.bold1}]{.small}

[From the very beginning, the Christian community in Rome was regarded
as "the greatest and most ancient Church known to all, founded and
organized at Rome by the two most glorious apostles, Peter and Paul. .
\[W\]ith this Church, because of her superior origin, all Churches must
agree, that is, all the faithful in the whole world; and it is in her
that the faithful everywhere have maintained the Tradition of the
apostles"(St. Irenaeus of Lyons, 135-202). The fact that both apostles
suffered martyrdom in Rome lent additional significance to the Roman
community.]{.small}

[143 ]{.bold1}[Is the Pope really infallible]{.italic}?

[Yes. But the →[P[OPE]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254325)
speaks infallibly only when he defines a dogma in a solemn
ecclesiastical act ("ex cathedra"), in other words, makes an
authoritative decision in doctrinal questions of faith and morals.
Magisterial decisions of the college of bishops in communion with the
Pope also possess an infallible character, for example, decisions of an
ecumenical council. \[888-892\]]{.bold1}

The infallibility of the
→[P[OPE]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254325) has nothing to
do with his moral integrity or his intelligence. What is infallible is
actually the [Church]{.italic}, for Jesus promised her the Holy Spirit,
who keeps her in the truth and leads her ever deeper into it. When a
truth of the faith that has been taken for granted is suddenly denied or
misinterpreted, the Church must have one final voice that
authoritatively says what is true and what is false. This is the voice
of the Pope. As the successor of Peter and the first among the bishops,
he has the authority to formulate the disputed truth according to the
Church's Tradition of faith in such a way that it is presented to the
faithful for all times as something "to be believed with certainty". We
say then that [the Pope defines a dogma]{.italic}. Therefore such a
dogma can never contain something substantially "new". Very rarely is a
dogma defined. The last time was in 1950.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["I have yet many things to say to you,
but you cannot bear them now. When the Spirit of truth comes, he will
guide you into all the truth."]{.small}

[Jn 16:12-13]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[ECUMENICAL COUNCIL]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(Greek [oikumene]{.italic} = the entire inhabited world): assembly of
the Catholic bishops from all over the world; not to be confused with
"ecumenism" in the sense of efforts to bring about unity among all
Christians.]{.small}

[144 ]{.bold1}[What is the task of the bishops]{.italic}?

[Bishops have responsibility for the local Church that is entrusted to
them and a share in the responsibility for the whole
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459). They exercise
their authority in communion with one another and for the benefit of the
whole Church under the leadership of the
→[P[OPE]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254325). \[886-887,
893-896, 938-939\]]{.bold1}

Bishops must first of all be
→[[APOSTLES]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos38443)---faithful
witnesses of Jesus, who personally called them to follow him and then
sent them. So they bring Christ to mankind and mankind to Christ. This
happens through their preaching, the celebration of the sacraments, and
their governance of the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459). As a
successor of the apostles, a bishop exercises his ministry by virtue of
his own apostolic authority; he is not an agent or a sort of assistant
to the Pope. Yet he acts with and under the Pope.

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[DOGMA]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(Greek [dogma]{.italic} = opinion, decision, doctrine): an article of
faith proclaimed by a Council or the Pope as divine
[revelation]{.italic} contained in Scripture and Tradition.]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[EX CATHEDRA]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(Latin = from the chair, symbol of teaching authority): This technical
expression designates the special case of an infallible magisterial
decision of the Pope.]{.small}

[145 ]{.bold1}[Why does Jesus want there to be Christians who live their
whole lives in poverty, unmarried chastity, and obedience]{.italic}?

[God is love. He longs for our love also. One form of loving surrender
to God is [to live as Jesus did---poor]{.italic}, chaste, and obedient.
Someone who lives in this way has head, heart, and hands free for God
and neighbor. \[914-933, 944-945\]]{.bold1}

In every age individual Christians let themselves be completely taken
over by Jesus, so that "for the sake of the kingdom of heaven" (Mt
19:12) they give everything away for God---even such wonderful gifts as
their own property, self-determination, and married love. This life
according to the →[[EVANGELICAL
COUNSELS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos265250) in poverty,
→[[CHASTITY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_018.html_filepos666264), and
obedience shows all Christians that the world is not everything. Only an
encounter with the divine Bridegroom "face to face" will ultimately make
a person happy.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["He who hears you hears me, and he who
rejects you rejects me, and he who rejects me rejects him who sent
me."]{.small}

[Lk 10:16]{.small}

![](images/00035.jpg){.calibre4}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[EVANGELICAL COUNSELS]{.bold1}]{.small}

[Poverty, unmarried chastity, and obedience are the counsels given in
the Gospel for imitating Jesus.]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[And Jesus looking upon him loved him,
and said to him, "You lack one thing; go, sell what you have . . . and
come, follow me."]{.small}

[Mk 10:21]{.small}

"I Believe in . . . the Communion of Saints"

[146]{.bold1} [What does the "communion of saints" mean]{.italic}?

[The "communion of saints" is made up of all men who have placed their
hope in Christ and belong to him through Baptism, whether they have
already died or are still alive. Because in Christ we are one Body; we
live in a communion that encompasses heaven and earth.
\[946-962\]]{.bold1}

The Church is larger and more alive than we think. Among her members are
the living and the deceased (whether they are still undergoing a process
of purification or are already in the glory of God), individuals known
and unknown, great saints and inconspicuous persons. We can help one
another even beyond the grave. We can call on our patrons and favorite
saints, but also our departed relatives and friends whom we believe are
already with God. Conversely, by our intercessory prayer, we can come to
the aid of our dear departed who are still undergoing purification.
Whatever the individual does or suffers in and for Christ benefits all.
Conversely, this unfortunately means also that every sin harms the
communion.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[126](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos227699)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[After I recognized that there is a God,
it was impossible for me not to live for him alone.]{.small}

[BL. CHARLES DE FOUCAULD\
(1858-1916, Christian hermit in the Sahara Desert)]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[If one member suffers, all suffer
together; if one member is honored, all rejoice together.]{.small}

[1 Cor 12:26]{.small}

[147]{.bold1} [Why does Mary have such a preeminent place in the
communion of saints]{.italic}?

[Mary is the Mother of God. She was united with Jesus on earth as no
other human being was or could be---in an intimacy that does not cease
in heaven. Mary is the Queen of Heaven, and in her motherhood she is
quite close to us. \[972\]]{.bold1}

Because she committed herself, body and soul, to a divine yet dangerous
undertaking, Mary was taken up body and soul into heaven. Anyone who
lives and believes as Mary did will get to heaven.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[80-85](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos156288)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Do not weep, for I shall be more useful
to you after my death, and I shall help you then more effectively than
during my life.]{.small}

[ST. DOMINIC\
(ca. 1170-1221, founder of the Dominicans), to his confreres as he was
dying]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The Assumption of Mary: "This mystery
is given to us so that . . . a divine light might be shed on our
death."]{.small}

[ROMANO GUARDINI]{.small}

[(1885-1968)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[God did not give mankind a maid, but a
Mother.]{.small}

[BL. ADOLF KOLPING\
(1813-1865, apostle to laborers and craftsmen)]{.small}

[148]{.bold1} [Can Mary really help us]{.italic}?

[Yes. Since the beginning of the Church, experience has taught that Mary
helps. Millions of Christians testify to it. \[967-970\]]{.bold1}

Being the Mother of Jesus, Mary is also our Mother. Good mothers always
stand up for their children. Certainly this Mother does. While still on
earth she interceded with Jesus for others; for example, she protected a
bride and groom in Cana from embarrassment. In the Upper Room on
Pentecost she prayed in the midst of the disciples. Because her love for
us never ceases, we can be sure that she will plead for us in the two
most important moments of our life: "[now]{.italic} and at the hour of
our death".
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[85](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos163365)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[When the wine failed, the mother of
Jesus said to him, "They have no wine." And Jesus said to her, "O woman,
what have you to do with me? My hour has not yet come." His mother said
to the servants, "Do whatever he tells you."]{.small}

[Jn 2:3-5]{.small}

[149]{.bold1} [May we worship Mary]{.italic}?

[No. Only God can be worshipped. But we can revere Mary as the Mother of
our Lord. \[971\]]{.bold1}

By worship we mean the humble, unconditional acknowledgment of the
absolute superiority of God over all creatures. Mary is a creature like
us. In faith she is our Mother. And we should honor our parents. There
is a biblical basis for this, since Mary herself says, "For behold,
henceforth all generations will call me blessed" (Lk 1:48b). So the
Church has Marian shrines and places of pilgrimage, feast days, hymns,
and prayers, for instance, the
→[R[OSARY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_021.html_filepos788617). It is a
compendium of the Gospels.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[353](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos585985),
[484](#YOUCAT_split_021.html_filepos790171)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The Rosary means lingering in the
sphere of Mary's life, the content of which is Christ.]{.small}

[ROMANO GUARDINI\
(1885-1968)]{.small}

"I Believe in . . . the Forgiveness of Sins"

[150]{.bold1} [Can the Church really forgive sins]{.italic}?

[Yes. Jesus not only forgave sins himself, he also conferred on the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) the mission
and the power to free men from their sins. \[981-983, 986-987\]]{.bold1}

Through the ministry of the → [PRIEST]{.small}, the penitent receives
God's forgiveness, and his guilt is wiped away as completely as if it
had never existed. A priest can do this only because Jesus allows him to
participate in his own divine power to forgive sins.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[225-239](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos403362)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["If you forgive the sins of any, they
are forgiven; if you retain the sins of any, they are
retained."]{.small}

[Jn 20:23]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Priests have received from God a power
that he has given neither to angels nor to archangels. . . . God above
confirms what priests do here below.]{.small}

[ST. JOHN CHRYSOSTOM\
(349/350-407)]{.small}

[151]{.bold1} [What possibilities are there for the forgiveness of sins
in the Church]{.italic}?

[Fundamentally the forgiveness of sins occurs in the
→[[SACRAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) of Baptism.
After that the sacrament of Reconciliation (Penance, confession) is
necessary for the forgiveness of serious sins. For less serious sins,
confession is recommended. But reading Sacred Scripture, prayer,
fasting, and the performance of good works also have the effect of
forgiving sins. \[976-980, 984-987\]
]{.bold1}![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[226-239](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos404016)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[I would be sore afraid, if it were not
for secret confession.]{.small}

[MARTIN LUTHER]{.small}

[(1483-1546)]{.small}

"I Believe in . . . the Resurrection of the Dead"

[152]{.bold1} [Why do we believe in the resurrection of the
dead]{.italic}?

[We believe in the resurrection of the dead because Christ rose from the
dead, lives forever, and causes us to share in this eternal life.
\[988-991\]]{.bold1}

When someone dies, his body is buried or cremated. Nevertheless, we
believe that there is a life after death for that person. In his
Resurrection, Jesus showed that he is Lord over death; his word is
trustworthy: "I am the resurrection and the life; he who believes in me,
though he die, yet shall he live" (Jn 11:25b).
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[103-108](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos191179)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[How can some of you say that there is
no resurrection of the dead? But if there is no resurrection of the
dead, then Christ has not been raised; if Christ has not been raised,
then our preaching is in vain and your faith is in vain. . If for this
life only we have hoped in Christ, we are of all men most to be pitied.
But in fact Christ has been raised from the dead, the first fruits of
those who have fallen asleep.]{.small}

[1 Cor 15:13-14, 19-20]{.small}

[153]{.bold1} [Why do we believe in the resurrection of the
"body"]{.italic}?

[In Jesus Christ, God himself took on "flesh"
(→[I[NCARNATION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos33395)) in order
to redeem mankind. The biblical word "flesh" characterizes man in his
weakness and mortality. Nevertheless, God does not regard human flesh as
something inferior. God does not redeem man's spirit only; he redeems
him entirely, body and soul. \[988-991, 997-1001, 1015\]]{.bold1}

God created us with a body (flesh) and a soul. At the end of the world
he does not drop the "flesh" like an old toy. On the "Last Day" he will
remake all creation and raise us up in the flesh---this means that we
will be transformed but still experience ourselves in [our
element]{.italic}. For Jesus, too, being in the flesh was not just a
phase. When the risen Lord showed himself, the disciples saw the wounds
on his body.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[And the Word \[of God\] became flesh
and dwelt among us.]{.small}

[Jn 1:14a]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Creation itself will be set free from
its bondage to decay and obtain the glorious liberty of the children of
God.]{.small}

[Rom 8:21]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[But some one will ask, "How are the
dead raised? With what kind of body do they come?" You foolish man! What
you sow does not come to life unless it dies. And what you sow is not
the body which is to be, but a bare kernel, perhaps of wheat or of some
other grain.]{.small}

[1 Cor 15:35-37]{.small}

[154]{.bold1} [What happens to us when we die]{.italic}?

[In death body and soul are separated. The body decays, while the soul
goes to meet God and waits to be reunited with its risen body on the
Last Day. \[992-1004, 1016-1018\]]{.bold1}

[How]{.italic} the resurrection will take place is a mystery. An image
can help us to accept it: When we look at a tulip bulb we cannot tell
into what a marvelously beautiful flower it will develop in the dark
earth. Similarly, we know nothing about the future appearance of our new
body. Paul is nevertheless certain: "It is sown in dishonor, it is
raised in glory" (1 Cor 15:43a).

[155 ]{.bold1}[How does Christ help us at our death, if we trust in
him]{.italic}?

[Christ comes to meet us and leads us into eternal life. "Not death, but
God will take me" (St. Thérèse of Lisieux). \[1005-1014, 1016,
1019\]]{.bold1}

In view of Jesus' suffering and death, death itself can become easier.
In an act of trust and love for the Father, we can say Yes, as Jesus did
in the Garden of Gethsemane. Such an attitude is called "spiritual
sacrifice": the dying person unites himself with Christ's sacrifice on
the Cross. Someone who dies this way, trusting in God and at peace with
men, and thus without serious sin, is on the way to communion with the
risen Christ. Our dying makes us fall no farther than into his hands. A
person who dies does not travel to nowhere but rather goes home into the
love of God, who created him.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[102](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos189413)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[If we live, we live to the Lord, and if
we die, we die to the Lord; so then, whether we live or whether we die,
we are the Lord's.]{.small}

[Rom 14:8]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[I want to see God and, in order to see
him, I must die.]{.small}

[ST. TERESA OF AVILA]{.small}

[(1515-1582)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[I am not dying; I am entering
life.]{.small}

[ST. THÉRÈSE OF LISIEUX]{.small}

[(1873-1897)]{.small}

"I Believe . . . in Life Everlasting"

[156 ]{.bold1}[What is eternal life]{.italic}?

[Eternal life begins with Baptism. It continues through death and will
have no end. \[1020\]]{.bold1}

Even when we are simply in love, we want this state of affairs to last
forever. "God is love", says the First Letter of John (1 Jn 4:16).
"Love", says the First Letter to the Corinthians, "never ends" (1 Cor
13:8). God is eternal because he is love; and love is everlasting
because it is divine. If we are in love, we enter into God's endless
presence.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[285](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos493903)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[But do not ignore this one fact,
beloved, that with the Lord one day is as a thousand years, and a
thousand years as one day.]{.small}

[2 Pet 3:8]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Nature is fleeting; we will outlive it.
Even when all suns and mists are gone, each one of us will still be
alive.]{.small}

[C. S. LEWIS]{.small}

[(1898-1963)]{.small}

[157 ]{.bold1}[Will we be brought to judgment after death]{.italic}?

[The so-called particular or personal judgment occurs at the moment of
death of the individual. The general judgment, which is also called the
Last Judgment, occurs on the Last Day, at the end of the world, when the
Lord comes again. \[1021-1022\]]{.bold1}

In dying every man arrives at the moment of truth. Now it is no longer
possible to repress or conceal anything; nothing more can be changed.
God sees us as we are. We come before his tribunal, where all is made
right, for if we are to be in God's holy presence at all, we must be
"right" with him---as right as God wanted us to be when he created us.
Perhaps we will still have to undergo a process of purification, or
maybe we will be able to fall into God's arms immediately. But perhaps
we will be so full of wickedness, hatred, and denial of everything that
we will turn our face away from love forever, away from God. A life
without love, however, is nothing but hell.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[163](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos291956)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Your place in heaven will seem to be
made for you and you alone, because you were made for it.]{.small}

[C. S. LEWIS]{.small}

[(1898-1963)]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[JUDGMENT]{.bold1}]{.small}

[The so-called particular or personal judgment occurs at the death of
the individual. The Last or General Judgment occurs on the Last Day, at
the end of the world, when the Lord comes again.]{.small}

[158]{.bold1} [What is heaven]{.italic}?

[Heaven is the endless moment of love. Nothing more separates us from
God, whom our soul loves and has sought our whole life long. Together
with all the angels and saints we will be able to rejoice forever in and
with God. \[1023-1026, 1053\]]{.bold1}

If you have ever observed a couple looking at each other lovingly or
seen a baby nursing who looks for his mother's eyes as though it wanted
to store up every smile forever, then you have some inkling of heaven.
To be able to see God face to face---that is like one, single,
never-ending moment of love.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[52](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos113387)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[For now we see as in a mirror dimly,
but then face to face. Now I know in part; then I shall understand
fully, even as I have been fully understood.]{.small}

[1 Cor 13:12]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[The fire will test what sort of work
each one has done. If the work which any man has built on the foundation
survives, he will receive a reward. If any man's work is burned up, he
will suffer loss, though he himself will be saved, but only as through
fire.]{.small}

[1 Cor 3:13-15]{.small}

[159]{.bold1} [What is purgatory]{.italic}?

[Purgatory, often imagined as a place, is actually a condition. Someone
who dies in God's grace (and therefore at peace with God and men) but
who still needs purification before he can see God face to face is in
purgatory. \[1030-1031\]]{.bold1}

When Peter had betrayed Jesus, the Lord turned around and looked at
Peter: "And Peter went out and wept bitterly"---a feeling [like being in
purgatory]{.italic}. Just such a purgatory probably awaits most of us at
the moment of our death: the Lord looks at us full of love---and we
experience burning shame and painful remorse over our wicked or "merely"
unloving behavior. Only after this purifying pain will we be capable of
meeting his loving gaze in untroubled heavenly joy.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Therefore he \[Judas Maccabeus\] made
atonement for the dead, that they might be delivered from their
sin.]{.small}

[2 Mac 12:45]{.small}

[160 ]{.bold1}[Can we help the departed who are in the condition of
purgatory]{.italic}?

[Yes, since all those who are baptized into Christ form one communion
and are united with one another, the living can also help the souls of
the faithful departed in purgatory. \[1032, 1414\]]{.bold1}

When a man is dead, he can do nothing more for himself. The time of
active probation is past. But we can do something for the faithful
departed in purgatory. Our love extends into the afterlife. Through our
fasting, prayers, and good works, but especially through the celebration
of Holy →[E[UCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475), we
can obtain grace for the departed.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[146](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos265962)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Let us not hesitate to help those who
have died and to offer our prayers for them.]{.small}

[ST. JOHN CHRYSOSTOM]{.small}

[(349/350-407)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[At the evening of life, we shall be
judged on love.]{.small}

[ST. JOHN OF THE CROSS]{.small}

[(1542-1591, Spanish mystic, Doctor of the Church and poet)]{.small}

[161 ]{.bold1}[What is hell]{.italic}?

[Hell is the condition of everlasting separation from God, the absolute
absence of love. \[1033-1037\]]{.bold1}

Someone who consciously and with full consent dies in serious sin,
without repenting, and refuses God's merciful, forgiving love forever,
excludes himself from communion with God and the saints. We do not know
whether anyone at the moment of death can look absolute Love in the face
and still say No. But our freedom makes that decision possible. Jesus
warns us again and again not to separate ourselves definitively from him
by shutting our hearts against the need of [his]{.italic} brothers and
sisters: "Depart from me, you cursed. . . . As you did it not to one of
the least of these, you did it not to me" (Mt 25:41, 45).
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[53](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos114351)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["And if your hand causes you to sin,
cut it off; it is better for you to enter life maimed than with two
hands to go to hell, to the unquenchable fire."]{.small}

[Mk 9:43]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[I ask myself: What does hell mean? I
maintain that it is the inability to love.]{.small}

[FYODOR M. DOSTOYEVSKY]{.small}

[(1821-1881, Russian writer)]{.small}

[162 ]{.bold1}[But if God is love, how can there be a hell]{.italic}?

[God does not damn men. Man himself is the one who refuses God's
merciful love and voluntarily deprives himself of (eternal) life by
excluding himself from communion with God. \[1036-1037\]]{.bold1}

God yearns for communion even with the worst sinner; he wants everyone
to convert and be saved. Yet God created man to be [free]{.italic} and
respects his decisions. Even God cannot compel love. As a lover he is
"powerless" when someone chooses hell instead of heaven.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[51](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos110379),
[53](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos114351)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[The Lord is not slow about his promise
as some count slowness, but is forbearing toward you, not wishing that
any should perish, but that all should reach repentance.]{.small}

[2 Pet 3:9]{.small}

![](images/00036.jpg){.calibre4}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[\[He\] desires all men to be saved and
to come to the knowledge of the truth.]{.small}

[1 Tim 2:4]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["When the Son of man comes in his
glory, and all the angels with him, then he will sit on his glorious
throne. Before him will be gathered all the nations, and he will
separate them one from another as a shepherd separates the sheep from
the goats, and he will place the sheep at his right hand, but the goats
at the left. . . . And they will go away into eternal punishment, but
the righteous into eternal life."]{.small}

[Mt 25:31-33, 46]{.small}

[163]{.bold1} [What is the Last Judgment]{.italic}?

[The Last Judgment will take place at the end of the world, at the
second coming of Christ. "All who are in the tombs will hear his voice
and come forth, those who have done good, to the resurrection of life,
and those who have done evil, to the resurrection of judgment" (Jn
5:29). \[1038-1041, 1058-1059\]]{.bold1}

When Christ comes again in glory, his full splendor will shine upon us.
The truth will come plainly to light: our thoughts, our deeds, our
relationship to God and to other men---nothing will remain hidden. We
will recognize the ultimate meaning of creation, comprehend God's
marvelous ways for the sake of our salvation, and finally receive also
an answer to the question of why evil can be so powerful if God is in
fact the Almighty. The Last Judgment is also our day in court. Here it
is decided whether we will rise to eternal life or be separated from God
forever. Toward those who have chosen life, God will act creatively once
again. In a "new body" (see 2 Cor 5) they will live forever in God's
glory and praise him with body and soul.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[110-112](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos201425),
[157](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos282174)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[According to his promise we wait for
new heavens and a new earth in which righteousness dwells.]{.small}

[2 Pet 3:13.]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[AMEN]{.bold1}]{.small}

[The word [amen]{.italic} (from Hebrew [aman]{.italic} = to be
steadfast, trustworthy) is used in the Old Testament most often in the
sense of "so be it", to reaffirm one's desire for God to act or to join
in the praise of God. In the New Testament it is commonly the
affirmative concluding word of a prayer. Most often, however, Jesus
himself uses it as an otherwise unusual introduction to a speech. It
underscores the authority of his words.]{.small}

[164 ]{.bold1}[How will the world come to an end]{.italic}?

[At the end of time, God will create a new heaven and a new earth. Evil
will no longer have any power or attractiveness. The redeemed will stand
face to face with God---as his friends. Their yearning for peace and
justice will be fulfilled. To behold God will be their blessedness. The
Triune God will dwell among them and wipe away every tear from their
eyes; there will be no more death, sorrow, lamentation, or trouble.
\[1042-1050, 1060\]]{.bold1}
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[110-112](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos201425)

[165 ]{.bold1}[Why do we say "Amen" to the profession of our
faith]{.italic}?

[We say Amen---"Yes"---to the profession of our faith because God
appoints us witnesses to the faith. Anyone who says Amen assents freely
and gladly to God's work in creation and redemption.
\[1061-1065\]]{.bold1}

The Hebrew word [amen]{.italic} comes from a family of words that mean
both "faith" and "steadfastness, reliability, fidelity". "He who says
[amen]{.italic} writes his signature" (St. Augustine). We can pronounce
this unconditional Yes only because Jesus in his death and Resurrection
has proved to be faithful and trustworthy for us. He himself is the
human Yes to all God's promises, just as he is also God's definitive Yes
to us.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[527](#YOUCAT_split_021.html_filepos853366)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[For all the promises of God find their
Yes in him. That is why we utter the Amen through him, to the glory of
God.]{.small}

[2 Cor 1:20]{.small}

::: {#YOUCAT_split_011.html_calibre_pb_11 .mbppagebreak}
:::

[]{#YOUCAT_split_012.html}

![](images/00037.jpg){.calibre4}

::: {#YOUCAT_split_012.html_calibre_pb_12 .mbppagebreak}
:::

[]{#YOUCAT_split_013.html}

## 2 {#YOUCAT_split_013.html_calibre_pb_13 .calibre12}

## How We Celebrate the Christian Mysteries {.calibre21}

Questions 166-278

[God Acts in Our Regard by Means of Sacred
Signs](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos297600)

[God and the sacred Liturgy](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos304976)

[How We Celebrate the Mysteries of
Christ](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos319727)

[The Seven Sacraments of the
Church](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos340760)

[The Sacraments of Initiation (Baptism, Confirmation, and
Eucharist)](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos343341)

[The sacraments of Healing (Reconciliation and the Anointing of the
Sick)](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos402290)

[The Sacraments of Communion and Mission (Holy Orders and
Marriage)](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos434040)

[Other Liturgical Celebrations](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos473869)

::: {#YOUCAT_split_013.html_calibre_pb_14 .mbppagebreak}
:::

[]{#YOUCAT_split_014.html}

Celebrating the Christian mysteries
(→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200)) is about
encountering Jesus Christ in time. Until the end of time he is present
in his →[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459). The
most profound encounter with him on earth is the
→[[LITURGY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos300013) (divine
worship). Therefore the [Rule of St. Benedict]{.italic} says:

"Nothing may have priority over the liturgy."

[ST. BENEDICT OF NURSIA (ca. 480-547, founder of Western
monasticism).]{.small}

[[SECTION ONE]{.bold1}]{.small}

God Acts in Our Regard\
by Means of sacred signs

[166]{.bold1} [Why does the Church celebrate the liturgy so
often]{.italic}?

[The people of Israel interrupted their work "seven times a day" (Ps
119:164) in order to praise God. Jesus participated in the liturgy and
prayer of his people; he taught his disciples to pray and gathered them
in the Upper Room so as to celebrate with them the
→[[LITURGY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos300013) of all
liturgies: the gift of himself in the Last Supper. The
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459), which calls
us to the liturgy, obeys his command, "Do this in remembrance of me" (1
Cor 11:24b). \[1066-1070\]]{.bold1}

Just as a man breathes air in order to stay alive, so too the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) lives and
breathes by celebrating the liturgy. God himself is the one who breathes
new life into her day by day and enriches her with gifts through his
Word and his
→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200). We can
use another image, too: every
→[[LITURGY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos300013) is like a
rendezvous of love that God writes on our calendar. Anyone who has
already experienced God's love is glad to go to church. Someone who from
time to time feels nothing and goes nevertheless shows God his
faithfulness.

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[LITURGY]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from greek [leiturgia]{.italic} = public work, service, achievement by
and for the people): In the Christian Tradition, liturgy means that the
People of god participate in the "work of God". The centerpiece of
liturgical celebrations is the Holy Eucharist; the other liturgies---for
example, the celebration of other sacraments, devotions, blessings,
processions, and the Liturgy of the Hours---are ordered to it.]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The liturgy is never a mere meeting of
a group of people, who make up their own form of celebration. . . .
\[T\]hrough our sharing in Jesus' appearing before the Father, we stand
both as members of the worldwide community of the whole Church and also
of the communion of saints. Yes, in a certain sense this is the liturgy
of heaven.]{.small}

[Joseph CARDINAL RATZINGER / POPE BENEDICT XVI, in [God and the
World]{.italic}]{.small}

[167]{.bold1} [What is liturgy]{.italic}?

[→[L[ITURGY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos300013) is the
official divine worship of the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459).
\[1077-1112\]]{.bold1}

A →[L[ITURGY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos300013) is not an
event that depends on good ideas and great songs. No one makes or
invents a Liturgy. It is something living that grew over millennia of
faith. A Mass is a holy, venerable action. Liturgy becomes exciting when
one senses that God himself is present under its sacred signs and its
precious, often ancient prayers.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Power came forth from him and healed
them all.]{.small}

[Lk 6:19b]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Without the Sunday Eucharist, we cannot
Live. Do you not know that the Christian exists for the Eucharist and
the Eucharist for Christians?]{.small}

[Answer of the martyr SATURNINUS (305) during cross-examination to the
accusation that he had taken part in the forbidden assembly on
Sunday.]{.small}

[168 ]{.bold1}[Why does the liturgy have priority in the life of the
Church and of the individual]{.italic}?

["The →[[LITURGY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos300013) is the
summit toward which the activity of the Church is directed; it is also
the font from which all her power flows" (Second Vatican Council,
[Sacrosanctum concilium]{.italic} 10). \[1074\]]{.bold1}

During Jesus' Lifetime, multitudes of people flocked to him, because
they were seeking his healing presence. Even today we can find him, for
he Lives in his Church. He assures us of his presence in two places: in
service to the poor (Mt 25:40) and in the
→[E[UCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475). There we
run directly into his arms. And when we Let him get close to us, he
teaches us, feeds us, transforms us, heals us, and becomes one with us
in the Holy Sacrifice of the Mass.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["I came that they may have life, and
have it abundantly."]{.small}

[Jn 10:10b]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[While he was yet at a distance, his
father saw him and had compassion, and ran and embraced him and kissed
him.]{.small}

[Lk 15:20]{.small}

[169 ]{.bold1}[What happens to us when we celebrate the
liturgy]{.italic}?

[When we celebrate the liturgy, we are drawn into the love of God,
healed, and transformed. \[1076\]]{.bold1}

The sole purpose of all Liturgies of the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) and all her
→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) is that we
might have Life and have it abundantly. When we celebrate the Liturgy,
we encounter the One who said about himseLf, "I am the way, and the
truth, and the Life" (Jn 14:6). Someone who is forsaken and goes to Mass
receives protection and consolation from God. Someone who feeLs Lost and
goes to Mass finds a God who is waiting for him.

![](images/00038.jpg){.calibre4}

[[CHAPTER ONE]{.bold1}]{.small}

[God and the Sacred Liturgy]{.bold1}

[170]{.bold1} [What is the most profound origin of the
Liturgy]{.italic}?

[The most profound origin of the
→[[LITURGY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos300013) is God, in
whom there is an eternal, heavenly banquet of love---the joy of the
Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit. Because God is love, he would like
to let us participate in the feast of his joy and to grant us his
→[[BLESSINGS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos306451).
\[1077-1109\]]{.bold1}

Our earthly Liturgies must be celebrations full of beauty and power:
Feasts [of the Father who created]{.italic} us---that is why the gifts
of the earth play such a great part: the bread, the wine, oil and Light,
incense, sacred music, and splendid colors. Feasts [of the Son who
redeemed]{.italic} us---that is why we rejoice in our liberation,
breathe deeply in listening to the Word, and are strengthened in eating
the Eucharistic Gifts. Feasts [of the Holy Spirit]{.italic} who lives in
us---that is why there is a wealth of consolation, knowledge, courage,
strength, and blessing that flows from these sacred assemblies.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[179](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos319934)

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[BLESSING]{.bold1}]{.small}

[A blessing is something good that comes from God (Latin
[benedicere]{.italic}; Greek [eu-logein]{.italic} = to call good); to
bless is a divine, life-giving, and life-preserving action. God, the
Father and Creator of all being, says: It is good that you exist. The
fact that you are is something beautiful.]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Then I will go to the altar of God, the
God my exceeding joy; and I will praise you with the lyre, O God, my
God.]{.small}

[Ps 43:4]{.small}

[171 ]{.bold1}[What is the essence of every liturgy]{.italic}?

[→[L[ITURGY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos300013) is always in
the first place communion or fellowship with Jesus Christ. Every
liturgy, not just the celebration of the Eucharist, is an Easter in
miniature. Jesus reveals his passage from death to life and celebrates
it with us. \[1085\]]{.bold1}

The most important
→[[LITURGY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos300013) in the world
was the Paschal Liturgy that Jesus celebrated with his disciples in the
Upper Room on the night before his death. The disciples thought that
Jesus would be commemorating the liberation of Israel from Egypt.
Instead, Jesus celebrated the liberation of all mankind from the power
of death. Back in Egypt it was the "blood of the Lamb" that preserved
the IsraeLites from the angel of death. Now he himself would be the Lamb
whose blood saves mankind from death. For Jesus' death and Resurrection
is the proof that someone can die and nevertheless gain life. This is
the genuine substance of every Christian liturgy. Jesus himself compared
his death and Resurrection with Israel's liberation from slavery in
Egypt. Therefore, the redemptive effect of Jesus' death and Resurrection
is called the [Paschal mystery]{.italic}. There is an analogy between
the life-saving blood of the Lamb at the Exodus of the Israelites from
Egypt (Ex 12) and Jesus, the true Paschal Lamb that has redeemed mankind
from the bondage of death and sin.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["The blood \[of the Lamb\] shall be a
sign for you, upon the houses where you are; and when I see the blood, I
will pass over you, and no plague shall faLL upon you to destroy you,
when I strike the Land of Egypt."]{.small}

[Ex 12:12-13f]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[SACRAMENT]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(Latin [sacramentum]{.italic} = military oath of allegiance; the usual
translation for the Greek [mysterion]{.italic} = mystery): Sacraments
are holy, visible signs instituted by Christ of an invisibLe reality, in
which Christians can experience the healing, forgiving, nourishing,
strengthening presence of God that enables them to love in turn; this is
possible because God's grace works in the sacraments.]{.small}

[172 ]{.bold1}[How many sacraments are there, and what are their
names]{.italic}?

[The →[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) has seven
