→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200): Baptism,
→[C[ONFIRMATION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos353709),
→[E[UCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475), Penance,
Anointing of the Sick, Holy Orders, and Matrimony. \[1210\]]{.bold1}

[173 ]{.bold1}[Why do we need sacraments in the first place]{.italic}?

[We need →[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200)
in order to outgrow our petty human life and to become like Jesus
through Jesus: children of God in freedom and glory. \[1129\]]{.bold1}

In Baptism the fallen children of men become cherished children of God;
through →[C[ONFIRMATION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos353709)
the weak become strong, committed Christians; through Penance the guilty
are reconciled; through the
→[E[UCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475) the hungry
become bread for others; through Matrimony and Holy Orders
individualists become servants of love; through the Anointing of the
Sick the despairing become people of confidence. The sacrament in all
the sacraments is Christ himself. In him we men, lost in seLfishness,
grow and mature into the true Life that has no end.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Because the creation itself will be set
free from its bondage to decay and obtain the glorious Liberty of the
children of God.]{.small}

[Rom 8:21]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[His divine power has granted to us all
things that pertain to life and godliness . . . by which he has granted
to us his precious and very great promises, that through these you may
escape from the corruption that is in the world because of passion, and
become partakers of the divine nature.]{.small}

[2 Pet 1:3]{.small}

[174 ]{.bold1}[[Why is faith in Jesus Christ not enough? Why does God
give us the sacraments, too]{.italic}?]{.bold1}

[We can and should come to God with all our senses, not just with the
intellect. That is why God gives himself to us in earthly
signs---especially in bread and wine, the Body and Blood of Christ.
\[1084, 1146-1152\]]{.bold1}

People saw Jesus, heard him, could touch him and thereby experience
salvation and healing in body and soul. The sensible signs of the
→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) show this
same signature of God, who desires to address the whole man---not just
his head.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[For any one who eats and drinks without
discerning the body \[of Christ\] eats and drinks judgment upon
himself.]{.small}

[1 Cor 11:29]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[As each one has received a gift, employ
it for one another, as good stewards of God's varied grace.]{.small}

[1 Pet 4:10]{.small}

[175 ]{.bold1}[Why do the sacraments belong to the Church? Why cannot
anyone use them however he wants]{.italic}?

[→[S[ACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) are
Christ's gift to his Church. It is her duty to administer them and to
protect them from misuse. \[1117-1119, 1131\]]{.bold1}

Jesus entrusted his words and signs to specific men, namely, the
apostles, who were to hand them on; he did not hand them over to an
anonymous crowd. Today we would say: He did not post his inheritance on
the Internet for free access but rather registered it under a domain
name. Sacraments exist [for]{.italic} the Church and [through]{.italic}
the Church. They are [for]{.italic} her, because the Body of Christ,
which is the Church, is established, nourished, and perfected through
the sacraments. They exist [through her]{.italic}, because the
sacraments are the power of Christ's Body, for example in confession,
where Christ forgives our sins through the
→[[PRIEST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464).

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[When the goodness and loving kindness
of our Saviour appeared, he saved us . . . by the washing of
regeneration and renewal in the Holy Spirit.]{.small}

[Tit 3:4-5]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[This is how one should regard us, as
servants of Christ and stewards of the mysteries of God.]{.small}

[1 Cor 4:1]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[What was visible in our Savior has
passed over into his mysteries.]{.small}

ST. LEO THE GREAT\
(ca. 400-461, Pope and Father of the Church)

[176 ]{.bold1}[Which sacraments can be received only once in a
lifetime]{.italic}?

[Baptism,
→[C[ONFIRMATION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos353709), and
Holy Orders. These
→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) imprint an
indelible mark on the soul of the Christian. Baptism and Confirmation
make him once and for all a child of God and Christlike. Holy Orders
similarly leaves an imprint on a Christian man. \[1121\]]{.bold1}

Just as someone always is and remains a child of his parents (and not
just "sometimes" or "a little bit"), so also through Baptism and
Confirmation one becomes forever a child of God, Christlike, and a
member of his Church. Similarly, Holy Orders is not a "job" that a man
does until retirement; rather, it is an irrevocable charism (gift of
grace). Because God is faithful, the effect of these sacraments is
maintained forever for the Christian---as receptivity to God's call, as
a vocation, and as protection. Consequently these sacraments cannot be
repeated.

[177 ]{.bold1}[Why is faith a prerequisite for the sacraments]{.italic}?

[→[S[ACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) are not
magic. A sacrament can be effective only if one understands and accepts
it in faith. Sacraments not only presuppose faith, they also strengthen
it and give expression to it. \[1122-1126\]]{.bold1}

Jesus commissioned the
→[[APOSTLES]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos38443) first to make
people disciples through their preaching, in other words, to awaken
their faith and only [then]{.italic} to baptize them. There are two
things, therefore, that we receive from the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459): faith and the
sacraments. Even today someone becomes a Christian, not through a mere
ritual or by being listed in a register, but rather through acceptance
of the true faith. We receive the true faith from the Church. She
vouches for it. Because the Church's faith is expressed in the
→[[LITURGY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos300013), no
sacramental ritual can be changed or manipulated at the discretion of an
individual minister or a congregation.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[For I delivered to you as of first
importance what I also received.]{.small}

[1 Cor 15:3]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[As one candle is lit from the flame of
another, so is faith kindled by faith.]{.small}

[ROMANO GUARDINI\
(1885-1968)]{.small}

![](images/00039.jpg){.calibre4}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[And so, with the Angels and all the
Saints we proclaim your glory, as with one voice we sing: Holy, Holy,
Holy Lord God of hosts. . . .]{.small}

[Eucharistic Prayer II of the Church]{.small}

[178 ]{.bold1}[If a sacrament is administered by someone who is
unworthy, does it fail to have its effect]{.italic}?

[No. The →[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200)
are effective on the basis of the sacramental action that is carried out
([ex opere operato]{.italic}), in other words, independently of the
moral conduct or spiritual outlook of the minister. It is enough for him
to intend to do what the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) does.
\[1127-1128, 1131\]]{.bold1}

By all means, ministers of the sacraments ought to live an exemplary
life. But the sacraments take effect, not because of the
→[[HOLINESS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos239756) of their
ministers, but rather because Christ himself is at work in them. In any
case, he respects our freedom when we receive the sacraments. That is
why they have a positive effect only if we rely on Christ.

[[CHAPTER TWO]{.bold1}]{.small}

How We Celebrate the Mysteries of Christ

[179 ]{.bold1}[Who celebrates the liturgy]{.italic}?

[In all earthly liturgies, Christ the Lord himself is the one who
celebrates the cosmic
→[[LITURGY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos300013), which
encompasses angels and men, the living and the dead, the past, present,
and future, heaven and earth.
→[P[RIESTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464) and believers
participate in different ways in Christ's divine worship.
\[1136-1139\]]{.bold1}

When we celebrate the
→[[LITURGY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos300013), we must
prepare ourselves interiorly for the great thing that takes place in it:
here and now Christ is present and, with him, all of heaven. There
everyone is filled with unspeakable joy and at the same time with loving
care for us. The last book of Sacred Scripture, Revelation, portrays in
mysterious images this liturgy to which we here on earth join our
voices.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[170](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos305176)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[It (the liturgy) is entering into the
liturgy of the heavens that has always been taking place. . . . It is
not the case that you think something up and then sing it; instead, the
song comes to you from the angels.]{.small}

[JOSEPH RATZINGER / POPE BENEDICT XVI, "In the Presence of the Angels I
Will Sing Your Praise" in [A New Song for the Lord]{.italic}]{.small}

[180 ]{.bold1}[Why is the Mass sometimes referred to as a "worship
service"]{.italic}?

[A worship service is in the first place a service that God performs for
us---and only then is it our service offered to God. God gives himself
to us under the form of holy signs---so that we might do the same: give
ourselves unreservedly to him. \[1145-1192\]]{.bold1}

Jesus is there in Word and
→[[SACRAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200)---God is
present. That is the first and most important thing about every liturgy.
Only then do we enter the picture. Jesus sacrifices his life for us so
that we might offer to him the spiritual sacrifice of our life. In the
→[E[UCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475), Christ
gives himself to us, so that we might give ourselves to him. Thus we
take part in the redeeming and transforming sacrifice of Christ. Our
little life is burst open and led into the kingdom of God. God can live
his life in our lives.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Symbols are the language of something
invisible spoken in the visible world.]{.small}

[GERTRUD VON LE FORT\
(1876-1971)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[I consider the language of symbols to
be the only foreign language that every one of us ought to
learn.]{.small}

[ERICH FROMM\
(1900-1980, psychoanalyst)]{.small}

[181]{.bold1} [Why are there so many signs and symbols in the
liturgies]{.italic}?

[God knows that we men are not only spiritual but also bodily creatures;
we need signs and symbols in order to perceive and describe spiritual or
interior realities. \[1145-1152\]]{.bold1}

Whether it is red roses, a wedding ring, black clothing, graffiti, or
[AIDS]{.small} armbands---we always express our interior realities
through signs and are understood immediately. The incarnate Son of God
gives us human signs in which he is living and active among us: bread
and wine, the water of Baptism, the anointing with the Holy Spirit. Our
response to God's sacred signs instituted by Christ consists in signs of
reverence: genuflecting, standing while listening to the Gospel, bowing,
folding our hands. And as though for a wedding we decorate the place of
God's presence with the most beautiful things we have: flowers, candles,
and music. In any case, signs also require words to interpret them.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[And whoever would be the first among
you must be the slave of all. For the Son of man also came not to be
served but to serve, and to give his life as a ransom for many.]{.small}

[Mk 10:44-45]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[And one \[of the angels\] called to
another and said: "Holy, holy, holy is the Lord of hosts; the whole
earth is full of his glory."]{.small}

[Is 6:3]{.small}

[182]{.bold1} [Why do the sacred signs of the liturgy need words,
too]{.italic}?

[Celebrating the
→[L[ITURGY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos300013) means
encountering God; allowing him to act, listening to him, responding to
him. Such dialogues are always expressed in gestures and words.
\[1153-1155, 1190\]]{.bold1}

Jesus spoke to men through signs and words. So it is in the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459), also, when
the priests offers the gifts and says, "This is my Body . . . this is my
Blood. . . ." Only these interpreting words of Jesus cause the signs to
become sacraments: signs that bring about what they signify.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Someone who sings prays twice.]{.small}

[ST. AUGUSTINE (354-430)]{.small}

[183]{.bold1} [Why is there music at liturgies, and what kind of music
must it be to be suitable for liturgy]{.italic}?

[Where words are not enough to praise God, music comes to our aid.
\[1156-1158, 1191\]]{.bold1}

When we turn to God, there is always something ineffable and unsaid left
over. Then music can help out. In rejoicing, language becomes
song---that is why the angels [sing]{.italic}. Music in a worship
service should make prayer more beautiful and more fervent, move more
deeply the hearts of all in attendance and bring them closer to God, and
prepare for God a feast of melody.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Be filled with the Spirit, addressing
one another in psalms and hymns and spiritual songs, singing and making
melody to the Lord with all your heart.]{.small}

[Eph 5:19]{.small}

[184]{.bold1} [How does the liturgy affect time]{.italic}?

[In the liturgy time becomes ]{.bold1}[[time for God]{.italic}.]{.bold1}

Often we do not know what to do with our time---we look for a
[pastime]{.italic}. In the liturgy, time becomes quite dense, because
every second is filled with meaning. When we celebrate the liturgy, we
experience the fact that God has sanctified time and made every second a
gateway into eternity.

[185]{.bold1} [Why does the liturgy repeat itself every year]{.italic}?

[Just as we celebrate a birthday or a wedding anniversary each year, so
too the →[[LITURGY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos300013)
celebrates over the course of the year the most important events in
Christian salvation history. With one important difference, however: All
time is God's time. "Memories" of Jesus' life and teaching are
simultaneously encounters with the living God. \[1163-1165,
1194-1195\]]{.bold1}

The Danish philosopher Søren Kierkegaard once said, "Either we are
contemporaries of Jesus, or we can have nothing at all to do with it."
Following the Church year in faith makes us indeed contemporaries of
Jesus. Not because we can imagine ourselves so precisely as part of
[his]{.italic} time and [his]{.italic} life, but rather because he comes
into [my]{.italic} time and [my]{.italic} life, if I make room for him
in this way, with his healing and forgiving presence, with the explosive
force of his Resurrection.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[\[Make\] the most of the time.]{.small}

[Eph 5:16]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[God's eternity is not mere
time-lessness, the negation of time, but a power over time that is
really present with time and in time.]{.small}

[JOSEPH RATZINGER / POPE BENEDICT XVI, in [The Spirit of the
Liturgy]{.italic}]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The Church year, which makes present
and portrays anew the life of Christ, is mankind's greatest work of art;
and God has acknowledged it and allows it year after year, always
granting it new light, as though one were encountering it for the first
time.]{.small}

[JOCHEN KLEPPER\
(1903-1942, German writer)]{.small}

[186]{.bold1} [What is the liturgical year (the Church year]{.italic})?

[The liturgical year, or the Church year, superimposes the mysteries of
the life of Christ---from his Incarnation to his second coming in
glory---on the normal course of the year. The liturgical year begins
with Advent, the time of waiting for the Lord, and has its first high
point in the Christmas season and its second, even greater climax in the
celebration of the redemptive suffering, death, and Resurrection of
Christ at Easter. The Easter season ends with the feast of Pentecost,
the descent of the Holy Spirit on the → C[HURCH]{.small}. The liturgical
year is repeatedly interrupted by feasts of Mary and the saints, in
which the Church praises God's grace, which has led mankind to
salvation. \[1168-1173, 1194-1195\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00040.jpg){.calibre4}

[The Church's New Year begins on the first Sunday of Advent and reaches
its highpoint at Easter.]{.small}

[187]{.bold1} [How important is Sunday]{.italic}?

[Sunday is the center of Christian time, for on Sunday we celebrate
Christ's Resurrection, and every Sunday is a miniature Easter.
\[1163-1167, 1193\]]{.bold1}

If Sunday is disregarded or abolished, only workdays are left in the
week. Man, who was created for joy, degenerates into a workhorse and a
mindless consumer. We must learn on earth how to celebrate properly, or
else we will not know what to do in heaven. Heaven is an endless Sunday.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[104-107](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos192374)

[188]{.bold1} [What is the Liturgy of the Hours]{.italic}?

[The Liturgy of the Hours is the universal, public prayer of the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459). Biblical
readings lead the person who prays it ever deeper into the mystery of
the life of Jesus Christ. Throughout the world this gives the Triune God
the opportunity at every hour of the day to transform gradually those
who pray and also the world. The Liturgy of the Hours is prayed not only
by →[[PRIESTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464) and
religious. Many Christians who take their faith seriously join their
voices with the many thousands of praises and petitions that ascend to
God from all over the world. \[1174-1178, 1196\]]{.bold1}

The seven "hours of prayer" are like a treasury of the
→[C[HURCH'S]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) prayers. It
also loosens our tongues when we have become speechless because of joy,
sorrow, or fear. Again and again one is astonished in reciting the
Liturgy of the Hours: an entire reading "coincidentally" applies
precisely to my situation. God hears us when we call to him. He answers
us in these texts---often in a way that is so specific as to be almost
disconcerting. In any case he also allows us to have long periods of
silence and dryness so that we can demonstrate our fidelity.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[473](#YOUCAT_split_021.html_filepos773136),
[492](#YOUCAT_split_021.html_filepos803832)

[The seven times for prayer in the Liturgy of the Hours are:]{.small}

[• Matins (Office of Readings or Vigils, in the early morning
hours)]{.small}

[• Lauds (Morning Prayer)]{.small}

[• Terce (9:00 a.m., Midmorning Prayer)]{.small}

[• Sext (12:00 noon, Midday Prayer)]{.small}

[• None (3:00 p.m., Midafternoon Prayer)]{.small}

[• Vespers (Evening Prayer)]{.small}

[• Compline (Night Prayer)]{.small}

[189]{.bold1} [How does the liturgy affect the spaces in which we
live]{.italic}?

[By his victory, Christ has penetrated all places in the world. He
himself is the true Temple, and the worship of God "in spirit and truth"
(Jn 4:24) is no longer bound up with a particular place. Nevertheless,
the Christian world is filled with churches and sacred signs, because
men need specific places in which to meet and signs to remind them of
this new reality. Every house of God is a symbol for our heavenly
Father's house, to which we are journeying. \[1179-1181,
1197-1198\]]{.bold1}

Certainly one can pray anywhere---in the forest, on the beach, in bed.
But since we men are not merely spiritual but also have a body, we need
to see, hear, and feel one another; we need a specific place if we want
to meet so as to be the Body of Christ; we must kneel down if we want to
worship God; we must eat the transformed bread when it is offered; we
must set our bodies in motion when [he]{.italic} calls us. And a cross
on the roadside will remind us of who owns the world and where our
journey is taking us.

![](images/00041.jpg){.calibre4}

![](images/00042.jpg){.calibre4}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[God has set up churches like harbors
along the coast, so that you may take refuge there out of the swirl of
earthly cares and find peace and quiet.]{.small}

[ST. JOHN CHRYSOSTOM\
(349/350-407)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The doctrine of the invisible church
leads quite logically to the doctrine of invisible religion, and the
next step is necessarily the disappearance of religion.]{.small}

[PAUL DE LAGARDE]{.small}

[(1827-1891, German Orientalist and philosopher)]{.small}

[190 ]{.bold1}[What is a Christian house of prayer]{.italic}?

[A Christian house of prayer is both a sign of the ecclesial communion
of people at a specific place and also a symbol of the heavenly
dwellings that God has prepared for us all. In God's house we gather
together to pray in common or alone and to celebrate the
→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200),
especially the
→[E[UCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475).
\[1179-1186, 1197-1199\]]{.bold1}

"It smells like heaven here." "Here you can be very quiet and reverent."
Many churches surround us perceptibly in a thick atmosphere of prayer.
We sense that God is present here. The beauty of church buildings
directs our attention to the beauty, greatness, and love of God.
Churches are not just stone messengers of the faith, but dwelling places
of God, who is really and truly and substantially present in the
sacrament of the altar.

[191 ]{.bold1}[What liturgical spaces define a house of God]{.italic}?

[The central places of a house of God are the altar with the crucifix,
the →[[TABERNACLE]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos393147), the
celebrant's chair, the ambo, the baptismal font, and the confessional.
\[1182-1188\]]{.bold1}

The [altar]{.italic} is the central point of the church. On it Jesus
Christ's sacrifice on the Cross and Resurrection are made present in the
celebration of the Eucharist. It is also the table to which the People
of God are invited. The [tabernacle]{.italic}, a kind of sacred safe,
houses with the greatest honor in a most worthy place in the church the
Eucharistic species in which the Lord himself is present. The so-called
[perpetual lamp]{.italic} indicates that the tabernacle is "occupied".
If the lamp is not burning, the tabernacle is empty. The raised
[chair]{.italic} (Latin [cathedra]{.italic}) of the
→[[BISHOP]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254778) or the priest
means that ultimately Christ is the one who leads the congregation. The
[ambo]{.italic} (from Greek [anabainein]{.italic} = to climb up), the
lectern for the Word of God, should manifest the value and dignity of
the biblical readings as the Word of the living God. Baptisms are
performed at the [baptismal font]{.italic}, and the [holy water
font]{.italic} should be a vivid reminder of our baptismal promises. A
[confessional]{.italic} or [confession room]{.italic} is there so that
we can acknowledge our guilt and receive forgiveness.

[192]{.bold1} [Can the Church also change and renew the
liturgy]{.italic}?

[There are changeable and unchangeable components of the
→[[LITURGY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos300013). Unchangeable
is everything that is of divine origin, for instance, the words of Jesus
at the Last Supper. Then there are changeable parts, which the Church
occasionally must change. After all, the mystery of Christ must be
proclaimed, celebrated, and lived out at all times and in all places.
\[1200-1209\]]{.bold1}

Jesus effectively addressed the entire person: mind and understanding,
heart and will. That is precisely what he wants to do today also in the
→[[LITURGY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos300013). That is why
it has different characteristics in Africa and in Europe, in nursing
homes and at World Youth Days, and differs in appearance in parishes and
monasteries. But it must still be recognizable that it is the one
liturgy of the whole worldwide Church.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[There is. . . one Lord, one faith, one
baptism, one God and Father of us all.]{.small}

[Eph 4:5-6]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[INITIATION]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Latin [initium]{.italic} = beginning): the term for the
introduction and integration of an outsider into an already existing
community or fellowship.]{.small}

[[SECTION TWO]{.bold1}]{.small}

The Seven Sacraments of the Church

[193]{.bold1} [Is there some inner logic that unites the sacraments with
each other]{.italic}?

[All →[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) are
an encounter with Christ, who is himself the original sacrament. There
are sacraments of → [INITIATION]{.small}, which introduce the recipient
into the faith: Baptism,
→[C[ONFIRMATION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos353709), and
→[E[UCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475). There are
sacraments of healing: Reconciliation and the Anointing of the Sick. And
there are sacraments of communion and mission: Matrimony and Holy
Orders. \[1210-1211\]]{.bold1}

Baptism joins us with Christ. Confirmation gives us his Spirit. The
Eucharist unites us with him. Confession reconciles us with Christ.
Through the Anointing of the Sick, Christ heals, strengthens, and
consoles. In the sacrament of Matrimony, Christ promises his love in our
love and his fidelity in our fidelity. Through the sacrament of Holy
Orders, →[[PRIESTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464) have
the privilege of forgiving sins and celebrating the Holy Sacrifice of
the Mass.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[We were buried therefore with him by
baptism into death, so that as Christ was raised from the dead by the
glory of the Father, we too might walk in newness of life.]{.small}

[Rom 6:4]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Through Baptism each child is inserted
into a gathering of friends who never abandon him in life or in
death. . . . This group of friends, this family of God, into which the
child is now admitted, will always accompany him, even on days of
suffering and in life's dark nights; it will give him consolation,
comfort, and light.]{.small}

[POPE BENEDICT XVI,\
January 8, 2006]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Therefore if any one is in Christ, he
is a new creation; the old has passed away, behold, the new has
come.]{.small}

[2 Cor 5:17]{.small}

[[CHAPTER ONE]{.bold1}]{.small}

The Sacraments of Initiation

The Sacrament of Baptism

[194]{.bold1} [What is Baptism]{.italic}?

[Baptism is the way out of the kingdom of death into life, the gateway
to the →[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459), and
the beginning of a lasting communion with God. \[1213-1216,
1276-1278\]]{.bold1}

Baptism is the foundational
→[[SACRAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) and the
prerequisite for all other sacraments. It unites us with Jesus Christ,
incorporates us into his redemptive death on the Cross, thereby freeing
us from the power of Original Sin and all personal sins, and causes us
to rise with him to a life without end. Since Baptism is a covenant with
God, the individual must say Yes to it. In the baptism of children, the
parents confess the Faith on behalf of the children.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[197](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos347418)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Go therefore and make disciples of all
nations, baptizing them in the name of the Father and of the Son and of
the Holy Spirit.]{.small}

[Mt 28:19]{.small}

[195 ]{.bold1}[How is Baptism administered]{.italic}?

[The classical form of administering Baptism is the threefold immersion
of the candidate in the water. Usually, however, water is poured three
times over the head of the candidate, while the minister of the
sacrament speaks the words, "N., I baptize you in the name of the
Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit." \[1229-1245,
1278\]]{.bold1}

Water symbolizes cleansing and new life, which was already expressed in
the baptism of repentance performed by John the Baptist. The Baptism
that is administered with water "in the name of the Father, and of the
Son, and of the Holy Spirit" is more than a sign of conversion and
repentance; it is [new life in Christ]{.italic}. That is why the
ceremony also includes the signs of anointing, the white garment, and
the baptismal candle.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[The night is far gone, the day is at
hand. Let us then cast off the works of darkness and put on the armor of
light . . . Put on the Lord Jesus Christ \[like a new
garment\].]{.small}

[Rom 13:12, 14]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[CATECHUMENATE]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Greek [kat' echein]{.italic} = to instruct, to teach by word of
mouth): Especially in the early Church candidates for adult Baptism
(catechumens) went through a three-stage preparation, the catechumenate,
in which they were instructed in the faith and were gradually allowed to
participate in the Liturgy of the Word.]{.small}

[196 ]{.bold1}[Who can be baptized, and what is required of a
candidate]{.italic}?

[Any person who is not yet baptized can be baptized. The only
prerequisite for Baptism is faith, which must be professed publicly at
the Baptism. \[1246-1254\]]{.bold1}

A person who turns to Christianity is not just changing a world view. He
travels a path of learning (the
→[[CATECHUMENATE]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos346212)), in
which he becomes a new man through personal conversion, but especially
through the gift of Baptism. He is now a living member of the Body of
Christ.

[197]{.bold1} [Why does the Church adhere to the practice of infant
Baptism]{.italic}?

[From antiquity the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) has practiced
infant Baptism. There is one reason for this: before we decide on God,
God has decided on us. Baptism is therefore a grace, an undeserved gift
of God, who accepts us unconditionally. Believing parents who want what
is best for their child want Baptism also, in which the child is freed
from the influence of original sin and the power of death. \[1250,
1282\]]{.bold1}

Infant Baptism presupposes that Christian parents will raise the
baptized child in the faith. It is an injustice to deprive the child of
Baptism out of a mistaken liberality. One cannot deprive a child of love
so that he can later decide on love for himself; so too it would be an
injustice if believing parents were to deprive their child of God's
grace in Baptism. Just as every person is born with the ability to speak
yet must learn a language, so too every person is born with the capacity
to believe but must become acquainted with the faith. At any rate,
Baptism can never be imposed on anyone. If someone has received Baptism
as a little child, he must "ratify" it later in life---this means he
must say Yes to it, so that it becomes fruitful.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The gift received by newborn infants
needs to be accepted by them freely and responsibly once they have
reached adulthood: the process of growing up will then bring them to
receive the sacrament of Confirmation, which precisely strengthens the
baptized and confers upon each one the "seal" of the Holy
Spirit.]{.small}

[POPE BENEDICT XVI,\
January 8, 2006]{.small}

![](images/00043.jpg){.calibre4}

[198]{.bold1} [Who can administer Baptism]{.italic}?

[Normally a →[[BISHOP]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254778), a
→ [PRIEST]{.small}, or a
→[[DEACON]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257061) administers
the sacrament of Baptism. In an emergency, any Christian, indeed anyone,
can baptize by pouring water over the head of the recipient and
pronouncing the baptismal formula, "I baptize you in the name of the
Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit." \[1256, 1284\]]{.bold1}

Baptism is so important that even a non-Christian can administer it. In
doing so, however, he must have the intention of doing what the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) does when she
baptizes.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[\[He\] desires all men to be saved and
to come to the knowledge of the truth.]{.small}

[1 Tim 2:4]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["Unless one is born of water and the
Spirit, he cannot enter the kingdom of God."]{.small}

[Jn 3:5]{.small}

[199 ]{.bold1}[Is Baptism in fact the only way to salvation]{.italic}?

[For all those who have received the Gospel and have heard that Christ
is "the way, and the truth, and the life" (Jn 14:6), Baptism is the only
way to God and salvation. At the same time, however, it is true that
Christ died for all mankind. Therefore all men who have had no
opportunity to learn about Christ and the faith but seek God sincerely
and live according to their conscience also find salvation (the
so-called ]{.bold1}[Baptism of desire]{.italic}). [\[1257-1261, 1281,
1283\]]{.bold1}

God has made salvation dependent on the → [SACRAMENTS]{.small}.
Therefore the Church must tirelessly offer them to mankind. To give up
her missionary work would be a betrayal of God's commission. God
himself, however, is not dependent on his sacraments. In places where
the Church does not exist or has had no success---whether by her own
fault or for other reasons---God himself paves another way to salvation
for the people.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[136](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos244910)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[If we live, we live to the Lord, and if
we die, we die to the Lord; so then, whether we live or whether we die,
we are the Lord's.]{.small}

[Rom 14:8]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[For by one Spirit we were all baptized
into one body---Jews or Greeks, slaves or free---and all were made to
drink of one Spirit.]{.small}

[1 Cor 12:13]{.small}

[200]{.bold1} [What happens in Baptism]{.italic}?

[In Baptism we become members of the Body of Christ, sisters and
brothers of our Redeemer, and children of God. We are freed from sin,
snatched from death, and destined from then on for a life in the joy of
the redeemed. \[1262-1274, 1279-1280\]]{.bold1}

Being baptized means that my personal life story is submerged in the
stream of God's love. "Our life", says Pope Benedict XVI, "now belongs
to Christ, and no longer to ourselves. . . . At his side and, indeed,
drawn up in his love, we are freed from fear. He enfolds us and carries
us wherever we may go---he who is Life itself" (April 7, 2007).
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[126](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos227699)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[And if \[we are\] children, then heirs,
heirs of God and fellow heirs with Christ.]{.small}

[Rom 8:17]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[CONFIRMATION]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Latin [confirmatio]{.italic} = strengthening, consolidation):
Confirmation, like Baptism and the Eucharist, is one of the three
sacraments of initiation of the Catholic Church. As the Holy Spirit
descended upon the disciples who were gathered on Pentecost, so the Holy
Spirit comes to every baptized person for whom the Church requests the
gift of the Holy Spirit. It secures and strengthens him to be a living
witness to Christ.]{.small}

[201 ]{.bold1}[What is the significance of receiving a name in
Baptism]{.italic}?

[Through the name that we receive in Baptism God tells us: "I have
called you by name, you are mine" (Is 43:1). \[2156-2159,
2165\]]{.bold1}

In Baptism a person is not dissolved into an anonymous divinity, but
rather is affirmed precisely in his individuality. To be baptized by a
name signifies that God knows me, he says Yes to me and accepts me
forever in my unrepeatable uniqueness.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[361](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos601576)

[202 ]{.bold1}[Why should Christians choose the names of saints at
Baptism]{.italic}?

[There are no better examples than the saints and no better helpers. If
my namesake is a saint, I have a friend with God. \[2156-2159,
2165-2167\]]{.bold1}

[[CHRISM]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Greek [chrisma]{.italic} = oil of anointing; and
[christos]{.italic} = anointed one): Chrism is an ointment made out of a
mixture of olive oil and balsam. On the morning of Holy Thursday, the
bishop consecrates it, so that it can be used in Baptism, Confirmation,
priestly and episcopal ordination, and also the consecration of altars
and bells. Oil is a symbol of joy, strength, and health. People anointed
with chrism are supposed to spread "the aroma of Christ" (2 Cor
2:15).]{.small}

The Sacrament of Confirmation

[203 ]{.bold1}[What is Confirmation]{.italic}?

[→[C[ONFIRMATION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos353709) is the
→[[SACRAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) that
completes Baptism; in it the gift of the Holy Spirit is bestowed upon
us. Anyone who freely decides to live a life as God's child and asks for
God's Spirit under the signs of the imposition of hands and anointing
with →[[CHRISM]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos355309) receives
the strength to witness to God's love and might in word and deed. He is
now a full-fledged, responsible member of the Catholic
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459).
\[1285-1314\]]{.bold1}

When a coach sends a soccer player onto the playing field, he puts his
hand on his shoulder and gives him final instructions. We can understand
Confirmation in a similar way. A hand is placed upon us. We step out
onto the field of life. Through the Holy Spirit we know what we have to
do and we have been given the power to do it. He has motivated us. His
mission resounds in our ears. We sense his help. We will not betray his
trust or disappoint him; we will win the game for him. We just have to
want to do it and listen to him.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[119](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos214172),
[120](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos217510)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Now when the apostles at Jerusalem
heard that Samaria had received the word of God, they sent to them Peter
and John, who came down and prayed for them that they might receive the
Holy Spirit; for the Spirit had not yet fallen on any of them, but they
had only been baptized in the name of the Lord Jesus.]{.small}

[Acts 8:14-16]{.small}

[204]{.bold1} [What does Sacred Scripture say about the sacrament of
Confirmation]{.italic}?

[In the →[O[LD]{.small}
T[ESTAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos45534), the People of
God expected the outpouring of the Holy Spirit upon the Messiah. Jesus
lived his life in a special Spirit of love and of perfect unity with his
Father in heaven. This Spirit of Jesus was the "Holy Spirit" for whom
the people of Israel longed; this was the same Spirit whom Jesus
promised to his disciples, the same Spirit who descended upon the
disciples fifty days after Easter, on the feast of Pentecost. And it is
again this same Holy Spirit of Jesus who descends upon everyone who
receives the
→[[SACRAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) of
→[C[ONFIRMATION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos353709).
\[1285-1288, 1315\]]{.bold1}

In the Acts of the Apostles, which were written a few decades after the
death of Jesus, we see Peter and John traveling about to confirm new
Christians by imposing hands on those who previously "had only been
baptized in the name of the Lord Jesus", so that their hearts might be
filled with the Holy Spirit.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[113-120](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos205878),
[310-311](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos525959)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[It is part of your vocation to shout
the Gospel from the rooftops, not by your words, but by your
life.]{.small}

[BL. CHARLES DE FOUCAULD\
(1858-1916)]{.small}

[205 ]{.bold1}[What happens in Confirmation]{.italic}?

[In →[C[ONFIRMATION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos353709) the
soul of a baptized Christian is imprinted with a permanent seal that can
be received only once and marks this individual forever as a Christian.
The gift of the Holy Spirit is the strength from above in which this
individual puts the grace of his Baptism into practice through his life
and acts as a "witness" for Christ. \[1302-1305, 1317\]]{.bold1}

To be confirmed means to make a "covenant" with God. The confirmand
says, "Yes, I believe in you, my God; give me your Holy Spirit, so that
I might belong entirely to you and never be separated from you and may
witness to you throughout my whole life, body and soul, in my words and
deeds, on good days and bad." And God says, "Yes, I believe in you, too,
my child---and I will give you my Spirit, my very self. I will belong
entirely to you. I will never separate myself from you, in this life or
eternally in the next. I will be in your body and your soul, in your
words and deeds. Even if you forget me, I will still be there---on good
days and bad."
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[120](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos217510)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Create in me a clean heart, O God, and
put a new and right spirit within me.]{.small}

[Ps 51:12]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[I have set before you life and death,
blessing and curse; therefore choose life, that you and your descendants
may live.]{.small}

[Deut 30:19]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[I am created to do or to be something
for which no one else is created: I have a place in God's counsels, in
God's world, which no one else has. Whether I be rich or poor, despised
or esteemed by man, God knows me and calls me by my name.]{.small}

[BL. JOHN HENRY NEWMAN\
(1801-1890)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The most important thing is to begin
decisively.]{.small}

[ST. TERESA OF AVILA\
(1515-1582)]{.small}

[206 ]{.bold1}[Who can be confirmed, and what is required of a candidate
for Confirmation]{.italic}?

[Any Catholic Christian who has received the
→[[SACRAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) of Baptism
and is in the "state of grace" can be admitted to
→[C[ONFIRMATION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos353709).
\[1306-1311, 1319\]]{.bold1}

To be "in the state of grace" means not to have committed any serious
sin (mortal sin). By a serious sin a person separates himself from God
and can be reconciled with God only by making a good confession. A
(young) Christian who is preparing for Confirmation finds himself in one
of the most important phases of his life. He will do everything possible
to grasp the faith with his heart and his understanding; he will pray
alone and with others for the Holy Spirit; he will reconcile himself in
every way with himself, with the people around him, and with God.
Confession is part of this, since it brings one closer to God even if
one has not committed a mortal sin.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[316-317](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos534269)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[God would have given us something
greater if he had had something greater than himself.]{.small}

[ST. JOHN VIANNEY\
(1786-1859, the "Cure of Ars")]{.small}

[207]{.bold1} [Who may confirm]{.italic}?

[The →[S[ACRAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) of
→[C[ONFIRMATION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos353709) is
normally administered by the
→[[BISHOP]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254778). For weighty
reasons when necessary, the bishop can also delegate a priest to do it.
In danger of death, any priest can administer Confirmation.
\[1312-1314\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The actual effect of the Eucharist is
the transformation of man into God.]{.small}

[ST. THOMAS AQUINAS\
(1225-1274)]{.small}

The Sacrament of the Eucharist

[208]{.bold1} [What is Holy Eucharist]{.italic}?

[Holy Eucharist is the
→[[SACRAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) in which
Jesus Christ gives his Body and Blood---himself---for us, so that we too
might give ourselves to him in love and be united with him in Holy
→[C[OMMUNION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos376549). In this
way we are joined with the one Body of Christ, the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459). \[1322, 1324,
1409, 1413\]]{.bold1}

After Baptism and
→[C[ONFIRMATION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos353709), the
Eucharist is the third sacrament of initiation of the Catholic Church.
The Eucharist is the mysterious center of all these sacraments, because
the historic sacrifice of Jesus on the Cross is made present during the
words of consecration in a hidden, unbloody manner. Thus the celebration
of the Eucharist is "the source and summit of the Christian life"
(Second Vatican Council, [Lumen gentium]{.italic} \[LG\], 11).
Everything aims at this; besides this there is nothing greater that one
could attain. When we eat the broken Bread, we unite ourselves with the
love of Jesus, who gave his body for us on the wood of the Cross; when
we drink from the chalice, we unite ourselves with him who even poured
out his blood out of love for us. We did not invent this ritual. Jesus
himself celebrated the Last Supper with his disciples and therein
anticipated his death; he gave himself to his disciples under the signs
of bread and wine and commanded them from then on, even after his death,
to celebrate the
→[E[UCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475). "Do this
in remembrance of me" (1 Cor 11:24).
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[126](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos227699),
[193](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos340961),
[217](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos391628)

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[EUCHARIST]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(Greek [eucharistia]{.italic} = thanksgiving): Eucharist was at first
the name for the prayer of thanksgiving that preceded the transformation
of the bread and wine into Christ's Body and Blood in the liturgy of the
early Church. Later the term was applied to the whole celebration of the
Mass.]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Draw near to God and he will draw near
to you.]{.small}

[Jas 4:8]{.small}

[209]{.bold1} [When did Christ institute the Eucharist]{.italic}?

[Christ instituted the Holy
→[E[UCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475) on the
evening before his death, "on the night when he was betrayed" (1 Cor
11:23), when he gathered the
→[[APOSTLES]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos38443) around him in
the Upper Room in Jerusalem and celebrated the Last Supper with them.
\[1323, 1337-1340\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[How can Jesus distribute his Body and
his Blood? By making the bread into his Body and the wine into his
Blood, he anticipates his death, he accepts it in his heart, and he
transforms it into an action of love. What on the outside is simply
brutal violence---the Crucifixion---from within becomes an act of total
self-giving love.]{.small}

[POPE BENEDICT XVI,]{.small}

[August 21, 2005]{.small}

[210 ]{.bold1}[How did Christ institute the Eucharist]{.italic}?

["For I received from the Lord what I also delivered to you, that the
Lord Jesus on the night when he was betrayed took bread, and when he had
given thanks, he broke it, and said, 'This is my body, which is for you.
Do this in remembrance of me.' In the same way also the chalice, after
supper, saying, 'This chalice is the new covenant in my blood. Do this,
as often as you drink it, in remembrance of me' " (1 Cor
11:23-25).]{.bold1}

This, the oldest account of the events in the Upper Room at the Last
Supper, is by the
→[[APOSTLE]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos38443) Paul, who was
not an eyewitness himself, but rather wrote down what was being
preserved as a holy mystery by the young Christian community and was
being celebrated in the liturgy.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[99](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos184301)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[It was as though I heard a voice from
on high: I am the food of the strong; eat then of me and grow. But you
will not transform me into yourself like food for the body, but rather
you will be transformed into me.]{.small}

[ST. AUGUSTINE\
(354-430) at the time of his conversion]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Not to go to Communion is like someone
dying of thirst beside a spring.]{.small}

[ST. JOHN VIANNEY\
(1786-1859, Curé of Ars)]{.small}

[211 ]{.bold1}[How important is the Eucharist for the Church]{.italic}?

[The celebration of the
→[E[UCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475) is the
heart of the Christian communion. In it the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) becomes
Church. \[1325\]]{.bold1}

We are not Church because we get along well, or because we happen to end
up in the same parish community, but rather because in the Eucharist we
receive the Body of Christ and are increasingly being transformed into
the Body of Christ.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[126](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos227699),
[217](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos391628)

![](images/00044.jpg){.calibre4}

[212]{.bold1} [What names are therefor Jesus' meat with us, and what do
they mean]{.italic}?

[The different names indicate the unfathomable richness of this mystery:
the Holy Sacrifice, Holy Mass, the Sacrifice of the Mass---the Lord's
Supper---the Breaking of Bread---the Eucharistic assembly---the memorial
of the Lord's Passion, death, and Resurrection---the Holy and Divine
Liturgy, the Sacred Mysteries---Holy
→[C[OMMUNION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos376549).
\[1328-1332\]]{.bold1}

[Holy Sacrifice, Holy Mass, the Sacrifice of the Mass]{.italic}: The one
sacrifice of Christ, which completes and surpasses all sacrifices, is
made present in the celebration of the Eucharist. The
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) and the
faithful, through their self-offering, unite themselves with Christ's
sacrifice. The word Mass comes from the Latin dismissal, [Ite, missa
est]{.italic}, "Go now, you are sent."

[The Lord's Supper]{.italic}: Every celebration of the Eucharist is
still the one supper that Christ celebrated with his disciples and, at
the same time, the anticipation of the banquet that the Lord will
celebrate with the redeemed at the end of time. We men do not make the
worship service; the Lord is the one who calls us to worship God and is
mysteriously present in the liturgy.

[The breaking of bread]{.italic}: "The breaking of bread" was an old
Jewish ritual at meals, which Jesus employed at the Last Supper to
express his gift of self "for us" (Rom 8:32). In the "breaking of bread"
the disciples recognized him again after the Resurrection. The early
Church called their liturgical feasts "the breaking of bread".

[Eucharistic assembly]{.italic}: The celebration of the Lord's Supper is
also an assembly of "thanksgiving", in which the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) finds her
visible expression.

[Memorial of the Lord's Passion, death, and Resurrection]{.italic}: In
the celebration of the Eucharist, the congregation does not celebrate
itself; rather it discovers and celebrates again and again the presence
of Christ's saving passage through suffering and death to life.

[Holy and Divine Liturgy, Sacred Mysteries]{.italic}: In the celebration
of the Eucharist, the Church in heaven and on earth unite in one feast.
Because the Eucharistic Gifts in which Christ is present are, so to
speak, the holiest thing in the world, we also speak about the Most
Blessed Sacrament.

[Holy Communion]{.italic}: Because we unite ourselves with Christ at
Holy Mass, and through him are united with one another, we speak about
Holy Communion ([communio]{.italic} = fellowship).

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[In the Holy Eucharist we become one
with God like food with the body.]{.small}

[ST. FRANCIS DE SALES\
(1567-1622)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Your life must be woven around the
Eucharist. Direct your eyes to Him, who is the Light; bring your hearts
very close to His Divine Heart; ask Him for the grace to know Him, for
the charity to love Him, for the courage to serve Him. Seek him
longingly.]{.small}

[BL. TERESA OF CALCUTTA\
(1910-1997)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[We must not separate our life from the
Eucharist. The moment we do so, something shatters. People ask us,
"Where do the Sisters get the joy and the strength to do what they do?"
The Eucharist contains more than just receiving; it contains also the
satisfaction of Christ's hunger. He says, "Come to me." He hungers for
souls.]{.small}

[BL. TERESA OF CALCUTTA\
(1910-1997)]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[CONSECRATION]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Latin [consecratio]{.italic} = hallowing, sanctification): A
consecration is a solemn act of making something holy. Thus during Holy
Mass at the consecration, bread and wine are "consecrated" and thereby
transformed into the Body and Blood of Christ. Bishops, priests, and
deacons are consecrated too, as are certain things for the service of
God, such as church buildings and altars.]{.small}

[213]{.bold1} [What elements are essential to a Holy Mass]{.italic}?

[Every Holy Mass (celebration of the Eucharist) unfolds in two main
parts, the Liturgy of the Word and the Liturgy of the Eucharist.
\[1346-1347\]]{.bold1}

In the Liturgy of the Word, we hear readings from the Old and
→[N[EW]{.small}
T[ESTAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos45964) and also the
Gospel. Besides that there is an opportunity for preaching and general
intercessory prayers. In the subsequent Liturgy of the Eucharist, bread
and wine are offered, consecrated, and distributed to the faithful at
→[C[OMMUNION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos376549).

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[COMMUNION]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Latin [communio]{.italic} = fellowship): By Communion, we mean
receiving the Body and Blood of Christ in the transformed (consecrated)
gifts of bread and wine. This occurs as a rule during Holy Mass, but
also on certain occasions outside of Mass (for instance, when Holy
Communion is brought to the sick). Communion only under the species of
bread is also a full communion with Christ.]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[KYRIE ELEISON]{.small}]{.bold1}

[(Greek = Lord, have mercy): The [Kyrie eleison]{.italic}, an ancient
cry of homage to gods and rulers, was applied very early to Christ;
around the year 500 it was carried over from the Greek liturgy, without
translating it, into the Roman and Western liturgy.]{.small}

[214]{.bold1} [How is the Mass structured]{.italic}?

[Holy Mass begins with the gathering of the faithful and the entrance of
the priest and the others who serve in the sanctuary (altar servers,
lectors, cantors, and so on). After the greeting comes the Penitential
Rite, which concludes with the
→[K[YRIE]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos377093). On Sundays
(outside of Advent and Lent) and feast days, the
→[G[LORIA]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos386164) is then sung
or recited. The prayer of the day introduces one or two readings from
the →[O[LD]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos45534) and
→[N[EW]{.small}
T[ESTAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos45964), followed by
the responsorial psalm. Before the Gospel is read, there is an
→[A[LLELUIA]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos386578), or
acclamation. After the proclamation of the Gospel on Sundays and feast
days, the →[[PRIEST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464) or
→[[DEACON]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257061) gives a
→[[HOMILY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos387014) at least on
Sundays and feast days. Then, again only on Sundays and feast days, the
congregation professes its common faith in the
→[C[REED]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos64853), followed by the
intercessions. The second part of Holy Mass begins with the preparation
of the gifts, which concludes with the Offertory prayer. The high point
of the Eucharistic celebration is the Eucharistic Prayer, which is
introduced by the Preface and the
→[S[ANCTUS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos388527). Now the
gifts of bread and wine are transformed into the Body and Blood of
Christ. The Eucharistic Prayer concludes, finally, in the
→[[DOXOLOGY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos395913), which makes
the transition to the Lord's Prayer. Then comes the prayer for peace,
the →[A[GNUS]{.small}
D[EI]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos390669), the breaking of
the bread, and the distribution of the holy Gifts to the faithful, which
often is done only under the form of the Body of Christ. Holy Mass ends
with meditation, thanksgiving, a concluding prayer, and a blessing by
the priest. \[1348-1355\]]{.bold1}

The →[K[YRIE]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos377093):

[Celebrant]{.bold1}  Lord, have mercy!

[Response]{.bold1}  Lord, have mercy!

[C.]{.bold1} Christ, have mercy!

[R.]{.bold1} Christ, have mercy!

[C.]{.bold1} Lord, have mercy!

[R.]{.bold1} Lord, have mercy!

[[C.]{.italic}]{.bold1} Kyrie eleison!  [R.]{.bold1} Kyrie eleison!

[[C.]{.italic}]{.bold1} Christe eleison!  [R.]{.bold1} Christe eleison!

[[C]{.italic}]{.bold1}. Kyrie eleison!  [R.]{.bold1} Kyrie eleison!

The →[G[LORIA]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos386164):

Glory to God in the highest,

and on earth peace to people of good will.

We praise you, we bless you,

we adore you, we glorify you,

we give you thanks for your great glory,

Lord God, heavenly King,

O God, almighty Father.

Lord Jesus Christ, Only Begotten Son,

Lord God, Lamb of God, Son of the Father,

you take away the sins of the world,

have mercy on us;

you take away the sins of the world,

receive our prayer;

you are seated at the right hand of the Father,

have mercy on us.

For you alone are the Holy One,

you alone are the Lord,

you alone are the Most High,

Jesus Christ,

with the Holy Spirit,

in the glory of God the Father.

[Gloria in excelsis Deo]{.italic}

[et in terra pax hominibus bonae voluntatis]{.italic}.

[Laudamus te, benedicimus te]{.italic},

[adoramus te, glorificamus te]{.italic},

[gratias agimus tibi propter magnam gloriam tuam]{.italic},

[Domine Deus, Rex caelestis]{.italic},

[Deus Pater omnipotens]{.italic},

[Domine Fili unigenite, Iesu Christe]{.italic},

[Domine Deus, Agnus Dei]{.italic},

[Filius Patris]{.italic},

[qui tollis peccata mundi, miserere nobis]{.italic};

[qui tollis peccata mundi]{.italic},

[suscipe deprecationem nostram]{.italic}.

[Qui sedes ad dexteram Patris, miserere nobis]{.italic}.

[Quoniam tu solus Sanctus]{.italic},

[tu solus Dominus]{.italic},

[tu solus Altissimus, Iesu Christe]{.italic},

[cum Sancto Spiritu]{.italic}:

[in gloria Dei Patris. Amen]{.italic}.

The →[S[ANCTUS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos388527):

Holy, Holy, Holy Lord God of hosts.

Heaven and earth are full of your glory.

Hosanna in the highest.

Blessed is he who comes in the name of the Lord.

Hosanna in the highest.

[Sanctus, Sanctus, Sanctus Dominus Deus Sabaoth]{.italic}.

[Pleni sunt caeli et terra gloria tua]{.italic}.

[Hosanna in excelsis]{.italic}.

[Benedictus qui venit in nomine Domini]{.italic}.

[Hosanna in excelsis]{.italic}.

The →[A[GNUS]{.small}
D[EI]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos390669):

Lamb of God, you take away the sins of the world,

have mercy on us.

Lamb of God, you take away the sins of the world,

have mercy on us.

Lamb of God, you take away the sins of the world,

grant us peace.

[Agnus Dei, qui tollis peccata mundi, miserere nobis]{.italic}.

[Agnus Dei, qui tollis peccata mundi, miserere nobis]{.italic}.

[Agnus Dei, qui tollis peccata mundi, dona nobis pacem]{.italic}.

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[GLORIA]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(Latin = honor): The joyful song of the angels heard by the shepherds
(Lk 2:14) on Christmas night serves as the introduction to an ancient
Christian hymn that has existed in this form since the ninth century, in
which the praise of God is sung with solemnity.]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[ALLELUIA]{.bold1}]{.small}

[("Let us praise the Lord!"; composed of the Hebrew [halal]{.italic} =
praise ye!, glorify!, and the divine name [YHWH]{.italic}): This
exclamation, which occurs twenty-four times in the Psalms, is used in
the Mass as an acclamation greeting the Word of the Lord in the
Gospel.]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[HOMILY]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Greek [homilein]{.italic} = to exhort someone, speak to him as a
peer, converse with him): Homily is another word for sermon. Within the
Eucharistic liturgy, the preacher has the task of proclaiming the Good
News (Greek [evangelion]{.italic}) and helping the faithful and
encouraging them to recognize and accept the practical consequences of
the Word of God that they have just heard. During Holy Mass the homily
is reserved to the priest or deacon; in other settings, Christian laity
may also preach.]{.small}

[215]{.bold1} [Who leads the celebration of the Eucharist]{.italic}?

[Actually Christ himself acts in every celebration of the Eucharist. The
→[[BISHOP]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254778) or the
→[[PRIEST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464) represents
him. \[1348\]]{.bold1}

It is the →[C[HURCH'S]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459)
belief that the celebrant stands at the altar [in persona Christi
capitis]{.italic} (Latin = in the person of Christ, the Head). This
means that priests do not merely act in Christ's place or at his
command; rather, on the basis of their ordination, Christ himself, as
Head of the Church, acts through them.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[249-254](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos436248)

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[SANCTUS]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(Latin = holy): The [Sanctus]{.italic} is one of the most ancient parts
of the Mass. It originated in the eighth century b.c. (!) and can never
be omitted. The song is composed of the cry of the angels in Isaiah 6:3
and a greeting in Psalm 118:26 that is applied to the presence of
Christ.]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[TRANSSUBSTANTIATION]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Latin [trans]{.italic} = through, and [substantia]{.italic} =
essence, substance): the theological term used to explain theologically
how Jesus can be present under the appearance of the gifts of bread and
wine in the Eucharist. Whereas the "substances" (meaning the "essences")
of bread and wine are changed by the working of the Holy Spirit at the
words of consecration into the Body and Blood of Christ, their outward
"species" or forms remain the same. Jesus Christ is really but invisibly
present in what looks like bread and wine as long as the appearances of
bread and wine are preserved.]{.small}

[216]{.bold1} [In what way is Christ there when the Eucharist is
celebrated]{.italic}?

[Christ is mysteriously but really present in the
→[[SACRAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) of the
→[E[UCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475). As often
as the →[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459)
fulfills Jesus' command, "Do this in remembrance of me" (1 Cor 11:24),
breaks the bread and offers the chalice, the same thing takes place
today that happened then: Christ truly gives himself for us, and we
truly gain a share in him. The unique and unrepeatable sacrifice of
Christ on the Cross is made present on the altar; the work of our
redemption is accomplished. \[1362-1367\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00045.jpg){.calibre4}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[AGNUS DEI]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(Latin = Lamb of God): In Exodus 12 the lamb of God is the sacrifice by
which the people of Israel were freed from slavery in Egypt; John the
Baptist applied this image to Jesus (Jn 1:29, "Behold, the Lamb of
God. . ."). Through Jesus, who is led like a lamb to slaughter, we are
freed from our sins and find peace with God. In the Eucharistic liturgy,
the invocation of Christ, "Lamb of God", begins a litany-like prayer
that has been part of every Holy Mass in the Roman Rite since the
seventh century.]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[For as often as you eat this bread and
drink the chalice, you proclaim the Lord's death until he
comes.]{.small}

[1 Cor 11:26]{.small}

[217]{.bold1} [What happens in the Church when she celebrates the
Eucharist]{.italic}?

[Every time the Church celebrates the
→[E[UCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475), she stands
before the source from which she herself constantly springs anew. By
"eating" the Body of Christ, the Church becomes the Body of Christ,
which is just another name for the Church. In the sacrifice of Christ,
who gives himself to us, body and soul, there is room for our whole
life. We can unite everything---our work and our sufferings, our
joys---with Christ's sacrifice. If we offer ourselves in this way, we
are transformed: We become pleasing to God and like good, nourishing
bread for our fellowmen. \[1368-1372, 1414\]]{.bold1}

Again and again we grumble about the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459), as though she
were just an association of more or less good people. In reality the
Church is what happens daily in a mysterious way at the altar. God gives
himself to each one of us individually, and he wants to transform us
through →[[COMMUNION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos376549)
with him. Once we are transformed, we are supposed to transform the
world. Everything else that the Church is besides that is secondary.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[126](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos227699),
[171](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos307169),
[208](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos364532)

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[TABERNACLE]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(Latin [tabernaculum]{.italic} = hut, tent): In a way reminiscent of
the Old Testament Ark of the Covenant, the tabernacle developed in the
Catholic Church as a prominent, richly ornamented place for preserving
the Most Blessed Sacrament (Christ in the form of bread).]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[MONSTRANCE]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(Latin [monstrare]{.italic} = to show): a sacred object used to display
Christ in the form of consecrated bread on special occasions for
adoration by the faithful.]{.small}

[218 ]{.bold1}[What is the right way to honor the Lord present in the
bread and wine]{.italic}?

[Because God is truly present in the consecrated species of bread and
wine, we must preserve the sacred gifts with the greatest reverence and
worship our Lord and Redeemer in the Most Blessed Sacrament.
\[1378-1381, 1418\]]{.bold1}

If there are consecrated hosts left over after the celebration of Holy
→[E[UCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475), they are
kept in sacred vessels in the tabernacle. Since the Most Blessed
Sacrament is present in them, the
→[[TABERNACLE]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos393147) is one of
the most venerable places in every church. We genuflect before any
tabernacle. Certainly, anyone who is really following Christ will
recognize him in the poorest of the poor and serve him in them. But he
will also find time to spend in adoration before the tabernacle and
offer his love to our Eucharistic Lord.

[219 ]{.bold1}[How often must a Catholic Christian participate in the
celebration of the Eucharist]{.italic}?

[A Catholic Christian is obliged to attend Holy Mass on all Sundays and
holy days of obligation. Anyone who is really seeking Jesus' friendship
responds as often as possible to Jesus' personal invitation to the
feast. \[1389, 1417\]]{.bold1}

Actually, for a genuine Christian, "Sunday duty" is just as
inappropriate an expression as "kiss duty" would be for someone who was
truly in love. No one can have a living relationship with Christ without
going to the place where he is waiting for us. Therefore, from ancient
times the celebration of Mass has been the "heart of Sunday" and the
most important appointment in the week.

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[DOXOLOGY]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(Greek [doxa]{.italic} = glory): A doxology is the solemn, formulaic,
laudatory conclusion of a prayer, for instance, the conclusion of the
Eucharistic Prayer, which reads: [Through him, and with him, and in him,
to you, O God, almighty Father, in the unity of the Holy Spirit, is all
honor and glory, for ever and ever]{.italic}. Often doxologies are
addressed to the Holy Trinity, for instance, [Glory be to the Father,
and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, as it was in the beginning, is
now, and ever shall be, world without end]{.italic}, the formula that
usually concludes a Christian prayer.]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Lord, I am not worthy to have you come
under my roof; but only say the word, and my servant will be
healed.]{.small}

[Mt 8:8]{.small}

[Before receiving Holy Communion, every Catholic Christian recites an
adaptation of these words that the centurion spoke to Jesus: "Lord, I am
not worthy that you should enter under my roof, but only say the word
and my soul shall be healed."]{.small}

[220 ]{.bold1}[What sort of preparation do I need in order to be able to
receive Holy Eucharist]{.italic}?

[Someone who would like to receive Holy
→[E[UCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475) must be
Catholic. If he has a serious sin on his conscience, he must first make
a confession. Before approaching the altar, one should be reconciled
with his neighbors. \[1389, 1417\]]{.bold1}

Until a few years ago, the practice was to eat nothing for at least
three hours before Mass; that was how people prepared to encounter
Christ in Holy
→[C[OMMUNION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos376549). Today the
→[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459) recommends at
least one hour of fasting. Another sign of reverence is to wear one's
finest clothing---after all, we have a rendezvous with the Lord of the
world.

[221 ]{.bold1}[How does Holy Communion change me]{.italic}?

[Every Holy →[C[OMMUNION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos376549)
unites me more deeply with Christ, makes me a living member of the Body
of Christ, renews the graces that I received in Baptism and
→[C[ONFIRMATION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos353709), and
fortifies me for the battle against sin. \[1391-1397, 1416\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Whoever, therefore, eats the bread or
drinks the cup of the Lord in an unworthy manner will be guilty of
profaning the body and blood of the Lord. Let a man examine himself, and
so eat of the bread and drink of the cup.]{.small}

[1 Cor 11:27-28]{.small}

[222]{.bold1} [May the Eucharist be administered to non-Catholic
Christians also]{.italic}?

[Holy →[C[OMMUNION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos376549) is
the expression of the unity of the Body of Christ. To belong to the
Catholic →[C[HURCH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos220459), one
must be baptized in her, share her faith, and live in union with her. It
would be a contradiction if the Church were to invite to Communion
people who do not (yet) share the faith and life of the Church. It would
damage the credibility of the sign of the
→[E[UCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475).
\[1398-1401\]]{.bold1}

Individual Orthodox Christians may ask to receive Holy Communion at a
Catholic liturgy, because they share the Eucharistic faith of the
Catholic Church, although their Church is not yet in full communion with
the Catholic Church. In the case of members of other Christian
"ecclesial communities" or denominations, Holy Communion may be
administered to an individual if there is a grave necessity and evidence
of faith in the Real Presence in the Eucharist. Joint celebrations of
the Eucharist / Lord's Supper by Catholics and Protestants are the goal
and the wish of all ecumenical efforts; to anticipate them, however,
without having established the reality of the Body of Christ in one
faith and in the one Church is dishonest and therefore not allowed.
Other ecumenical liturgies, in which Christians of various denominations
pray together, are good and are also desired by the Catholic Church.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Our sharing in the Body and Blood of
Christ has no other purpose than to transform us into that which we
receive.]{.small}

[POPE ST. LEO THE GREAT\
(ca. 400-461)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[We break the one bread that provides
the medicine of immortality, the antidote for death, and the food that
makes us live forever in Jesus Christ.]{.small}

[ST. IGNATIUS OF ANTIOCH\
(?-107/117)]{.small}

[223 ]{.bold1}[In what way is the Holy Eucharist an anticipation of
eternal life]{.italic}?

[Jesus promised his disciples, and us with them, that we will one day
sit at table with him. Therefore every Holy Mass is a "memorial of the
blessed Passion" (Eucharistic Prayer I, called the Roman Canon), the
fullness of grace, and a pledge of future glory. \[1402-1405\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[There \[in your kingdom\] we hope to
enjoy forever the fullness of your glory, when you will wipe away every
tear from our eyes. For seeing you, our God, as you are, we shall be
like you for all the ages and praise you without end.]{.small}

[Eucharistic Prayer III]{.small}

[[CHAPTER TWO]{.bold1}]{.small}

The Sacraments of Healing

The Sacrament of Penance and Reconciliation

[224 ]{.bold1}[Why did Christ give us the sacrament of Penance and the
Anointing of the Sick]{.italic}?

[Christ's love is shown in the fact that he seeks the lost and heals the
sick. That is why he gave us the
→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) of healing
and restoration, in which we are freed from sin and strengthened in our
physical and spiritual weakness. \[1420-1421\]
]{.bold1}![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[67](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos137157)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["For the Son of man came to seek and to
save the lost."]{.small}

[Lk 19:10]{.small}

[225]{.bold1} [What names are there for the sacrament of
Penance]{.italic}?

[The sacrament of Penance is also called the
→[[SACRAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) of
Reconciliation, of forgiveness, of conversion, or of confession.
\[1422-1424, 1486\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["Those who are well have no need of a
physician, but those who are sick; I came not to call the righteous, but
sinners."]{.small}

[Mk 2:17]{.small}

[226]{.bold1} [But we have Baptism, which reconciles us with God; why
then do we need a special sacrament of Reconciliation]{.italic}?

[Baptism does snatch us from the power of sin and death and brings us
into the new life of the children of God, but it does not free us from
human weakness and the inclination to sin. That is why we need a place
where we can be reconciled with God again and again. That place is
confession.\[1425-1426\]]{.bold1}

It does not seem like a modern thing to go to confession; it can be
difficult and may cost a great deal of effort at first. But it is one of
the greatest graces that we can receive again and again in our life---it
truly renews the soul, completely unburdens it, leaving it without the
debts of the past, accepted in love, and equipped with new strength. God
is merciful, and he desires nothing more earnestly than for us, too, to
lay claim to his mercy. Someone who has gone to confession turns a
clean, new page in the book of his life.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[67-70](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos137157)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[If we say we have no sin, we deceive
ourselves, and the truth is not in us.]{.small}

[1 Jn 1:8]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[And the son said to him, "Father, I
have sinned against heaven and before you; I am no longer worthy to be
called your son." But the father said to his servants, "Bring quickly
the best robe, and put it on him; and put a ring on his hand, and shoes
on his feet."]{.small}

[Lk 15:21-22]{.small}

[227]{.bold1} [Who instituted the sacrament of Penance]{.italic}?

[Jesus himself instituted the sacrament of Penance when he showed
himself to his apostles on Easter day and commanded them, "Receive the
Holy Spirit. If you forgive the sins of any, they are forgiven; if you
retain the sins of any, they are retained" (Jn 20:22a-23). \[1439,
1485\]]{.bold1}

Nowhere did Jesus express more beautifully what happens in the sacrament
of Penance than in the parable of the Prodigal Son: We go astray, we are
lost and can no longer cope. Yet our Father waits for us with great,
indeed, infinite longing; he forgives us when we come back; he takes us
in again, forgives our sins. Jesus himself forgave the sins of many
individuals; it was more important to him than working miracles. He
regarded this as the great sign of the dawning of the kingdom of God, in
which all wounds are healed and all tears are wiped away. Jesus forgave
sins in the power of the Holy Spirit, and he handed that power on to his
→[[APOSTLES]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos38443). We fall into
the arms of our heavenly Father when we go to a
→[[PRIEST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464) and confess.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[314](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos530335),
[524](#YOUCAT_split_021.html_filepos849048)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[What is repentance? A great sorrow over
the fact that we are the way we are.]{.small}

[MARIE VON EBNER-ESCHENBACH]{.small}

[(1830-1916, Austrian writer)]{.small}

[228 ]{.bold1}[Who can forgive sins]{.italic}?

[God alone can forgive sins. Jesus could say "Your sins are forgiven"
(Mk 2:5) only because he is the Son of God. And
→[[PRIESTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464) can forgive
sins in Jesus' place only because Jesus has given them that authority.
\[1441-1442\]]{.bold1}

Many people say, "I can go directly to God; why do I need a priest?"
God, though, wants it otherwise. We rationalize our sins away and like
to sweep things under the rug. That is why God wants us to tell our sins
and to acknowledge them in a personal encounter. Therefore, the
following words from the Gospel are true of priests: "If you forgive the
sins of any, they are forgiven; if you retain the sins of any, they are
retained" (Jn 20:23).

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Some saints described themselves as
terrible criminals because they saw God, they saw themselves---and they
saw the difference.]{.small}

[BL. TERESA OF CALCUTTA]{.small}

[(1910-1997)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Penance is the second Baptism, the
baptism of tears.]{.small}

[ST. GREGORY NAZIANZEN]{.small}

[(330-390)]{.small}

[229 ]{.bold1}[What prepares a person for repentance]{.italic}?

[The insight into one's personal guilt produces a longing to better
oneself; this is called contrition. We arrive at contrition when we see
the contradiction between God's love and our sin. Then we are full of
sorrow for our sins; we resolve to change our life and place all our
hope in God's help. \[1430-1433, 1490\]]{.bold1}

The reality of sin is often repressed. Some people even think that guilt
feelings should be dealt with in a merely psychological way. But genuine
guilt feelings are important. It is like driving an automobile: When the
speedometer indicates that the speed limit has been exceeded, the
speedometer is not responsible, but the driver is. The closer we come to
God, who is all light, the clearer our dark sides come to light also.
Yet God is not a light that burns but, rather, a light that heals. That
