is why repentance impels us to go into the Light in which we will be
completely healed.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[312](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos528995)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Love covers a multitude of
sins.]{.small}

[1 Pet 4:8]{.small}

[230 ]{.bold1}[What is penance]{.italic}?

[Penance is making restitution or satisfaction for a wrong that has been
committed. Penance must not take place exclusively in my head; I must
express it in acts of charity and in solidarity with others. One does
penance also by praying, fasting, and supporting the poor spiritually
and materially. \[1434-1439\]]{.bold1}

Penance is often misunderstood. It has nothing to do with low
self-esteem or scrupulosity. Penance is not brooding over what a bad
person I am. Penance frees and encourages us to make a new start.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[After a fall, stand up again right
away! Do not leave sin in your heart for even a moment!]{.small}

[ST. JOHN VIANNEY\
(1786-1859)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[God esteems repentance so highly that
the slightest repentance in the world, as long as it is genuine, causes
him to forget any kind of sin, so that even the devils would have all
their sins forgiven, if only they could have remorse.]{.small}

[ST. FRANCIS DE SALES]{.small}

[(1567-1622)]{.small}

[231 ]{.bold1}[What are the two basic elements required for the
forgiveness of a Christian's sins to occur in the sacrament of
Penance]{.italic}?

[What is required for the forgiveness of sins is the person who
undergoes conversion and the
→[[PRIEST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464) who in God's
name gives him absolution from his sins.]{.bold1}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["If you forgive the sins of any, they
are forgiven; if you retain the sins of any, they are
retained."]{.small}

[Jn 20:23]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Repentance results from a knowledge of
the truth.]{.small}

[THOMAS STEARNS ELIOT]{.small}

[(1888-1965, American-English poet)]{.small}

[232]{.bold1} [What must I bring to a confession]{.italic}?

[Essential elements of every confession are an examination of
conscience, contrition, a purpose of amendment, confession, and penance.
\[1450-1460; 1490-1492; 1494\]]{.bold1}

The [examination of conscience]{.italic} should be done thoroughly, but
it can never be exhaustive. No one can be absolved from his sin without
real [contrition]{.italic}, merely on the basis of "lip-service".
Equally indispensable is the [purpose of amendment]{.italic}, the
resolution not to commit that sin again in the future. The sinner
absolutely must declare the sin to the confessor and, thus,
[confess]{.italic} to it. The final essential element of confession is
the [atonement]{.italic} or [penance]{.italic} that the confessor
imposes on the sinner to make restitution for the harm done.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[A sign of sincere repentance is
avoiding the occasion of sin.]{.small}

[ST. BERNARD OF CLAIRVAUX]{.small}

[(1090-1153)]{.small}

[233]{.bold1} [What sins must be confessed]{.italic}?

[Under normal circumstances, all serious sins that one remembers after
making a thorough examination of conscience and that have not yet been
confessed can be forgiven only in individual sacramental confession.
\[1457\]]{.bold1}

Of course there will be reluctance before making a confession.
Overcoming it is the first step toward interior healing. Often it helps
to think that even the
→[P[OPE]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254325) has to have the
courage to confess his failings and weaknesses to another priest---and
thereby to God. Only in life-or-death emergencies (for instance, during
an airstrike in wartime or on other occasions when a group of people are
in danger of death) can a priest administer "general absolution" to a
group of people without the personal confession of sins beforehand.
However, afterwards, one must confess serious sins in a personal
confession at the first opportunity.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[315-320](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos532386)

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[ABSOLUTION]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Latin [absolvere]{.italic} = to loosen, acquit): The absolution
of the priest is the sacramental forgiveness of one or more sins after
the penitent's confession of sins. The formula of absolution
reads:]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[God, the merciful Father, by the death
and Resurrection of his Son has reconciled the world to himself and sent
the Holy Spirit for the forgiveness of sins. Through the ministry of the
Church may he give you pardon and peace. And I absolve you from your
sins, in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy
Spirit.]{.small}

[234]{.bold1} [When is a Catholic obliged to confess his serious sins?
How often should one go to confession]{.italic}?

[Upon reaching the age of reason, a Catholic is obliged to confess his
serious sins. The Church urgently advises the faithful to do this at
least once a year. At any rate one must go to confession before
receiving Holy
→[C[OMMUNION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos376549) if one has
committed a serious sin. \[1457\]]{.bold1}

By "the age of reason", the Church means the age at which one has
arrived at the use of reason and has learned to distinguish between good
and bad.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[315-320](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos532386)

![](images/00046.jpg){.calibre4}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[It is not correct to think we must live
like this, so that we are never in need of pardon. We must accept our
frailty but keep on going, not giving up but moving forward and becoming
converted ever anew through the sacrament of Reconciliation for a new
start, and thus grow and mature in the Lord by our communion with
him.]{.small}

[POPE BENEDICT XVI,\
February 17, 2007]{.small}

[235]{.bold1} [Can I make a confession even if I have not committed any
serious sins]{.italic}?

[Confession is a great gift of healing that brings about closer union
with the Lord, even if, strictly speaking, you do not have to go to
confession. \[1458\]]{.bold1}

In Taizé, at Catholic conferences, at World Youth Day
celebrations---everywhere, you see young people being reconciled with
God. Christians who take seriously their decision to follow Jesus seek
the joy that comes from a radical new beginning with God. Even the
saints went to confession regularly, if possible. They needed it in
order to grow in humility and charity, so as to allow themselves to be
touched by God's healing light even in the inmost recesses of their
souls.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[We put off our conversion again and
again until death, but who says that we will still have the time and
strength for it then?]{.small}

[ST. JOHN VIANNEY\
(1786-1859)]{.small}

[236]{.bold1} [Why are priests the only ones who can forgive
sins]{.italic}?

[No man can forgive sins unless he has a commission from God to do so
and the power given by him to ensure that the forgiveness he promises
the penitent really takes place. The
→[[BISHOP]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254778), in the first
place, is appointed to do that and, then, his helpers, the ordained
→[[PRIESTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464). \[1461-1466,
1495\]]{.bold1}
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[150](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos272153),
[228](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos407615),
[249-250](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos436248)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Candor toward a brother must not be
mistaken for confession. The latter is made to the Lord of heaven and
earth in the presence of a man who is authorized to hear it.]{.small}

[BROTHER ROGER SCHUTZ\
(1915-2005)]{.small}

[237 ]{.bold1}[Are there sins that are so serious that not even the
average priest can forgive them]{.italic}?

[There are sins in which a man turns completely away from God and at the
same time, because of the seriousness of the deed, incurs
→[[EXCOMMUNICATION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos421323). When
a sin results in "excommunication", absolution can be granted only by
the →[[BISHOP]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254778) or a
→[[PRIEST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464) delegated by
him, and, in a few cases, only by the
→[P[OPE]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254325). In danger of
death, any priest can absolve from every sin and excommunication.
\[1463\]]{.bold1}

A Catholic who commits murder, for example, or cooperates in an abortion
automatically excludes himself from sacramental communion; the Church
simply acknowledges this fact. The purpose of "excommunication" is to
correct the sinner and to lead him back to the right path.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The closest thing to a father confessor
is probably a bartender.]{.small}

[PETER SELLERS\
(1925-1980, British actor)]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[EXCOMMUNICATION]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Latin [ex]{.italic} = out of and [communicatio]{.italic} =
participation, communion): the exclusion of a Catholic Christian from
the sacraments.]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Love Jesus! Have no fear! Even if you
had committed all the sins in this world, Jesus repeats these words to
you: Your many sins are forgiven, because you loved much.]{.small}

[ST. PADRE PIO OF PIETRELCINA]{.small}

[(1887-1968, one of the most popular saints of Italy)]{.small}

[238 ]{.bold1}[May a priest later repeat something he has learned in
confession]{.italic}?

[No. Under no circumstances. The secrecy of the confessional is
absolute. Any →[[PRIEST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464)
who would tell another person something he had learned in the
confessional would be excommunicated. Even to the police, the priest
cannot say or suggest anything. \[1467\]]{.bold1}

There is hardly anything that priests take more seriously than the seal
of the confessional. There are priests who have suffered torture for it
and have gone to their deaths. Therefore, you can speak candidly and
unreservedly to a priest and confide in him with great peace of mind,
because his only job at that moment is to be entirely "the ear of God".

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[However awkward confession may be, it
is the decisive place where one experiences anew the freshness of the
Gospel, where one is reborn. There we also learn to blow away our pangs
of conscience, just as a child blows a falling autumn leaf. There we
find the happiness of God, the dawn of perfect joy.]{.small}

[BROTHER ROGER SCHUTZ]{.small}

[(1915-2005)]{.small}

[239]{.bold1} [What are the positive effects of confession]{.italic}?

[Confession reconciles the sinner with God and the Church. \[1468-1470,
1496\]]{.bold1}

The second after absolution is like a shower after playing sports, like
the fresh air after a summer storm, like waking up on a sunlit summer
morning, like the weightlessness of a diver. . . . Everything is
contained in the word "reconciliation" (from a Latin verb meaning "to
bring back together, to restore"): we are at peace with God again.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[This was to fulfill what was spoken by
the prophet Isaiah, "He took our infirmities and bore our
diseases."]{.small}

[Mt 8:17]{.small}

The Sacrament of the Anointing of the Sick

[240 ]{.bold1}[How was "sickness" interpreted in the Old
Testament]{.italic}?

[In the Old Testament sickness was often experienced as a severe trial,
against which one could protest but in which one could also see God's
hand. In the prophets the thought appears that sufferings are not just a
curse and not always the consequence of personal sin, that by patiently
bearing sufferings one can also be there for others. \[1502\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[When Jesus heard it, he said to them,
"Those who are well have no need of a physician, but those who are sick;
I came not to call the righteous, but sinners."]{.small}

[Mt 2:17]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The sick person is able to sense more
than other people notice.]{.small}

[REINHOLD SCHNEIDER\
(1903-1958, German author)]{.small}

[241 ]{.bold1}[Why did Jesus show so much interest in the
sick]{.italic}?

[Jesus came in order to show God's love. He often did this in places
where we feel especially threatened: in the weakening of our life
through sickness. God wants us to become well in body and soul and,
therefore, to believe and to acknowledge the coming of God's kingdom.
\[1503-1505\]]{.bold1}

Sometimes a person has to become sick in order to recognize what we
all---healthy or sick---need more than anything else: God. We have no
life except in him. That is why sick people and sinners can have a
special instinct for the essential things. Already in the
→[N[EW]{.small}
T[ESTAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos45964) it was
precisely the sick people who sought the presence of Jesus; they tried
"to touch him, for power came forth from him and healed them all" (Lk
6:19).
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[91](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos171196)

[242]{.bold1} [Why should the Church take special care of the
sick]{.italic}?

[Jesus shows us: Heaven suffers with us when we suffer. God even wants
to be rediscovered in "the least of these my brethren" (Mt 25:40). That
is why Jesus designated care of the sick as a central task for his
disciples. He commands them, "Heal the sick" (Mt 10:8), and he promises
them divine authority: "In my name they will cast out demons;. . . they
will lay their hands on the sick, and they will recover" (Mk 16:17-18).
\[1506-1510\]]{.bold1}

One of the distinctive characteristics of Christianity has always been
that the elderly, the sick, and the needy are central to it. Mother
Teresa, who cared for those who were dying in the gutters of Calcutta,
is only one in a long series of Christian women and men who have
discovered Christ precisely in those who were marginalized and avoided
by others. When Christians are really Christian, a healing influence
goes out from them. Some even have the gift of healing others physically
in the power of the Holy Spirit (the charism of healing,
→[[CHARISM]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos205015)).

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[I would prefer even the worst possible
Christian world to the best pagan world, because in a Christian world
there is room for those for whom no pagan world ever made room: cripples
and sick people, the old and the weak. And there was more than room for
them: there was love for those who seemed and seem useless to the pagan
and the godless world.]{.small}

[HEINRICH BOLL\
(1917-1985, German writer)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Care for the sick must have priority
over everything else: They should be served as though they were really
Christ.]{.small}

[ST. BENEDICT OF NURSIA\
(ca. 480-547)]{.small}

[243]{.bold1} [For whom is the sacrament of the Anointing of the Sick
intended]{.italic}?

[The →[[SACRAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) of the
Anointing of the Sick can be received by any Catholic whose health is in
a critical state. \[1514-1515, 1528-1529\]]{.bold1}

One can receive the Anointing of the Sick several times in one's life.
Therefore it makes sense for young people to ask for this sacrament
also, if, for example, they are about to undergo a serious operation. On
such occasions many Catholics combine the Anointing of the Sick with a
(general) confession; in case the operation fails, they want to go to
meet God with a clear conscience.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[And we make one more promise that no
other man makes, for we promise to be servants and slaves to our lords,
the sick.]{.small}

[Rule for the Military Hospitaller Order of St. John of Jerusalem of
Rhodes and of Malta (= "Order of Malta")]{.small}

[244]{.bold1} [How is the Anointing of the Sick administered]{.italic}?

[The essential ritual by which the
→[[SACRAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) of the
Anointing of the Sick is administered consists of an anointing of the
forehead and hands with holy oil, accompanied by prayers. \[1517-1519,
1531\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Is any among you sick? Let him call for
the elders of the Church, and let them pray over him, anointing him with
oil in the name of the Lord.]{.small}

[Jas 5:14]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Even though I walk through the valley
of the shadow of death, I fear no evil; for you are with me.]{.small}

[Ps 23:4]{.small}

[245]{.bold1} [How does the Anointing of the Sick work]{.italic}?

[The Anointing of the Sick imparts consolation, peace, and strength and
unites the sick person, in his precarious situation and his sufferings,
with Christ in a profound way. For the Lord experienced our fears and
bore our pains in his body. For many people the Anointing of the Sick
brings about physical healing. But if God should decide to call someone
home to himself, he gives him in the Anointing of the Sick the strength
for all the physical and spiritual battles on his final journey. In any
case, the Anointing of the Sick has the effect of forgiving sins.
\[1520-1523, 1532\]]{.bold1}

Many sick people are afraid of this
→[[SACRAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) and put it
off until the last minute because they think it is a sort of death
sentence. But the opposite is true: the Anointing of the Sick is a sort
of life insurance. A Christian who is caring for a sick person should
relieve him of any false fear. Most people in serious danger sense
intuitively that nothing is more important for them at the moment than
to embrace immediately and unconditionally the One who overcame death
and is life itself: Jesus, the Savior.

[246]{.bold1} [Who can administer the Anointing of the Sick]{.italic}?

[Administering the Anointing of the Sick is reserved to bishops and
priests, for it is Christ who acts through them by virtue of their
ordination. \[1516, 1530\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Through this holy anointing may the
Lord in his love and mercy help you with the grace of the Holy
Spirit. . . . May the Lord who frees you from sin save you and raise you
up.]{.small}

[From the Rite of the Anointing of the Sick]{.small}

[247]{.bold1} [What is meant by "Viaticum"]{.italic}?

[Viaticum means the last Holy
→[C[OMMUNION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos376549) that a
person receives before dying. \[1524-1525\]]{.bold1}

Rarely is Communion so vitally necessary as in the moment when a person
sets out on the path that completes his earthly life: In the future he
will have only as much life as he has in union (= communion) with God.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[He who eats my flesh and drinks my
blood has eternal life, and I will raise him up at the last
day.]{.small}

[Jn 6:54]{.small}

[[CHAPTER THREE]{.small}]{.bold1}

The Sacraments of Communion and Mission

[248 ]{.bold1}[What are the names of the sacraments that serve to build
up communion in the Church]{.italic}?

[Someone who is baptized and confirmed can receive moreover a special
mission in the Church in two special sacraments and thus be enlisted in
the service of God: Holy Orders and Matrimony. \[1533-1535\]]{.bold1}

The two →[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200)
have something in common: They are directed [to the good of
others]{.italic}. No one is ordained just for himself, and no one enters
the married state merely for his own sake. The sacrament of Holy Orders
and the sacrament of Matrimony are supposed to build up the People of
God; in other words, they are a channel through which God pours out love
into the world.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[I will bless you . . . so that you will
be a blessing.]{.small}

[Gen 12:2b]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Only Christ \[is\] truly priest. But
the others are his ministers.]{.small}

[ST. THOMAS AQUINAS\
(1225-1274)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Only Christ is the true priest, the
others being only his ministers.]{.small}

[ST. THOMAS AQUINAS\
(1225-1274)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Priestly ordination is administered as
a means of salvation, not for an individual man, but rather for the
whole Church.]{.small}

[ST. THOMAS AQUINAS\
(1225-1274)]{.small}

The Sacrament of Holy Orders

[249 ]{.bold1}[What happens in Holy Orders]{.italic}?

[The man who is ordained receives a gift of the Holy Spirit that gives
him a sacred authority that is conferred upon him by Christ through the
bishop. \[1538\]]{.bold1}

Being a →[[PRIEST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464) does
not mean just assuming an office or a ministry. Through Holy Orders a
priest receives as a gift a definite power and a mission for his
brothers and sisters in faith.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[150](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos272153),
[215](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos387668),
[228](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos407615),
[236](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos418913)

![](images/00047.jpg){.calibre4}

[250]{.bold1} [How does the Church understand the sacrament of Holy
Orders]{.italic}?

[The priests of the Old Covenant saw their duty as mediating between
heavenly and earthly things, between God and his people. Since Christ is
the "one mediator between God and men" (1 Tim 2:5), he perfected and
[ended]{.italic} that priesthood. After Christ there can be an ordained
priesthood only [in]{.italic} Christ, [in]{.italic} Christ's sacrifice
on the Cross, and [through]{.italic} a calling and apostolic mission
from Christ. \[1539-1553, 1592\]]{.bold1}

A Catholic →[[PRIEST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464) who
administers the
→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) acts not
on the basis of his own power or moral perfection (which unfortunately
he often lacks), but rather "in persona Christi". Through his
ordination, the transforming, healing, saving power of Christ is grafted
onto him. Because a priest has nothing of his own, he is above all a
[servant]{.italic}. The distinguishing characteristic of every authentic
priest, therefore, is humble astonishment at his own vocation.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[215](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos387668)

[251]{.bold1} [What are the degrees of the sacrament of Holy
Orders]{.italic}?

[The sacrament of Holy Orders has three degrees:
→[[BISHOP]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254778)
([episcopate]{.italic}),
→[[PRIEST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464)
([presbyterate]{.italic}),
→[[DEACON]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257061)
([diaconate]{.italic}). \[1554, 1593\]]{.bold1}
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[140](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos252555)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[If someone wants to destroy religion,
he begins by attacking priests, for wherever there are no more priests,
there is no sacrifice, either, and where there is no longer a sacrifice,
there is no religion, either.]{.small}

[ST. JOHN VIANNEY\
(1786-1859)]{.small}

[252]{.bold1} [What happens in episcopal ordination]{.italic}?

[In episcopal ordination the fullness of the sacrament of Holy Orders is
conferred upon a
→[[PRIEST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464). He is
ordained a successor of the
→[[APOSTLES]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos38443) and enters
the college of bishops. Together with the other bishops and the
→[P[OPE]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254325), he is from now
on responsible for the entire Church. In particular the Church appoints
him to the offices of teaching, sanctifying, and governing.
\[1555-1559\]]{.bold1}

The episcopal ministry is the real pastoral ministry in the Church, for
it goes back to the original witnesses to Jesus, the apostles, and
continues the pastoral ministry of the apostles that was instituted by
Christ. The Pope, too, is a
→[[BISHOP]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254778), but the first
among them and the head of the college.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[92](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos173422),
[137](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos247395)

[253]{.bold1} [How important for a Catholic Christian is his
bishop]{.italic}?

[A Catholic Christian feels that he is under an obligation to his
→[[BISHOP]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254778); the bishop is
appointed for him, too, as Christ's representative. Moreover, the
bishop, who exercises his pastoral ministry together with
→[[PRIESTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464) and
→[[DEACONS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257061) as his
ordained assistants, is the visible principle and the foundation of the
local Church (diocese). \[1560-1561\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[You must all follow the lead of the
bishop, as Jesus Christ followed that of the Father; follow the
presbyterate as you would the apostles; reverence the deacons as you
would God's commandment. Let no one do anything touching the Church
apart from the bishop.]{.small}

[ST. IGNATIUS OF ANTIOCH\
(d. 107)]{.small}

[254]{.bold1} [What happens in priestly ordination]{.italic}?

[In priestly ordination the
→[[BISHOP]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254778) calls down
God's power upon the candidates for ordination. It imprints upon the
souls of these men an indelible seal that can never be lost. As a
collaborator with his bishop, the
→[[PRIEST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464) will proclaim
the Word of God, administer the
→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200), and,
above all, celebrate the Holy
→[[EUCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475).
\[1562-1568\]]{.bold1}

During the celebration of a Holy Mass, the actual ordination of priests
begins when the candidates are called by name. After the bishop's
homily, the future priest promises obedience to the bishop and his
successors. The actual ordination takes place through the imposition of
the bishop's hands and his prayer.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[215](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos387668),
[236](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos418913),
[259](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos451543)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The priest continues \[Christ's\] work
of redemption on earth.]{.small}

[ST. JOHN VIANNEY\
(1786-1859)]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[DEACON]{.bold1}]{.small}

[The deacon (Greek [diakonos]{.italic} = servant) is the first degree in
the sacrament of Holy Orders in the Catholic Church. As the name itself
implies, a deacon is involved especially in charitable work
([diakonia]{.italic}), yet he also teaches and catechizes, proclaims the
Gospel, preaches at Mass, and assists during the liturgy.]{.small}

[255]{.bold1} [What happens in diaconal ordination]{.italic}?

[In diaconal ordination the candidate is appointed to a special service
within the sacrament of Holy Orders. For he represents Christ as the one
who came, "not to be served but to serve, and to give his life as a
ransom for many" (Mt 20:28). In the liturgy of ordination we read: "As a
minister of the Word, of the altar, and of charity, \[the
→[[DEACON]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257061)\] will make
himself a servant to all."]{.bold1}

The original model of the deacon is the martyr St. Stephen. When the
→[[APOSTLES]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos38443) in the
original Church of Jerusalem saw that they were overwhelmed by their
many charitable duties, they appointed seven men "to serve tables", whom
they then ordained. The first mentioned is Stephen: "full of grace and
power", he accomplished much for the new faith and for the poor in the
Christian community. Over the centuries the diaconate became merely a
degree of Holy Orders on the way to the presbyterate, but today it is
once again an independent vocation for both celibates and married men.
On the one hand, this is supposed to reemphasize service as a
characteristic of the Church; on the other hand, it helps the priests,
as in the early Church, by establishing an order of ministers who take
on particular pastoral and social duties of the Church. Diaconal
ordination, too, makes a lifelong, irrevocable mark on the ordained man.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[140](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos252555)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Deacons likewise must be serious, not
double-tongued, not addicted to much wine, not greedy for gain. . . .
Let deacons be the husband of one wife, and let them manage their
children and their households well.]{.small}

[1 Tim 3:8, 12]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[No one could have been a better priest
than she was. She could say without hesitation, "This is my Body",
because she really did give Jesus her own body. And yet Mary remained
the simple handmaid of the Lord, so that we can always turn to her as
our mother. She is one of us, and we are always united with her. After
the death of her Son, she continued to live on earth so as to strengthen
the apostles in their ministry, to be their mother, until the young
Church had taken shape.]{.small}

[BL. TERESA OF CALCUTTA]{.small}

[(1910-1997)]{.small}

[256]{.bold1} [Who can receive the sacrament of Holy Orders]{.italic}?

[A baptized, Catholic man who is called by the Church to be a
→[[DEACON]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257061),
→[[PRIEST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464), or
→[[BISHOP]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos254778) can be validly
ordained to that ministry. \[1577-1578\]]{.bold1}

[257]{.bold1} [Is it demeaning to women that only men may receive the
sacrament of Holy Orders]{.italic}?

[The rule that only men may receive Holy Orders in no way demeans women.
In God's sight, man and woman have the same dignity, but they have
different duties and
→[[CHARISMS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos205015). The Church
sees herself as bound by the fact that Jesus chose
]{.bold1}[[men]{.italic} ]{.bold1}[exclusively to be present at the Last
Supper for the institution of the priesthood. Pope John Paul II declared
in 1994 "that the Church has no authority whatsoever to confer priestly
ordination on women and that this judgment is to be definitively held by
all the Church's faithful".]{.bold1}

Like no one else in antiquity, Jesus provocatively affirmed the value of
women, bestowed his friendship on them, and protected them. Women were
among his followers, and Jesus highly valued their faith. Moreover, the
first witness to the Resurrection was a woman. That is why Mary
Magdalene is called "the apostle of the
→[[APOSTLES]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos38443)".
Nevertheless, the ordained priesthood (and consequently pastoral
ministry) has always been conferred on men. In male priests the
Christian community was supposed to see a representation of Jesus
Christ. Being a priest is a special service that also makes demands on a
man in his gender-specific role as male and father. It is, however, not
some form of masculine superiority over women. As we see in Mary, women
play a role in the Church that is no less central than the masculine
role, but it is feminine. Eve became the mother of all the living (Gen
3:20). As "mothers of all the living", women have special gifts and
abilities. Without their sort of teaching, preaching, charity,
→[[SPIRITUALITY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_021.html_filepos814006), and
guidance, the Church would be "paralyzed on one side". Whenever men in
the Church use their priestly ministry as an instrument of power or do
not allow opportunities to women, they offend against charity and the
Holy Spirit of Jesus.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[64](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos129869)

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[CELIBACY]{.bold1}]{.small}

[Celibacy (from Latin [caelebs]{.italic} = a single, unmarried person)
is the personal commitment of a person to live in the unmarried state
"for the sake of the kingdom of heaven". In the Catholic Church this
promise is lived out especially by people in religious orders (who take
religious vows) and by the clergy (who make a promise of
celibacy).]{.small}

[258]{.bold1} [Why does the Church require priests and bishops to live a
celibate life]{.italic}?

[Jesus lived as a celibate and in this way intended to show his
undivided love for God the Father. To follow Jesus' way of life and to
live in unmarried chastity "for the sake of the kingdom of heaven" (Mt
19:12) has been since Jesus' time a sign of love, of undivided devotion
to the Lord, and of a complete willingness to serve. The Roman Catholic
Church requires this way of life of its bishops and priests, while the
Eastern Catholic Churches demand it only of their bishops. \[1579-1580,
1599\]]{.bold1}

Celibacy, says Pope Benedict, cannot mean "remaining empty in love, but
rather must mean allowing oneself to be overcome by a passion for God".
A →[[PRIEST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464) who lives as
a celibate should be fruitful inasmuch as he represents the fatherly
character of God and Jesus. The Pope goes on to say, "Christ needs
priests who are mature and manly, capable of exercising a true spiritual
fatherhood."

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Does the Catholic Church know what a
radical reversal of values she would introduce \[by abolishing
celibacy\]? The celibacy of priests, the folly of the Gospel, has
preserved a hidden reality within it. The Church has thereby directed
herself toward the invisible, toward the mystery of Christ.]{.small}

[BROTHER ROGER SCHUTZ]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Like living stones be yourselves built
into a spiritual house, to be a holy priesthood, to offer spiritual
sacrifices acceptable to God through Jesus Christ.]{.small}

[1 Pet 2:5]{.small}

[259]{.bold1} [How is the universal priesthood of all the faithful
different from the ordained priesthood]{.italic}?

[Through Baptism Christ has made us into a kingdom of "priests to his
God and Father" (Rev 1:6). Through the universal priesthood, every
Christian is called to work in the world in God's name and to bring
→[[BLESSINGS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos306451) and grace
to it. In the Upper Room during the Last Supper and when he commissioned
the →[[APOSTLES]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos38443), however,
Christ equipped some with a sacred authority to serve the faithful;
these ordained priests represent Christ as pastors (shepherds) of his
people and as head of his Body, the Church. \[1546-1553, 1592\]]{.bold1}

Using the same word, →
"[[PRIEST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464)", for two
related things that nevertheless "differ essentially and not only in
degree" (Second Vatican Council, LG 10, 2) often leads to
misunderstandings. On the one hand, we should observe with joy that all
the baptized are "priests" because we live in Christ and share in
everything he is and does. Why, then, do we not call down a permanent
blessing on this world? On the other hand, we must rediscover God's gift
to his Church, the ordained priests, who represent the Lord himself
among us.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[138](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos248389)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[I am created to do or to be something
for which no one else is created: I have a place in God's counsels, in
God's world, which no one else has.]{.small}

[BL. JOHN HENRY NEWMAN\
(1801-1890)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[One drop of love is more than an ocean
of understanding.]{.small}

[BLAISE PASCAL\
(1588-1651)]{.small}

The Sacrament of Marriage

[260]{.bold1} [Why did God dispose man and woman for each
other]{.italic}?

[God disposed man and woman for each other so that they might be "no
longer two but one" (Mt 19:6). In this way they are to live in love, be
fruitful, and thus become a sign of God himself, who is nothing but
overflowing love. \[1601-1605\]]{.bold1}
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[64](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos129869),
[400](#YOUCAT_split_018.html_filepos658373),
[417](#YOUCAT_split_018.html_filepos686433)

![](images/00048.jpg){.calibre4}

[261]{.bold1} [How does the sacrament of Matrimony come about]{.italic}?

[The →[[SACRAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) of
Matrimony comes about through a promise made by a man and a woman before
God and the Church, which is accepted and confirmed by God and
consummated by the bodily union of the couple. Because God himself forms
the bond of sacramental marriage, it is binding until the death of one
of the partners. \[1625-1631\]]{.bold1}

The man and the woman mutually administer the sacrament of Matrimony.
The →[[PRIEST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464) or the
→[[DEACON]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257061) calls down
God's →[[BLESSING]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos306451) on the
couple and, furthermore, witnesses that the marriage comes about under
the right circumstances and that the promise is comprehensive and is
made publicly. A marriage can come about only if there is [marital
consent]{.italic}, that is, if the man and the woman enter marriage of
their own free will, without fear or coercion, and if they are not
prevented from marrying by other natural or ecclesiastical ties (for
example, an existing marriage, a vow of celibacy).

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[How can I ever express the happiness of
a marriage joined by the Church. . .? How wonderful the bond between two
believers, now one in hope, one in desire, one in discipline, one in the
same service,. undivided in spirit and flesh, truly two in one flesh.
Where the flesh is one, one also is the spirit.]{.small}

[TERTULLIAN\
(160-after 220, Latin ecclesiastical writer)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[An "open" marriage is a marriage that
was never entered into.]{.small}

[THEODOR WEISSENBORN\
(b. 1933, German writer)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Love is perfected in fidelity.]{.small}

[SØREN KIERKEGAARD\
(1813-1855)]{.small}

[262]{.bold1} [What is necessary for a Christian, sacramental
marriage]{.italic}?

[A sacramental marriage has three necessary elements: (a) free consent,
(b) the affirmation of a lifelong, exclusive union, and (c) openness to
children. The most profound thing about a Christian marriage, however,
is the couple's knowledge: "We are a living image of the love between
Christ and the Church." \[1644-1654, 1664\]]{.bold1}

The requirement of [unity]{.italic} and [indissolubility]{.italic} is
directed in the first place against →
[[POLYGAMY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos458251), which
Christianity views as a fundamental offense against charity and human
rights; it is also directed against what could be called "successive
polygamy", a series of non-binding love affairs that never arrive at
one, great, irrevocable commitment. The requirement of [marital
fidelity]{.italic} entails a willingness to enter a lifelong union,
which excludes affairs outside the marriage. The requirement of
[openness to fertility]{.italic} means that the Christian married couple
are willing to accept any children that God may send them. Couples who
remain childless are called by God to become "fruitful" in some other
way. A marriage in which one of these elements is excluded at the
marriage ceremony is not valid.

![](images/00049.jpg){.calibre4}

[[MONOGAMY, POLYGAMY]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from the Greek [monos]{.italic} = one, [polys]{.italic} = many, and
[gamos]{.italic} = marriage): marriage to one person or marriage to
several persons at once. Christianity forbids polygamy, which is also
punished by the state as the crime of "bigamy" (Greek [bi]{.italic} =
two).]{.small}

[263]{.bold1} [Why is marriage indissoluble]{.italic}?

[Marriage is triply indissoluble: first, because the essence of love is
mutual self-giving without reservation; second, because it is an image
of God's unconditional faithfulness to his creation; and third, because
it represents Christ's devotion to his Church, even unto death on the
Cross. \[1605, 1612-1617, 1661\]]{.bold1}

At a time when 50 percent of marriages in many places end in divorce,
every marriage that lasts is a great sign---ultimately a sign for God.
On this earth, where so much is [relative]{.italic}, people ought to
believe in God, who alone is [absolute]{.italic}. That is why everything
that is not relative is so important: someone who speaks the truth
[absolutely]{.italic} or is [absolutely]{.italic} loyal. Absolute
fidelity in marriage is not so much a human achievement as it is a
testimony to the faithfulness of God, who is there even when we betray
or forget him in so many ways. To be married in the Church means to rely
more on God's help than on one's own resources of love.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Someone who loves a neighbor allows him
to be as he is, as he was, and as he will be.]{.small}

[MICHEL QUOIST\
(1921-1997, French priest and author)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[To love a person means to see him as
God intended him.]{.small}

[FYODOR M. DOSTOYEVSKY\
(1821-1881)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[To love someone means to be the only
one to see a miracle that is invisible to others.]{.small}

[FRANCOIS MAURIAC\
(1885-1979)]{.small}

![](images/00050.jpg){.calibre4}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Husbands, love your wives, as Christ
loved the Church and gave himself up for her, that he might sanctify
her, having cleansed her by the washing of water with the word. . . .
Even so husbands should love their wives as their own bodies.]{.small}

[Eph 5:25-26, 28]{.small}

[264 ]{.bold1}[What threatens marriages]{.italic}?

[What really threatens marriages is sin; what renews them is
forgiveness; what makes them strong is prayer and trust in God's
presence. \[1606-1608\]]{.bold1}

Conflict between men and women, which sometimes reaches the point of
mutual hatred in marriages, of all places, is not a sign that the sexes
are incompatible; nor is there such a thing as a genetic disposition to
infidelity or some special psychological disability for lifelong
commitments. Many marriages, however, are endangered by a lack of
communication and consideration. Then there are economic and societal
problems. The decisive role is played by the reality of sin: envy, love
of power, a tendency to quarrel, lust, infidelity, and other destructive
forces. That is why forgiveness and reconciliation, in confession as
well, is an essential part of every marriage.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[If we are faithless, he \[Jesus
Christ\] remains faithful---for he cannot deny himself.]{.small}

[2 Tim 2:13]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Christ has no hands but ours to do his
work today.]{.small}

[ANONYMOUS,\
14th century]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Cast all your anxieties on him, for he
cares about you.]{.small}

[1 Pet 5:7]{.small}

[265 ]{.bold1}[Are all people called to marriage]{.italic}?

[Not everyone is called to marriage. Even people who live alone can have
fulfillment in life. To many of them Jesus shows a special way; he
invites them to remain unmarried "for the sake of the kingdom of heaven"
(Mt 19:12). \[1618-1620\]]{.bold1}

Many people who live alone suffer from loneliness, which they perceive
only as a lack and a disadvantage. Yet a person who does not have to
care for a spouse or a family also enjoys freedom and independence and
has time to do meaningful and important things that a married person
would never get to. Maybe it is God's will that he should care for
people for whom no one else cares. Not uncommonly God even calls such a
person to be especially close to him. This is the case when one senses a
desire to renounce marriage "for the sake of the kingdom of heaven". Of
course a Christian vocation can never mean despising marriage or
sexuality. Voluntary celibacy can be practiced only [in]{.italic} love
and [out]{.italic} of love, as a powerful sign that God is more
important than anything else. The unmarried person renounces a sexual
relationship but not love; full of longing he goes out to meet Christ
the bridegroom who is coming (Mt 25:6).

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["Where you go I will go, and where you
lodge I will lodge; your people shall be my people, and your God my God;
where you die I will die, and there will I be buried. May the Lord do so
to me and more also if even death parts me from you."]{.small}

[Ruth 1:16-17]{.small}

[266 ]{.bold1}[How is a Church wedding celebrated]{.italic}?

[As a rule a wedding must take place publicly. The bride and bridegroom
are questioned as to their intention to marry. The
→[[PRIEST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464) or the
→[[DEACON]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257061) blesses their
rings. The bride and bridegroom exchange rings and mutually promise "to
be true in good times and in bad, in sickness and in health" and vow to
each other: "I will love you and honor you all the days of my life." The
celebrant ratifies the wedding and administers the
→[[BLESSING]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos306451).
\[1621-1624, 1663\]]{.bold1}

Here are some excerpts from one form of the Rite of Catholic Marriage:
[Celebrant]{.bold1}: N. and N., have you come here freely and without
reservation to give yourselves to each other in marriage?" [Bride and
bridegroom]{.bold1}: "Yes." [Celebrant]{.bold1}: "Will you love and
honor each other as man and wife for the rest of your lives?" [Bride and
bridegroom]{.bold1}: "Yes." The celebrant then asks the bride and
bridegroom together the following questions. "Will you accept children
lovingly from God and bring them up according to the law of Christ and
his Church?" [Bride and bridegroom]{.bold1}: "Yes."

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Difference of confession between the
spouses does not constitute an insurmountable obstacle for marriage,
when they succeed in placing in common what they have received from
their respective communities, and learn from each other the way in which
each lives in fidelity to Christ.]{.small}

[[CCC,\
1634]{.bold1}]{.small}

[267 ]{.bold1}[What should be done if a Catholic wants to marry a
non-Catholic Christian]{.italic}?

[Church approval must be obtained for the wedding. This is because a
so-called "mixed" (that is, interdenominational) marriage requires from
both partners a special fidelity to Christ, so that the scandal of
Christian division, which has still not been remedied, does not continue
in miniature and perhaps even lead to giving up the practice of the
faith. \[1633-1637\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[DISPENSATION]{.bold1}]{.small}

[A dispensation in Catholic canon law is an exemption from a Church law.
The authority competent to issue a dispensation is the local bishop or
the Apostolic See.]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Do not let the sun go down on your
anger, and give no opportunity to the devil.]{.small}

[Eph 4:26-27]{.small}

[268 ]{.bold1}[Can a Catholic Christian marry a person from another
religion]{.italic}?

[For Catholic believers, to enter into and live in marriage with a
person who belongs to another
→[[RELIGION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos22585) can cause
difficulties for their own faith and for their future children. Given
her responsibility for the faithful, the Church has therefore
established the impediment of disparity of religion. Such a marriage can
therefore be contracted validly only if a
→[[DISPENSATION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos467255) from
this impediment is obtained before the wedding. The marriage is not
sacramental. \[1633-1637\]]{.bold1}

[269 ]{.bold1}[May a husband and wife who are always fighting get a
divorce]{.italic}?

[The Church has great respect for the ability of a person to keep a
promise and to bind himself in lifelong fidelity. She takes people at
their word. Every marriage can be endangered by crises. Talking things
over together, prayer (together), and often therapeutic counseling as
well can open up ways out of the crisis. Above all, remembering that in
a sacramental marriage there is always a third party to the
bond---Christ---can kindle hope again and again. Someone for whom
marriage has become unbearable, however, or who may even be exposed to
spiritual or physical violence, may divorce. This is called a
"separation from bed and board", about which the Church must be
notified. In these cases, even though the common life is broken off, the
marriage remains valid. \[1629, 1649\]]{.bold1}

Indeed, there are also cases in which the crisis in a marriage
ultimately goes back to the fact that one spouse or both was not
eligible at the time of the wedding or did not fully consent to the
marriage. Then the marriage is invalid in the canonical (legal) sense.
In such cases an annulment procedure can be introduced at the diocesan
tribunal.
![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[424](#YOUCAT_split_018.html_filepos698274)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Yet the divorced and remarried continue
to belong to the Church, which accompanies them with special concern and
encourages them to live as fully as possible the Christian life through
regular participation at Mass, albeit without receiving communion,
listening to the word of God, eucharistic adoration, prayer,
participation in the life of the community, honest dialogue with a
priest or spiritual director, dedication to the life of charity, works
of penance, and commitment to the education of their children.
(paragraph 29)]{.small}

[BENEDICT XVI,\
[Sacramentum Caritatis]{.italic}]{.small}

[270 ]{.bold1}[What is the Church's stance on people who are divorced
and remarried]{.italic}?

[She accepts them lovingly, following Jesus' example. Anyone who
divorces after being married in the Church and then during the lifetime
of the spouse enters into a new union obviously contradicts Jesus' clear
demand for the indissolubility of marriage. The Church cannot abolish
this demand. This retraction of fidelity is contrary to the
→[E[UCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475), in which
it is precisely the irrevocable character of God's love that the Church
celebrates. That is why someone who lives in such a contradictory
situation is not admitted to Holy
→[C[OMMUNION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos376549). \[1665,
2384\]]{.bold1}

Far from treating all specific cases alike, Pope Benedict XVI speaks
about "painful situations" and calls on pastors "to discern different
situations carefully, in order to be able to offer appropriate spiritual
guidance to the faithful involved" (Apostolic Exhortation [Sacramentum
caritatis]{.italic}, 29).

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[No one is without a family in this
world: the Church is a home and family for everyone, especially those
who "labor and are heavy laden".]{.small}

POPE JOHN PAUL II (1920-2005),\
Apostolic Exhortation [Familiaris consortio]{.italic}

[271 ]{.bold1}[What does it mean to say that the family is a "Church in
miniature"]{.italic}?

[What the Church is on a large scale, the family is on a small scale: an
image of God's love in human fellowship. Indeed, every marriage is
perfected in openness to others, to the children that God sends, in
mutual acceptance, in hospitality and being for others.
\[1655-1657\]]{.bold1}

Nothing in the early Church fascinated people more about the "New Way"
of the Christians than their "domestic churches". Often someone
"believed in the Lord, together with all his household; and many . . .
believed and were baptized" (Acts 18:8). In an unbelieving world,
islands of living faith were formed, places of prayer, mutual sharing,
and cordial hospitality.
→[R[OME]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos258822), Corinth,
Antioch, the great cities of antiquity, were soon permeated with
domestic churches that were like points of light. Even today families in
which Christ is at home are the leaven that renews our society.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[368](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos613460)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[If you want someone to become
Christian, let him live for a year in your house.]{.small}

[ST. JOHN CHRYSOSTOM\
(349/350-407)]{.small}

[[CHAPTER FOUR]{.bold1}]{.small}

Other Liturgical Celebrations

[272 ]{.bold1}[What are sacramentals]{.italic}?

[Sacramentals are sacred signs or sacred actions in which a blessing is
conferred. \[1667-1672, 1677-1678\]]{.bold1}

Examples of sacramentals are holy water, the consecration of a bell or
an organ, the blessing of a house or an automobile, the blessing of
throats on the feast of St. Blaise, receiving ashes on Ash Wednesday,
palm branches on Palm Sunday, the Easter candle, and the blessing of
produce on the feast of the Assumption of the Blessed Virgin Mary.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[I was glad when they said to me, "Let
us go to the house of the Lord!" Our feet have been standing within your
gates, O Jerusalem!]{.small}

[Ps 122:1-2]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Be sober, be watchful. Your adversary
the devil prowls around like a roaring lion, seeking some one to devour.
Resist him, firm in your faith!]{.small}

[1 Pet 5:8-9]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[EXORCISM]{.bold1}]{.small}

[Exorcism (Greek [exorkismós]{.italic} = summoning out) is a prayer by
virtue of which a person is protected from the evil one or delivered
from the evil one.]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Popular piety is one of our strengths
because it consists of prayers deeply rooted in people's hearts. These
prayers even move the hearts of people who are somewhat cut off from the
life of the Church and who have no special understanding of faith. All
that is required is to "illuminate" these actions and "purify" this
tradition so that it may become part of the life of the Church
today.]{.small}

[POPE BENEDICT XVI,\
February 22, 2007]{.small}

[273 ]{.bold1}[Does the Church still practice exorcism]{.italic}?

[At every Baptism a so-called simple
→[[EXORCISM]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos475319) is
performed, a prayer in which the person being baptized is taken away
from the devil and is strengthened against the "principalities and
powers" that Jesus overcame. Major exorcism is a prayer offered by
Jesus' authority and in his strength through which a baptized Christian
is delivered from the influence and power of the devil; the Church
rarely uses this prayer, and only after the most rigorous examination.
\[1673\]]{.bold1}

The depiction of "exorcisms" in Hollywood films for the most part does
not reflect the truth about Jesus and the Church. It was often reported
about Jesus that he drove out demons. He had power over evil
principalities and powers and could deliver men from them. To the
apostles Jesus gave "authority over unclean spirits, to cast them out,
and to heal every disease and every infirmity" (Mt 10:1). The Church
does the same today whenever an authorized →
[[PRIEST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464) pronounces the
prayer of exorcism over a person who requests it. First, however, the
possibility must be excluded that the phenomenon is
[psychological]{.italic} in nature (a psychiatrist is competent in such
matters). An exorcism wards off [spiritual]{.italic} temptation and
oppression and delivers from the power of the evil one.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[90-91](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos170090)

[274]{.bold1} [How important is so-called "popular piety"]{.italic}?

[Popular piety, which is expressed in veneration for relics,
processions, pilgrimages, and devotions, is an important way in which
the faith becomes inculturated. It is good as long as it is in and of
the Church, leads to Christ, and does not try to "earn" heaven by works,
apart from God's grace. \[1674-1676\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[RELICS]{.bold1}]{.small}

[Relics (from Latin [relictum]{.italic} = remains) are remnants of the
bodies of saints or else objects that the saints used during their
lifetime.]{.small}

![](images/00051.jpg){.calibre4}

[275]{.bold1} [Is it permissible to venerate relics]{.italic}?

[The veneration of relics is a natural human need, a way of showing
respect and reverence to the persons who are venerated.
→[R[ELICS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos478405) of saints are
properly venerated when the faithful praise God's work in people who
have devoted themselves completely to God. \[1674\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Unwaveringly, the Church marches
forward on her pilgrim way between the world's persecutions and God's
consolations.]{.small}

[ST. AUGUSTINE\
(354-430)]{.small}

[276]{.bold1} [What is the purpose of a pilgrimage]{.italic}?

[Someone who goes on a pilgrimage "prays with his feet" and experiences
with all his senses that his entire life is one long journey to God.
\[1674\]]{.bold1}

In ancient Israel people made pilgrimages to the Temple in Jerusalem.
Christians adopted this custom. And so this developed, especially in the
Middle Ages, into a regular pilgrimage movement to the holy places
(above all to Jerusalem and to the tombs of the apostles in Rome and
Santiago de Compostela). Often people went on pilgrimage so as to do
penance, and sometimes their actions were affected by the false notion
that one had to justify oneself before God by tormenting and punishing
oneself. Today pilgrimages are experiencing a unique revival. People are
looking for the peace and the strength that come from those grace-filled
localities. They are tired of going it alone; they want to get out of
the rut of the daily routine, get rid of some ballast, and start moving
toward God.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[God's ways are the ways that he himself
walked and that we must now walk with him.]{.small}

[DIETRICH BONHOEFFER\
(1906-1945)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The Lord's Cross embraces the world;
his [Via Crucis]{.italic} crosses continents and epochs. In the Way of
the Cross, we cannot merely be spectators. We too are involved, so we
must seek our place: Where are we?]{.small}

[POPE BENEDICT XVI,\
April 14, 2006]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Your Cross The everlasting God has in
His wisdom foreseen from eternity the cross that He now presents to you
as a gift from His inmost heart. This cross He now sends you He has
considered with His all-knowing eyes, understood with His divine mind,
tested with His wise justice, warmed with loving arms and weighed with
His own hands to see that it be not one inch too large and not one ounce
too heavy for you. He has blessed it with His holy Name, anointed it
with His consolation, taken one last glance at you and your courage, and
then sent it to you from heaven, a special greeting from God to you, an
alms of the all-merciful love of God.]{.small}

[ST. FRANCIS OF SALES]{.small}

[(1567-1622)]{.small}

[277]{.bold1} [What are the Stations of the Cross]{.italic}?

[Following Jesus on his Way of the Cross by praying and meditating on
the fourteen Stations is a very ancient devotion in the Church, which is
practiced especially in Lent and Holy week. \[1674-1675\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00052.jpg){.calibre4}

![](images/00053.jpg){.calibre4}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[We are not at all separated from one
another \[by death\], because we all run the same course and we will
find one another again in the same place.]{.small}

[SIMEON OF THESSALONICA\
(d. 1429, theologian and mystic)]{.small}

[278]{.bold1} What is the [purpose of a Christian funeral]{.italic}?

[A Christian funeral is a service performed by the Christian community
for the benefit of its dead. It expresses the sorrow of the survivors,
yet it always has a Paschal character. Ultimately, we die in Christ so
as to celebrate with him the feast of the Resurrection.
\[1686-1690\]]{.bold1}

::: {#YOUCAT_split_014.html_calibre_pb_15 .mbppagebreak}
:::

[]{#YOUCAT_split_015.html}

![](images/00054.jpg){.calibre4}

[]{#YOUCAT_split_016.html}

## 3 {#YOUCAT_split_016.html_calibre_pb_17 .calibre12}

## How we are to have Life in Christ {.calibre21}

Questions 279-468

[Why We Are Put on Earth, What We Are Supposed to Do,\
and How God's Holy Spirit Helps Us to Do
It](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos484949)

and with All Your Soul, and with All Your
Mind](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos583391)

[You Shall Love Your Neighbor as
Yourself](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos610774)

[]{#YOUCAT_split_017.html}

[[SECTION ONE]{.bold1}]{.small}

Why We Are Put on Earth,\
What We Are Supposed to Do,\
And How God's Holy Spirit\
Helps Us to Do It

[279]{.bold1} [Why do we need faith and the sacraments in order to live
a good, upright life]{.italic}?

[If we were to rely only on ourselves and our own strength, we would not
get far in our attempts to be good. Through faith we discover that we
are God's children and that God makes us strong. When God gives us his
strength, we call this "grace". Especially in the sacred signs that we
call the →[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200),
God gives us the ability actually to do the good that we want to do.
\[1691-1695\]]{.bold1}

Since God saw our misery, he "delivered us from the dominion of
darkness" (Col 1:13) through his Son, Jesus Christ. He granted us the
opportunity to make a new start in fellowship with him and to walk the
path of love.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[172-178](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309763)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["Apart from me you can do
nothing."]{.small}

[Jn 15:5]{.small}

[Let nothing trouble you / Let nothing frighten you Everything
passes / God never changes Patience obtains all Whoever has God Wants
for nothing God alone is enough.]{.small}

[ST. TERESA OF AVILA]{.small}

[(1515-1582)]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[So God created man in his own image; in
the image of God he created him.]{.small}

[Gen 1:27]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[When God disappears, men and women do
not become greater; indeed, they lose the divine dignity, their faces
lose God's splendor. In the end, they turn out to be merely products of
a blind evolution and, as such, can be used and abused. This is
precisely what the experience of our epoch has confirmed for
us.]{.small}

[POPE BENEDICT XVI,]{.small}

[August 15, 2005]{.small}

[[CHAPTER ONE]{.bold1}]{.small}

The Dignity of the Human Person

[280]{.bold1} [What reasons do Christians give for human
dignity]{.italic}?

[Every person, from the first moment of his life in the womb, has an
inviolable dignity, because from all eternity God willed, loved,
created, and redeemed that person and destined him for eternal
happiness. \[1699-1715\]]{.bold1}

If human dignity were based solely on the successes and accomplishments
of individuals, then those who are weak, sick, or helpless would have no
dignity. Christians believe that human dignity is, in the first place,
the result of God's respect for us. He looks at every person and loves
him as though he were the only creature in the world. Because God has
looked upon even the least significant child of Adam, that person
possesses an infinite worth, which must not be destroyed by men.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[56-65](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos118775)

![](images/00055.jpg){.calibre4}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[God wants us to be happy. But where
does the source of this hope lie? It lies in a communion with God, who
lives in the depths of the soul of every man.]{.small}

[BROTHER ROGER SCHUTZ\
(1915-2005)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Happiness is not in us, nor is
happiness outside of us. Happiness is in God alone. And if we have found
him, then it is everywhere.]{.small}

[BLAISE PASCAL\
(1588-1651)]{.small}

[281 ]{.bold1}[Why do we yearn for happiness]{.italic}?

[God has placed in our hearts such an infinite desire for happiness that
nothing can satisfy it but God himself. All earthly fulfillment gives us
only a foretaste of eternal happiness. Above and beyond that, we should
be drawn to God. \[1718-1719, 1725\]]{.bold1}
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[1-3](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos18422)

[282 ]{.bold1}[Does Sacred Scripture speak about a way to
happiness]{.italic}?

[We become happy by trusting in Jesus' words in the Beatitudes.
\[1716-1717\]]{.bold1}

The Gospel is a promise of happiness to all people who wish to walk in
God's ways. Especially in the Beatitudes (Mt 5:3-12), Jesus has told us
specifically that eternal blessedness
(→[[BLESSING]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos306451)) is based
on our following his example and seeking peace with a pure heart.

[283]{.bold1} [What are the Beatitudes]{.italic}?

[Blessed are the poor in spirit,]{.bold1}

[for theirs is the kingdom of heaven.]{.bold1}

[Blessed are those who mourn,\
for they shall be comforted.]{.bold1}

[Blessed are the meek,]{.bold1}

[for they shall inherit the earth.]{.bold1}

[Blessed are those who hunger and thirst for\
righteousness, for they shall be satisfied.]{.bold1}

[Blessed are the merciful,\
for they shall obtain mercy.]{.bold1}

[Blessed are the pure in heart,\
for they shall see God.]{.bold1}

[Blessed are the peacemakers,]{.bold1}

[for they shall be called sons of God.]{.bold1}

[Blessed are those who are persecuted for\
righteousness' sake, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven.]{.bold1}

[Blessed are you when men revile you and persecute you and utter all
kinds of evil against you falsely on my account. Rejoice and be glad,
for your reward is great in heaven, for so men persecuted the prophets
who were before you. (Mt 5:3-12)]{.bold1}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[For he alone is the way that is worth
following,]{.small}

[the light that is worth lighting,]{.small}

[the life that is worth living,]{.small}

[and the love that is worth loving.]{.small}

[BL. TERESA OF CALCUTTA\
(1910-1997)]{.small}

[284]{.bold1} [Why are the Beatitudes so important]{.italic}?

[Those who yearn for the kingdom of God look to Jesus' list of
priorities: the Beatitudes. \[1716-1717, 1725-1726\]]{.bold1}

From Abraham on, God made promises to his people. Jesus takes them up,
extends their application to heaven, and makes them the program for his
own life: the Son of God becomes poor so as to share our poverty; he
rejoices with those who rejoice and weeps with those who weep (Rom
12:15); he employs no violence but rather turns the other cheek (Mt
5:39); he has mercy, makes peace, and thereby shows us the sure way to
heaven.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[To will all that God wills, and always
to will it, on all occasions and without reservations, is the kingdom of
God that is completely within.]{.small}

[FRANÇOIS FÉNELON]{.small}

[(1651-1715)]{.small}

[285]{.bold1} [What is eternal happiness]{.italic}?

[Eternal happiness is seeing God and being taken up into God's
happiness. \[1720-1724, 1729\]]{.bold1}

In God the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit there is unending life,
joy, and communion. To be taken up into it will be an incomprehensible,
infinite happiness for us men. This happiness is the pure gift of God's
grace, for we men can neither bring it about ourselves nor comprehend it
in its magnitude. God would like us to decide in favor of our happiness;
we should choose God freely, love him above all things, do good and
avoid evil insofar as we are able.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[52](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos113387),
[156-158](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos280813)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Man is so great that nothing on earth
can satisfy him. Only when he turns to God is he content.]{.small}

[ST. JOHN VIANNEY\
(1786-1859)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Only he who made man makes man
happy.]{.small}

[ST. AUGUSTINE]{.small}

[(354-430)]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[We shall see him as he is.]{.small}

[1 Jn 3:2]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Being free means
self-possession.]{.small}

[DOMINIQUE LACORDAIRE\
(1802-1861, famous Dominican preacher)]{.small}

[286]{.bold1} [What is freedom and what is it for]{.italic}?

[Freedom is the God-given power to be able to act of one's own accord; a
person who is free no longer acts under the influence of someone else.
\[1730-1733, 1743-1744\]]{.bold1}

God created us as free men and wills our freedom so that we might decide
wholeheartedly in favor of the good, indeed for the greatest "good"---in
other words, for God. The more we do what is good, the freer we become.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[51](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos110379)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The person who abandons himself totally
in God's hands does not become God's puppet, a boring "yes man"; he does
not lose his freedom. Only the person who entrusts himself totally to
God finds true freedom, the great, creative immensity of the freedom of
good. The person who turns to God does not become smaller but greater,
for through God and with God he becomes great, he becomes divine, he
becomes truly himself.]{.small}

[POPE BENEDICT XVI,\
December 8, 2005]{.small}

[287]{.bold1} [But doesn't "freedom" consist of being able to choose
evil as well]{.italic}?

[Evil is only apparently worth striving for, and deciding in favor of
evil only apparently makes us free. Evil does not make us happy but
rather deprives us of what is truly good; it chains us to something
futile and in the end destroys our freedom entirely. \[1730-1733,
1743-1744\]]{.bold1}

We see this in addiction: Here a person sells his freedom to something
that appears good to him. In reality he becomes a slave. Man is freest
when he is always able to say Yes to the good; when no addiction, no
compulsion, no habit prevents him from choosing and doing what is right
and good. A decision in favor of the good is always a decision leading
toward God.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[51](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos110379)

[288]{.bold1} [Is man responsible for everything he does]{.italic}?

[Man is responsible for everything he does consciously and voluntarily.
\[1734-1737, 1745-1746\]]{.bold1}

No one can be held (fully) responsible for something he did under
coercion, out of fear, ignorance, under the influence of drugs or the
power of bad habits. The more a person knows about the good and
practices the good, the more he moves away from the slavery of sin (Rom
6:17; 1 Cor 7:22). God desires that such free persons should (be able
to) take responsibility for themselves, for their environment, and for
the whole earth. But all of God's merciful love is also for those who
are not free; every day he offers them an opportunity to allow
themselves to be set free for freedom.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The good man is free, even if he is a
slave. The evil man is a slave, even if he is a king.]{.small}

[ST. AUGUSTINE]{.small}

[(354-430)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The way to the goal begins on the day
when you assume full responsibility for your actions.]{.small}

[DANTE ALIGHIERI]{.small}

[(1265-1321, Italian poet, author of the [Divine
Comedy]{.italic})]{.small}

[289]{.bold1} [Must we allow a person to use his free will, even when he
decides in favor of evil]{.italic}?

[For a person to be able to use his freedom is a fundamental right based
on his human dignity. An individual's freedom can be curtailed only if
the exercise of his freedom is detrimental to the freedom of others.
\[1738, 1740\]]{.bold1}

Freedom would be no freedom at all if it were not the freedom to choose
even what is wrong. It would violate the dignity of a man if we did not
respect his freedom. One of the central duties of the State is to
protect the liberties of all its citizens (freedom of religion, of
assembly, and association, freedom of opinion, freedom to choose one's
occupation, and so on). The freedom of one citizen is the limit to the
freedom of another.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The martyrs of the early Church died
for their faith in that God who was revealed in Jesus Christ, and for
this very reason they also died for freedom of conscience and the
freedom to profess one's own faith---a profession that no State can
impose but which, instead, can only be claimed with God's grace in
freedom of conscience. A missionary Church known for proclaiming her
message to all peoples must necessarily work for the freedom of the
faith.]{.small}

[POPE BENEDICT XVI,]{.small}

[December 22, 2005]{.small}

![](images/00056.jpg){.calibre4}

[290]{.bold1} [How does God help us to be free men]{.italic}?

[Christ wants us to be "set free for freedom" (see Gal 5:1) and to
become capable of brotherly love. That is why he sends us the Holy
Spirit, who makes us free and independent of worldly powers and
strengthens us for a life of love and responsibility. \[1739-1742,
1748\]]{.bold1}

The more we sin, the more we think only about ourselves and the less
well we can develop freely. In sinning we also become more inept at
doing good and practicing charity. The Holy Spirit, who has come down
into our hearts, gives us a heart that is filled with love for God and
mankind. We avail ourselves of the Holy Spirit as the power that leads
us to inner freedom, opens our hearts for love, and makes us better
instruments for what is good and loving.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[120](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos217510),
[310-311](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos525959)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[For you did not receive the spirit of
slavery to fall back into fear, but you have received the spirit of
sonship. When we cry, "Abba! Father!" it is the Spirit himself bearing
witness with our spirit that we are children of God.]{.small}

[Rom 8:15]{.small}

[291 ]{.bold1}[How can a person tell whether his action is good or
bad]{.italic}?

[A person is capable of distinguishing good actions from bad ones
because he possesses reason and a conscience, which enable him to make
clear judgments. \[1749-1754, 1757-1758\]]{.bold1}

The following guidelines make it easier to distinguish good actions from
bad ones: (1) [What I do]{.italic} must be good; a good intention alone
is not enough. Bank robbery is always bad, even if I commit that crime
with the good intention of giving the money to poor people. (2) Even
when what I do is truly good, if I perform the good action with a [bad
intention]{.italic}, it makes the whole action bad. If I walk an elderly
woman home and help her around the house, that is good. But if I do it
while planning a later break-in, that makes the whole action something
bad. (3) The [circumstances]{.italic} in which someone acts can diminish
his responsibility, but they cannot change at all the good or bad
character of an action. Hitting one's mother is always bad, even if the
mother has previously shown little love to the child.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[295-297](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos508050)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[In the Christian tradition,
"conscience", "con-scientia", means "with knowledge": that is, \[we\]
ourselves, our being is open and can listen to the voice of being
itself, the voice of God. . . . In the depths of our being, not only can
we listen to the needs of the moment, to material needs, but we can also
hear the voice of the Creator himself and thus discern what is good and
what is bad.]{.small}

[POPE BENEDICT XVI,\
July 24, 2007]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[His conscience is man's most secret
core and his sanctuary. There he is alone with God whose voice echoes in
his depths.]{.small}

[Second Vatican Council, GS]{.small}

[292 ]{.bold1}[May we do something bad so that good can result from
it]{.italic}?

[No, we may never deliberately do something evil or tolerate an evil so
that good can result from it. Sometimes there is no other course of
action but to tolerate a lesser evil in order to prevent a greater evil.
\[1755-1756, 1759-1761\]]{.bold1}

The end does not justify the means. It cannot be right to commit
infidelity so as to stabilize one's marriage. It is just as wrong to use
embryos for stem cell research, even if one could thereby make medical
breakthroughs. It is wrong to try to "help" a rape victim by aborting
her child.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[If a man is truly to will what is good,
he must be willing to do everything for that good or be willing to
suffer anything for that good.]{.small}

[SØREN KIERKEGAARD\
(1813-1855)]{.small}

[293]{.bold1} [Why did God give us "passions" or emotions]{.italic}?

[We have passions so that through strong emotions and distinct feelings
we might be attracted to what is right and good and repelled from what
is evil and bad. \[1762-1766, 1771-1772\]]{.bold1}

God made man in such a way that he can love and hate, desire or despise
something, be attracted by some things and afraid of others, be full of
joy, sorrow, or anger. In the depths of his heart man always loves good
and hates evil---or what he considers to be such.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[There is good without evil, but there
is nothing evil without good.]{.small}

[ST. THOMAS AQUINAS\
(1225-1274)]{.small}

![](images/00057.jpg){.calibre4}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Virtue is what one does passionately;
vice is what one cannot stop doing because of passion.]{.small}

[ST. AUGUSTINE\
(354-430)]{.small}

[294]{.bold1} [Is someone a sinner if he experiences strong passions
within himself]{.italic}?

[No, passions can be very valuable. They are designed to lead to and
reinforce good actions; only when they are disordered do the passions
contribute to evil. \[1767-1770, 1773-1775\]]{.bold1}

Passions that are ordered to the good become [virtues]{.italic}. They
then become the motive force of a life of fighting for love and justice.
Passions that overpower a person, rob him of his freedom and entice him
to evil, we call [vice]{.italic}.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[396](#YOUCAT_split_018.html_filepos653619)

[295 ]{.bold1}[What is conscience]{.italic}?

[Conscience is the inner voice in a man that moves him to do good under
any circumstances and to avoid evil by all means. At the same time it is
the ability to distinguish the one from the other. In the conscience God
speaks to man. \[1776-1779\]]{.bold1}

Conscience is compared with an inner voice in which God manifests
himself in a man. God is the one who becomes apparent in the conscience.
When we say, "I cannot reconcile that with my conscience", this means
for a Christian, "I cannot do that in the sight of my Creator!" Many
people have gone to jail or been executed because they were true to
their conscience.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[120](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos217510),
[290-292](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos501312),
[312](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos528995),
[333](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos557178)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Anything that is done against
conscience is a sin.]{.small}

[ST. THOMAS AQUINAS]{.small}

[(1225-1274)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[To do violence to people's conscience
means to harm them seriously, to deal an extremely painful blow to their
dignity. In a certain sense it is worse than killing them.]{.small}

[BL. JOHN XXIII]{.small}

[(1881-1963, the Pope who convoked the Second Vatican Council)]{.small}

[296 ]{.bold1}[Can someone be compelled to do something that is against
his conscience]{.italic}?

[No one may be compelled to act against his conscience, provided he acts
within the limits of the →[[COMMON
GOOD]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos546593). \[1780-1782,
1798\]]{.bold1}

Anyone who overlooks the conscience of a person, ignores it and uses
coercion, violates that person's dignity. Practically nothing else makes
man more human than the gift of being able personally to distinguish
good from evil and to choose between them. This is so even if the
decision, seen in an objective light, is wrong. Unless man's conscience
has been incorrectly formed, the inner voice speaks in agreement with
what is generally reasonable, just, and good in God's sight.
