![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[If . . . we feel responsibility, are
ashamed, are frightened, at transgressing the voice of conscience, this
implies that there is One to whom we are responsible, before whom we are
ashamed, and whose claims upon us we fear.]{.small}

[BL. JOHN HENRY NEWMAN]{.small}

[(1801-1890)]{.small}

[297]{.bold1} [Can a person form his conscience]{.italic}?

[Yes, in fact he must do that. The conscience, which is innate to every
person endowed with reason, can be misled and deadened. That is why it
must be formed into an increasingly fine-tuned instrument for acting
rightly. \[1783-1788, 1799-1800\]]{.bold1}

The first school of conscience is self-criticism. We have the tendency
to judge things to our own advantage. The second school of conscience is
orientation to the good actions of others. The correct formation of
conscience leads a man into the freedom to do what has been correctly
identified as good. With the help of the Holy Spirit and Scripture, the
Church over her long history has accumulated a vast knowledge about
right action; it is part of her mission to instruct people and also to
give them directions.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[344](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos573390)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Everything that has to do with morality
logically is derived ultimately from theology, at any rate not from
secular reasoning.]{.small}

[MAX HORKHEIMER]{.small}

[(1895-1973, German philosopher and sociologist)]{.small}

[298 ]{.bold1}[Is someone who in good conscience acts wrongly guilty in
God's sight]{.italic}?

[No. If a person has thoroughly examined himself and arrived at a
certain judgment, he must in any case follow his inner voice, even at
the risk of doing something wrong. \[1790-1794, 1801-1802\]]{.bold1}

God does not blame us for the objective harm that results from a wrong
judgment of conscience, provided that we ourselves are not responsible
for having a badly formed conscience. While it is quite true that
ultimately one must follow one's conscience, it must likewise be kept in
mind that people have swindled, murdered, tortured, and betrayed others
on the basis of what they wrongly suppose to be their conscience.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Fear not that thy life shall come to an
end, but rather fear that it shall never have a beginning.]{.small}

[BL. JOHN HENRY NEWMAN]{.small}

[(1801-1890)]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["Blessed are those who hunger and
thirst for righteousness, for they shall be satisfied."]{.small}

[Mt 5:6]{.small}

[299 ]{.bold1}[What is meant by a "virtue"]{.italic}?

[A virtue is an interior disposition, a positive habit, a passion that
has been placed at the service of the good. \[1803, 1833\]]{.bold1}

"You, therefore, must be perfect, as your heavenly Father is perfect"
(Mt 5:48). That means that we must change on our way to God. By our
human abilities we can do that only in fits and starts. With his grace
God supports the [human virtues]{.italic} and gives us, above and beyond
that, the so-called [supernatural virtues]{.italic}
(![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[305](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos520755)),
which help us to come closer to God and live more securely in his light.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[293-294](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos506055)

[300]{.bold1} [Why do we have to work to form our character]{.italic}?

[We must work at forming our character so that we can freely, joyfully,
and easily accomplish what is good. A firm faith in God, in the first
place, helps us to do this, but also the practice of the
[virtues]{.italic}, which means developing within ourselves, with God's
help, firm dispositions, not giving ourselves over to disorderly
passions, and directing our faculties of intellect and will more and
more consistently toward the good.\[1804-1805, 1810-1811, 1834,
1839\]]{.bold1}

The most important virtues are: prudence, justice, fortitude,
temperance. These are also called the "cardinal virtues" (from Latin
[cardo]{.italic} = hinge, or from [cardinalis]{.italic} = principal).

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[To live well is nothing other than to
love God with all one's heart, with all one's soul, and with all one's
efforts; from this it comes about that love is kept whole and
uncorrupted (through temperance). No misfortune can disturb it (and this
is fortitude). It obeys only \[God\] (and this is justice) and is
careful in discerning things, so as not to be surprised by deceit or
trickery (and this is prudence).]{.small}

[ST. AUGUSTINE\
(354-430)]{.small}

[301]{.bold1} [How does a person become prudent]{.italic}?

[A person becomes prudent by learning to distinguish what is essential
from what is non-essential, to set the right goals and to choose the
best means of attaining them. \[1806, 1835\]]{.bold1}

The virtue of prudence directs all the other virtues. For prudence is
the ability to recognize what is right. After all, someone who wants to
lead a good life must know what the "good" is and recognize its worth.
Like the merchant in the Gospel "who, on finding one pearl of great
value, went and sold all that he had and bought it" (Mt 13:46). Only a
prudent person can apply the virtues of justice, fortitude, and
moderation so as to do good.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Prudence has two eyes, one that
foresees what one has to do, the other that examines afterward what one
has done.]{.small}

[ST. IGNATIUS LOYOLA\
(1491-1556)]{.small}

[302]{.bold1} [How does one act justly]{.italic}?

[One acts justly by always making sure to give to God and to one's
neighbor what is due to them. \[1807, 1836\]]{.bold1}

The guiding principle of justice is: "To each his due". A child with a
disability and a highly gifted child must be encouraged in different
ways so that each may fulfill his potential. Justice is concerned with
equity and longs to see people get that to which they are entitled. We
must allow justice to govern our relations with God also and give him
what is his: our love and worship.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Justice without mercy is unloving;
mercy without justice is degrading.]{.small}

[FRIEDRICH VON BODELSCHWINGH\
(1831-1910 Lutheran theologian and founder of the Bethel
Hospitals)]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Preach the word, be urgent in season
and out of season.]{.small}

[2 Tim 4:2]{.small}

[303 ]{.bold1}[What does it mean to have fortitude]{.italic}?

[Someone who practices fortitude perseveres in his commitment to the
good, once he has recognized it, even if in the extreme case he must
sacrifice even his own life for it. \[1809, 1837\]]{.bold1}
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[295](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos508050)

[304 ]{.bold1}[Why is it virtuous to be moderate]{.italic}?

[Moderation is a virtue because immoderate behavior proves to be a
destructive force in all areas of life. \[1809, 1838\]]{.bold1}

Someone who is immoderate abandons himself to the rule of his impulses,
offends others by his inordinate desires, and harms himself. In the
→[N[EW]{.small}
T[ESTAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos45964) words like
"sobriety" and "discretion" stand for "moderation".

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[For the valiant man, fortune and
misfortune are like his right and left hands; he uses both.]{.small}

[ST. CATHERINE OF SIENA\
(1347-1380)]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[For the grace of God has appeared for
the salvation of all men, training us to renounce irreligion and worldly
passions, and to live sober, upright, and godly lives in this
world.]{.small}

[Tit 2:11-12]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[So faith, hope, love abide, these
three; but the greatest of these is love.]{.small}

[1 Cor 13:13]{.small}

[305]{.bold1} [What are the three supernatural virtues]{.italic}?

[The supernatural virtues are faith, hope, and charity. They are called
"supernatural" because they have their foundation in God, are directly
related to God, and are for us men the way by which we can reach God
directly. \[1812-1813, 1840\]]{.bold1}

[306]{.bold1} [Why are faith, hope, and charity virtues]{.italic}?

[Faith, hope, and charity, too, are genuine powers-bestowed by God, of
course---that a person can develop and consolidate with the grace of God
so as to obtain "the abundant life" (see Jn 10:10). \[1812-1813,
1840-1841\]]{.bold1}

[307]{.bold1} [What is faith]{.italic}?

[Faith is the power by which we assent to God, acknowledge his truth,
and commit ourselves personally to him. \[1814-1816, 1842\]]{.bold1}

Faith is the path created by God leading to the truth that is God
himself. Because Jesus is "the way, and the truth, and the life" (Jn
14:6), this faith cannot be merely an attitude or "confidence" about
something or other. On the one hand, the faith has definite contents,
which the Church professes in the
→[C[REED]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos64853) (= profession of
faith), and it is her duty to safeguard them. Anyone who wants to accept
the gift of faith, in other words, anyone who wants to believe,
acknowledges this faith, which has been preserved constantly through the
ages and in many different cultures. On the other hand, part of faith is
a trusting relationship to God with heart and mind, with all one's
emotional strength. For faith becomes effective only through charity,
practical love (see Gal 5:6). Whether someone really believes in the God
of love is shown, not in his solemn affirmations, but rather in
charitable deeds.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[He who says "I know him" but disobeys
his commandments is a liar, and the truth is not in him.]{.small}

[1 Jn 2:4]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["Every one who acknowledges me before
men, I also will acknowledge before my Father who is in
heaven."]{.small}

[Mt 10:32]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Eternal glory means that the door on
which we have knocked for a lifetime is finally opened to us.]{.small}

[C. S. LEWIS\
(1898-1963)]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Likewise the Spirit helps us in our
weakness; for we do not know how to pray as we ought, but the Spirit
himself intercedes for us with sighs too deep for words.]{.small}

[Rom 8:26]{.small}

[308]{.bold1} [What is hope]{.italic}?

[Hope is the power by which we firmly and constantly long for what we
were placed on earth to do: to praise God and to serve him; and for our
true happiness, which is finding our fulfillment in God; and for our
final home: in God. \[1817-1821, 1843\]]{.bold1}

Hope is trusting in what God has promised us in creation, in the
prophets, but especially in Jesus Christ, even though we do not yet see
it. God's Holy Spirit is given to us so that we can patiently hope for
the Truth.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[1-3](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos18422)

[309]{.bold1}[What is charity]{.italic}?

[Charity is the power by which we, who have been loved first by God, can
give ourselves to God so as to be united with him and can accept our
neighbor for God's sake as unconditionally and sincerely as we accept
ourselves. \[1822-1829, 1844\]]{.bold1}

Jesus places love above all laws, without however abolishing the latter.
Therefore St. Augustine rightly says, "Love, and do what you will."
Which is not at all as easy as it sounds. That is why charity, love, is
the greatest virtue, the energy that inspires all the other virtues and
fills them with divine life.

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[If I have all faith, so as to remove
mountains, but have not love, I am nothing.]{.small}

[1 Cor 13:2]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[God is love, and he who abides in love
abides in God, and God abides in him.]{.small}

[1 Jn 4:16]{.small}

[310]{.bold1} [What are the seven gifts of the Holy Spirit]{.italic}?

[The seven gifts of the Holy Spirit are wisdom, understanding, counsel,
fortitude, knowledge, piety, and fear of the Lord. With these the Holy
Spirit "endows" Christians, in other words, he grants them particular
powers that go beyond their natural aptitudes and gives them the
opportunity to become God's special instruments in this world.
\[1830-1831, 1845\]]{.bold1}

We read in one of Paul's letters: "To one is given through the Spirit
the utterance of wisdom, and to another the utterance of knowledge
according to the same Spirit, to another faith by the same Spirit, to
another gifts of healing by the one Spirit, to another the working of
miracles, to another prophecy, to another the ability to distinguish
between spirits, to another various kinds of tongues, to another the
interpretation of tongues" (1 Cor 12:8-10).
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[113-120](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos205878)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The most important hour is always the
present; the most significant person is precisely the one who is sitting
across from you right now; the most necessary work is always
love.]{.small}

[MEISTER ECKHART]{.small}

[(ca. 1260-1328)]{.small}

[311]{.bold1} [What are the fruits of the Holy Spirit]{.italic}?

[The →[[FRUITS OF THE]{.small} H[OLY]{.small}
S[PIRIT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos216517) are charity,
joy, peace, patience, kindness, goodness, generosity, gentleness,
faithfulness, modesty, self-control, and chastity (cf. Gal 5:22-23).
\[1832\]]{.bold1}

In the →["[FRUITS OF THE]{.small} H[OLY]{.small}
S[PIRIT]{.small}"](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos216517) the world can
see what becomes of people who let themselves be adopted, led, and
completely formed by God. The fruits of the Holy Spirit show that God
really plays a role in the life of Christians.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[120](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos217510)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Truly, truly, I say to you, he who
believes in me will also do the works that I do; and greater works than
these will he do, because I go to the Father.]{.small}

[Jn 14:12]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Draw your strength simply from your joy
in Jesus. Be happy and peaceful. Accept whatever he gives. And give
whatever he takes, with a big smile.]{.small}

[BL. TERESA OF CALCUTTA\
(1910-1997) to her coworkers]{.small}

[312]{.bold1} [How does a person know that he has sinned]{.italic}?

[A person knows that he has sinned through his conscience, which accuses
him and motivates him to confess his offenses to God. \[1797,
1848\]]{.bold1}
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[229](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos409241),
[295-298](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos508050)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[If we say we have no sin, we deceive
ourselves, and the truth is not in us.]{.small}

[1 John 1:8]{.small}

[313]{.bold1} [Why must a sinner turn to God and ask him for
forgiveness]{.italic}?

[Every sin destroys, obscures, or denies what is good; God, however, is
all-good and the author of all good. Therefore every sin goes against
God (also) and must be set right again through contact with him.
\[1847\]]{.bold1}
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[224-239](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos402593)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[If we confess our sins, he is faithful
and just, and will forgive our sins and cleanse us from all
unrighteousness.]{.small}

[1 Jn 1:9]{.small}

[314]{.bold1} [How do we know that God is merciful]{.italic}?

[In many passages in Sacred Scripture God shows that he is merciful,
especially in the parable of the merciful father (Lk 15) who goes out to
meet his prodigal son, accepts him unconditionally, and celebrates his
return and their reconciliation with a joyful banquet. \[1846,
1870\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Never doubt God's mercy!]{.small}

[ST. BENEDICT OF NURSIA\
(ca. 480-547, Founder of Western monasticism)]{.small}

Already in the →[O[LD]{.small}
T[ESTAMENT]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos45534) God says
through the prophet Ezekiel: "I have no pleasure in the death of the
wicked, but that the wicked turn from his way and live" (Ezek 33:11).
Jesus is sent "to the lost sheep of the house of Israel" (Mt 15:24), and
he knows that "Those who are well have no need of a physician, but those
who are sick" (Mt 9:12). Therefore he eats with tax collectors and
sinners, and then toward the end of his earthly life he even interprets
his death as an initiative of God's merciful love: "This is my blood of
the covenant, which is poured out for many for the forgiveness of sins"
(Mt 26:28).
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[227](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos405864),
[524](#YOUCAT_split_021.html_filepos849048)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Besides God's mercy there is no other
source of hope for mankind.]{.small}

[POPE JOHN PAUL II\
(1920-2005)]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Even if our hearts condemn us---God is
greater than our hearts, and he knows everything.]{.small}

[(See 1 Jn 3:20)]{.small}

[315 ]{.bold1}[What is a sin in the first place]{.italic}?

[A sin is a word, deed, or intention by which man deliberately and
voluntarily offends against the true order of things, as God's loving
providence has arranged them. \[1849-1851, 1871-1872\]]{.bold1}

To sin means more than to violate some rules about which men have
agreed. Sin turns freely and deliberately against God's love and ignores
him. Sin is ultimately "love of oneself even to contempt of God" (St.
Augustine), and in the extreme case the sinful creature says, "I want to
be like God" (see Gen 3:5). Just as sin burdens me with guilt, wounds
me, and by its consequences ruins me, so too it poisons and damages the
world in which I live. It becomes possible to recognize sin and its
seriousness by drawing near to God.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[67](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos137157),
[224-239](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos402593)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Many say, "I have done too much evil;
the dear Lord cannot forgive me." That is an outright blasphemy which
sets a limit to God's mercy. But it has none: it is infinite. Nothing
offends our dear Lord so much as to doubt his mercy.]{.small}

[ST. JOHN VIANNEY]{.small}

[(1786-1859)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Only someone who has seriously
meditated on how heavy the Cross is can understand how serious sin
is.]{.small}

[ST. ANSELM OF CANTERBURY]{.small}

[(1033/1034-1109)]{.small}

[316 ]{.bold1}[How can we distinguish serious sins (mortal sins) from
less serious (venial) sins]{.italic}?

[Serious sin destroys the divine power of love in a person's heart,
without which there can be no eternal beatitude. Hence it is also called
mortal sin. Serious sin breaks with God, whereas venial sin only strains
the relationship with him. \[1852-1861, 1874\]]{.bold1}

A serious sin cuts a person off from God. One requirement for such a sin
is that it be opposed to an important value, for instance, directed
against [life]{.italic} or [God]{.italic} (for example, murder,
blasphemy, adultery, and so on) and that it be committed with full
knowledge and full consent. Venial sins are opposed to secondary values
(honor, truth, property, and so on) or are committed without full
knowledge of their seriousness or without full consent of the will. Such
sins disrupt the relationship with God but do not sever it.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[I have just produced expensive ashes: I
have burned a five-hundred-franc note. Oh, that is not as bad as if I
had committed a venial sin.]{.small}

[ST. JOHN VIANNEY\
(1786-1859)]{.small}

[317 ]{.bold1}[How can a person be delivered from a serious sin and
reunited with God]{.italic}?

[In order to heal the break with God that is caused by a serious sin, a
Catholic Christian must be reconciled with God through confession.
\[1856\]]{.bold1}
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[224-239](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos402593)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[If there were no forgiveness of sins in
the Church, there would be no hope for eternal life and eternal
deliverance. Let us thank God, who gave his Church such a great
gift.]{.small}

[ST. AUGUSTINE\
(354-430)]{.small}

[318 ]{.bold1}[What are vices]{.italic}?

[Vices are negative habits that deaden and dull the conscience, incline
a person to evil, and habitually prepare him for sin.
\[1865-1867\]]{.bold1}

Human vices are found in connection with the capital sins of pride,
avarice, envy, anger, lust, gluttony, and sloth (or acedia, spiritual
boredom).

[319 ]{.bold1}[Are we responsible for the sins of other
people]{.italic}?

[No, we are not responsible for other people's sins, unless we are
guilty of misleading or seducing another person to sin or of cooperating
in it or of encouraging someone else to sin or of neglecting to offer a
timely warning or our help. \[1868\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[And so both virtue and vice are in our
power. For when the deed is in our power, so is the omission, and when
we can say No, we can also say Yes.]{.small}

[ARISTOTLE]{.small}

[(382-322 b.c., along with Plato, the greatest philosopher in
antiquity)]{.small}

[320 ]{.bold1}[Is there such a thing as structures of sin]{.italic}?

[Structures of sin exist only in a manner of speaking. A sin is always
connected with an individual person, who knowingly and willingly agrees
to something evil. \[1869\]]{.bold1}

Nevertheless, there are societal situations and institutions that are so
contradictory to God's commandments that we speak about "structures of
sin"---yet these, too, are the consequence of personal sins.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The greatest gift that man can have
this side of heaven is to be able to get along well with the people with
whom he lives.]{.small}

[BL. EGIDIO OF ASSISI\
(?-1262, one of the closest companions of St. Francis of
Assisi)]{.small}

[[CHAPTER TWO]{.bold1}]{.small}

Human Community

[321]{.bold1} [Can a Christian be a radical individualist]{.italic}?

[No, a Christian can never be a radical individualist, because man is by
nature designed for fellowship. \[1877-1880, 1890-1891\]]{.bold1}

Every person has a mother and a father; he receives help from others and
is obliged to help others and to develop his talents for the benefit of
all. Since man is God's "image", in a certain way he reflects God, who
in his depths is not alone but triune (and thus life, love, dialogue,
and exchange). Finally, love is the central commandment for all
Christians; through it we profoundly belong together and are
fundamentally dependent on one another. "You shall love your neighbor as
yourself" (Mt 22:39).

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Even if you are not afraid to fall
alone, how do you presume that you will rise up alone? Consider: two
together can accomplish more than one alone.]{.small}

[ST. JOHN OF THE CROSS\
(1542-1591)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Each of us is the result of a thought
of God. Each of us is willed, each of us is loved, each of us is
necessary.]{.small}

[POPE BENEDICT XVI]{.small}

[at his inauguration]{.small}

![](images/00058.jpg){.calibre4}

[322]{.bold1} [What is more important, society or the
individual]{.italic}?

[In God's sight every individual matters in the first place as a person
and only then as a social being. \[1881, 1892\]]{.bold1}

Society can never be more important than the individual person. Men may
never be means to a societal end. Nevertheless, social institutions such
as the State and the family are necessary for the individual; they even
correspond to his nature.

[323 ]{.bold1}[How can the individual be integrated into society in such
a way that he nevertheless can develop freely]{.italic}?

[The individual can develop freely in society if the "principle of
subsidiarity" is observed. \[1883-1885, 1894\]]{.bold1}

The principle of subsidiarity, which was developed as part of
→[C[ATHOLIC]{.small} S[OCIAL]{.small}
T[EACHING]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos542170), states: What
individuals can accomplish by their own initiative and efforts should
not be taken from them by a higher authority. A greater and higher
social institution must not take over the duties of a subordinate
organization and deprive it of its competence. Its purpose, rather, is
to intervene in a subsidiary fashion (thus offering help) when
individuals or smaller institutions find that a task is beyond them.

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[CATHOLIC SOCIAL TEACHING / SOCIAL
PRINCIPLES]{.bold1}]{.small}

[The Church's teaching about the ordering of life in society and about
the attainment of individual and social justice. Its four central
principles are: personhood, the common good, solidarity, and
subsidiarity.]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Today's justice is yesterday's charity;
today's charity is tomorrow's justice.]{.small}

[BL. ETIENNE-MICHEL GILLET]{.small}

[(1758-1792, priest and martyr)]{.small}

[324 ]{.bold1}[On what principles does a society build]{.italic}?

[Every society builds on a hierarchy of values that is put into practice
through justice and love. \[1886-1889, 1895-1896\]]{.bold1}

No society can last unless it is based on a clear orientation toward
values that are reflected in a just ordering of relationships and an
active implementation of this justice. Thus man may never be made into a
means to an end of societal action. Every society needs constant
conversion from unjust structures. Ultimately this is accomplished only
by love, the greatest social commandment. It respects others. It demands
justice. It makes conversion from inequitable conditions possible.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[449](#YOUCAT_split_018.html_filepos737653)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The Church values the democratic system
inasmuch as it ensures the participation of citizens in making political
choices and guarantees to the governed the possibility both of electing
and holding accountable those who govern them and of replacing them
through peaceful means when appropriate.]{.small}

[POPE JOHN PAUL II\
(1920-2005), Encyclical [Centesimus annus]{.italic}]{.small}

[325 ]{.bold1}[What is the basis for authority in society]{.italic}?

[Every society relies on a legitimate authority to ensure that it is
orderly, cohesive, and smooth-running and to promote its development. It
is in keeping with human nature, as created by God, that men allow
themselves to be governed by legitimate authority. \[1897-1902,
1918-1919, 1922\]]{.bold1}

Of course an authority in society must never originate in the raw
usurpation of power but must have legitimacy under law. Who rules and
what form of government is appropriate are left to the will of the
citizens. The Church is not committed to particular forms of government
but only says that they must not contradict the →[[COMMON
GOOD]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos546593).

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[There is no society without an ultimate
authority.]{.small}

[ARISTOTLE\
(382-322 b.c.)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[In all sciences and arts the end is a
good, and especially and above all in the highest of all---this is the
political science of which the good is justice, in other words, the
common interest.]{.small}

[ARISTOTLE\
(382-322 b.c.)]{.small}

[326 ]{.bold1}[When does an authority act legitimately]{.italic}?

[An authority acts legitimately when it works for the sake of the
→[[COMMON GOOD]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos546593) and
applies just methods of attaining the goals thereof. \[1903-1904,
1921\]]{.bold1}

The people in a State must be able to rely on the fact that they live
under a "government of laws", which has rules that are binding for all.
No one is obliged to obey laws that are arbitrary and unjust or that
contradict the natural moral order. In that case there is a right, or in
some circumstances even the duty, to resist.

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[THE COMMON GOOD]{.bold1}]{.small}

[The common good is the good that is shared by all in common. It
includes "the sum total of social conditions which allow people, either
as groups or as individuals, to reach their fulfillment more fully and
more easily" ([Gaudium et spes]{.italic}).]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["We must obey God rather than
men."]{.small}

[Acts 5:29]{.small}

[327 ]{.bold1}[How can the common good be promoted]{.italic}?

[The →[[COMMON GOOD]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos546593)
follows wherever the fundamental rights of the person are respected and
men can freely develop their intellectual and religious potential. The
common good implies that men can live in society with freedom, peace,
and security. In an age of globalization, the common good must also
acquire a worldwide scope and allow for the rights and duties of all
mankind. \[1907-1912, 1925, 1927\]]{.bold1}

The →[[COMMON GOOD]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos546593) is
best served where the good of the individual person and of the smaller
units of society (for instance, the family) is central. The individual
and the smaller social unit need to be protected and promoted by the
stronger power of State institutions.

[328]{.bold1} [What can the individual contribute to the common
good]{.italic}?

[Working for the →[[COMMON
GOOD]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos546593) means assuming
responsibility for others. \[1913-1917, 1926\]]{.bold1}

The common good must be the business of everyone. This happens first of
all when men get involved in their particular surroundings---family,
neighborhood, workplace---and take responsibility. It is important also
to exercise social and political responsibility. Someone who assumes
this sort of responsibility, however, wields power and is always in
danger of misusing that power. Therefore, everyone in a position of
responsibility is called upon to engage in an ongoing process of
conversion, so that he can exercise that responsibility for others in
lasting justice and charity.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[No one can claim, as Cain did, that he
is not responsible for the fate of his brother.]{.small}

[Pope John Paul II]{.small}

[(1920-2005)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Respect the good reputation of your
enemies.]{.small}

[ST. JOHN VIANNEY]{.small}

[(1786-1859)]{.small}

[329 ]{.bold1}[How does social justice come about in a
society]{.italic}?

[Social justice comes about where the inalienable dignity of every
person is respected and the resulting rights are safeguarded and
championed without reservation. Among these is also the right to active
participation in the political, economic, and cultural life of the
society. \[1928-1933, 1943-1944\]]{.bold1}

The basis of all justice is respect for the inalienable dignity of the
human person, "whose defense and promotion have been entrusted to us by
the Creator, and to whom the men and women at every moment of history
are strictly and responsibly in debt" (Pope John Paul II, Encyclical
[Sollicitudo rei socialis]{.italic}, published 1987). Human rights are
an immediate consequence of human dignity, and no State can abolish or
change them. States and authorities that trample these rights underfoot
are unjust regimes and lose their authority. A society is not perfected
by laws, however, but rather by love of neighbor, which makes it
possible for everyone to "look upon his neighbor (without any exception)
as 'another self' " (GS 27, 1).
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[280](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos487812)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["As you did it to one of the least of
these my brethren, you did it to me."]{.small}

[Mt 25:40]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[There is not, never has been, and never
will be a single person for whom Christ did not suffer.]{.small}

[Council of Quiercy, a.d. 853]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[\[All\] men are to deal with their
fellows in justice and civility.]{.small}

[Second Vatican Council,\
[Dignitatis humanae]{.italic} \[DH\]]{.small}

[330 ]{.bold1}[To what extent are all men equal in God's
sight]{.italic}?

[All men are equal in God's sight insofar as all have the same Creator,
all were created in the same image of God with a rational soul, and all
have the same Redeemer. \[1934-1935, 1945\]]{.bold1}

Because all men are equal in God's sight, every person possesses the
same dignity and has a claim to the same human rights. Hence every kind
of social, racist, sexist, cultural, or religious discrimination against
a person is an unacceptable injustice.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[God says: I have willed that one should
need another and that all should be my ministers in distributing the
graces and gifts they have received from me.]{.small}

[ST. CATHERINE OF SIENA\
(1347-1380)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Nothing is really ours until we share
it.]{.small}

[C. S. LEWIS\
(1898-1963)]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["He who has two coats, let him share
with him who has none; and he who has food, let him do
likewise."]{.small}

[Lk 3:11]{.small}

![](images/00059.jpg){.calibre4}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The Creator has inscribed on our being
the "natural law", which is the reflection in our hearts of his plan for
creation, as the guide and inner standard for our life.]{.small}

[POPE BENEDICT XVI,\
May 27, 2006]{.small}

[331]{.bold1} [Why is there nevertheless injustice among men]{.italic}?

[All men have the same dignity, but not all of them meet with the same
living conditions. In cases where injustice is man-made, it contradicts
the Gospel. In cases where men have been endowed by God with different
gifts and talents, God is asking us to rely on one another: in charity
one should make up for what the other lacks. \[1936-1938,
1946-1947\]]{.bold1}

There is a kind of inequality among men that does not come from God but
rather originates in societal conditions, especially in the unjust
distribution of raw materials, land, and capital worldwide. God expects
us to remove from the world everything that is plainly contrary to the
Gospel and disregards human dignity. Yet there is another sort of
inequality among men that is quite in keeping with God's will:
inequality in talents, initial conditions, and opportunities. These are
an indication that being human means being there for others in charity
so as to share and to promote life.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[61](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos125897)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Love the poor, and do not turn your
back on them, for if you turn your back on the poor, you turn your back
on Christ. He made himself hungry, naked, homeless, so that you and I
would have an opportunity to love him.]{.small}

[BL. TERESA OF CALCUTTA]{.small}

[(1910-1997)]{.small}

[332 ]{.bold1}[How is the solidarity of Christians with other people
expressed]{.italic}?

[Christians are committed to just societal structures. Part of this is
universal access to the material, intellectual, and spiritual goods of
this world. Christians also make sure that the dignity of human work is
respected, which includes a just wage. Handing on the faith is also an
act of solidarity with all mankind. \[1939-1942, 1948\]]{.bold1}

Solidarity is the practical hallmark of a Christian. Practicing
solidarity is not just a command of reason. Jesus Christ, our Lord,
identified completely with the poor and the lowly (Mt 25:40). To refuse
solidarity with them would be to reject Christ.

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[PRINCIPLE OF
SOLIDARITY]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Latin [solidus]{.italic} = thick, firm, strong): a principle of
Catholic social teaching that aims at strengthening community and
promoting a "civilization of love" (Pope John Paul II).]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[NATURAL MORAL LAW]{.bold1}]{.small}

["In all cultures there are examples of ethical convergence, some
isolated, some interrelated, as an expression of the one human nature,
willed by the Creator; the tradition of ethical wisdom knows this as the
natural law" (Pope Benedict XVI, Encyclical [Caritas in
veritate]{.italic}).]{.small}

[333 ]{.bold1}[Is there a natural law that everyone can know]{.italic}?

[If people are to do good and avoid evil, certainty about what is good
or evil must be inscribed within them. In fact there is such a moral law
that is, so to speak, "natural" to men and can be known in principle by
every person by reason. \[1949-1960, 1975, 1978-1979\]]{.bold1}

The natural moral law is valid for everyone. It tells men what
fundamental rights and duties they have and thus forms the real
foundation for life together in the family, in society, and in the
State. Because our natural knowledge is often troubled by sin and human
weakness, a person needs God's help and his
→[[REVELATION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos28374) in order to
stay on the right path.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[God wrote on the tables of the Law what
men did not read in their hearts.]{.small}

[ST. AUGUSTINE (354-430)]{.small}

[334]{.bold1} [What connection is there between the "natural moral law"
and the Law of the Old Covenant]{.italic}?

[The Law of the Old Covenant expresses truths that by nature are evident
to human reason yet are now proclaimed and authenticated as God's Law.
\[1961-1963, 1981\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The Law is a pedagogy and a prophecy of
things to come.]{.small}

[ST. IRENAEUS OF LYONS\
(ca. 135-202)]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["Whoever then relaxes one of the least
of these commandments and teaches men so, shall be called least in the
kingdom of heaven; but he who does them and teaches them shall be called
great in the kingdom of heaven."]{.small}

[Mt 5:19]{.small}

[335 ]{.bold1}[What significance does the Law of the Old Covenant
have]{.italic}?

[In the Law (the Torah), and its centerpiece, the Ten Commandments (the
→[D[ECALOGUE]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos581630)), the will
of God is manifested to the people of Israel; following the Torah is for
Israel the central way to salvation. Christians know that we can tell by
the Law what ought to be done. They also know, however, that it is not
the Law that saves us. \[1963-1964, 1981-1982\]]{.bold1}

Every man has the experience of finding that something good is, so to
speak, "prescribed". But one does not have the strength to accomplish
it; it is too difficult; one feels "helpless" (see Rom 8:3 and Rom
7:14-25). One sees the Law and feels that one has been handed over to
sin. And so precisely through the Law it becomes clear how urgently we
rely on inner strength in order to fulfill the Law. That is why the Law,
as good and important as it is, only prepares the way for faith in the
saving God.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[349](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos579685)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[You are a child of grace. If God gave
you grace, because he gave it freely, then you should love freely. Do
not love God for the sake of a reward; let God be your reward!]{.small}

[ST. AUGUSTINE\
(354-430)]{.small}

[336 ]{.bold1}[How did Jesus deal with the Law of the Old
Covenant]{.italic}?

["Do not think", says Jesus in the Sermon on the Mount, "that I have
come to abolish the law and the prophets; I have come not to abolish
them but to fulfil them" (Mt 5:17). \[1965-1972, 1977,
1983-1985\]]{.bold1}

Jesus, being a faithful Jew, lived according to the ethical ideas and
requirements of his time. But on a series of issues he departed from a
literal, merely formal interpretation of the Law.

[337]{.bold1} [How are we saved]{.italic}?

[No man can save himself. Christians believe that they are saved by God,
who for this purpose sent his Son Jesus Christ into the world. For us
salvation means that we are freed by the Holy Spirit from the power of
sin and have been brought back from the realm of death to a life without
end, a life in God's presence. \[1987-1995, 2017-2020\]]{.bold1}

Paul observes: "All have sinned and fall short of the glory of God" (Rom
3:23). Sin cannot exist in the presence of God, who is justice and
goodness through and through. If sin is worth nothing, what about the
sinner, then? In his love, God found a way by which he destroys sin but
saves the sinner. He makes him "right" again, that is to say, righteous
or [just]{.italic}. That is why from ancient times salvation has also
been called [justification]{.italic}. We are not made just by our own
power. A man can neither forgive his own sins nor rescue himself from
death. For that, God has to act on our behalf---out of mercy, not
because we could deserve or merit it. In Baptism, God grants us "the
righteousness of God through faith in Jesus Christ" (Rom 3:22). Through
the Holy Spirit, who is poured out into our hearts, we take part in the
death and Resurrection of Christ---we die to sin and are born to new
life in God. The divine gifts of faith, hope, and charity come over us
and make us able to live in the light and to obey God's will.

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[JUSTIFICATION]{.bold1}]{.small}

[A central concept from the "doctrine about grace". It means the
restoration of the right relation between God and man. Since only Jesus
Christ achieved this right relation ("righteousness"), we can come again
into God's presence only if we are "justified" by Christ and, so to
speak, enter into his intact relationship with God. To believe,
therefore, means to accept the righteousness of Jesus for oneself and
one's life.]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[For by grace you have been saved
through faith; and this is not your own doing, it is the gift of
God---not because of works, lest any man should boast.]{.small}

[Eph 2:8-9]{.small}

[338]{.bold1} [What is grace]{.italic}?

[By grace we mean God's free, loving gift to us, his helping goodness,
the vitality that comes from him. Through the Cross and Resurrection,
God devotes himself entirely to us and communicates himself to us in
grace. Grace is everything God grants us, without our deserving it in
the least. \[1996-1998, 2005, 2021\]]{.bold1}

"Grace", says Pope Benedict XVI, "is being looked upon by God, our being
touched by his love." Grace is not a thing, but rather God's
communication of himself to men. God never gives less than himself. In
grace we are in God.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[God never gives less than
himself.]{.small}

[ST. AUGUSTINE]{.small}

[(354-430)]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[What have you that you did not
receive?]{.small}

[1 Cor 4:7]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Everything is grace.]{.small}

[ST. THÉRÈSE OF LISIEUX]{.small}

[(1873-1897)]{.small}

[339 ]{.bold1}[What does God's grace do to us]{.italic}?

[God's grace brings us into the inner life of the Holy Trinity, into the
exchange of love between Father, Son, and Holy Spirit. It makes us
capable of living in God's love and of acting on the basis of this love.
\[1999-2000, 2003-2004, 2023-2024\]]{.bold1}

Grace is infused in us from above and cannot be explained in terms of
natural causes ([supernatural grace]{.italic}). It makes us---especially
through Baptism---children of God and heirs of heaven ([sanctifying or
deifying grace]{.italic}). It bestows on us a permanent disposition to
do good ([habitual grace]{.italic}). Grace helps us to know, to will,
and to do everything that leads us to what is good, to God, and to
heaven ([actual grace]{.italic}). Grace comes about in a special way in
the sacraments, which according to the will of our Savior are the
preeminent places for our encounter with God ([sacramental
grace]{.italic}). Grace is manifested also in special gifts of grace
that are granted to individual Christians
(→[[CHARISMS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos205015)) or in
special powers that are promised to those in the state of marriage, the
ordained state, or the religious state ([graces of state]{.italic}).

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[My past no longer concerns me. It
belongs to divine mercy. My future does not yet concern me. It belongs
to divine providence. What concerns me and what challenges me is today,
which belongs to God's grace and to the devotion of my heart and my good
will.]{.small}

[ST. FRANCIS DE SALES]{.small}

[(1567-1622)]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[And Mary said, "Behold, I am the
handmaid of the Lord; let it be \[fiat\] to me according to your
word."]{.small}

[Lk 1:38]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Since all have sinned and fall short of
the glory of God, they are justified by his grace as a gift, through the
redemption which is in Christ Jesus.]{.small}

[Rom 3:23-24]{.small}

[340 ]{.bold1}[How is God's grace related to our freedom]{.italic}?

[God's grace is freely bestowed on a person, and it seeks and summons
him to respond in complete freedom. Grace does not compel. God's love
wants our free assent. \[2001-2002, 2022\]]{.bold1}

One can also say No to the offer of grace. Grace, nevertheless, is not
something external or foreign to man; it is what he actually yearns for
in his deepest freedom. In moving us by his grace, God anticipates man's
free response.

[341 ]{.bold1}[Can someone earn heaven by good works]{.italic}?

[No. No man can gain heaven merely by his own efforts. The fact that we
are saved is God's grace, pure and simple, which nevertheless demands
the free cooperation of the individual. \[2006-2011,
2025-2027\]]{.bold1}

Although it is grace and faith through which we are saved, nevertheless,
our good works ought to show the love produced by God's action in us.

![](images/00060.jpg){.calibre4}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[As he who called you is holy, be holy
yourselves in all your conduct; since it is written, "You shall be holy,
for I am holy."]{.small}

[1 Pet 1:15-16]{.small}

[342]{.bold1} [Are we all supposed to become "saints"]{.italic}?

[Yes. The purpose of our life is to be united with God in love and to
correspond entirely to God's wishes. We should allow God "to live his
life in us" (Mother Teresa). That is what it means to be holy: a
"saint". \[2012-2016, 2028-2029\]]{.bold1}

Every man asks himself the question: Who am I and why am I here, how do
I find myself? Faith answers: Only in
→[[HOLINESS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos239756) does man
become that for which God created him. Only in holiness does man find
real harmony between himself and his Creator. Holiness, however, is not
some sort of self-made perfection; rather, it is union with the
incarnate love that is Christ. Anyone who gains new life in this way
finds himself and becomes holy.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Holiness is not the luxury of a few
people, but a simple duty for you and me.]{.small}

[BL. TERESA OF CALCUTTA]{.small}

[(1910-1997)]{.small}

[[CHAPTER THREE]{.bold1}]{.small}

The Church

[343 ]{.bold1}[How does the Church help us to lead a good, responsible
life]{.italic}?

[In the Church we are baptized. In the Church we receive the faith that
the Church has preserved intact down through the centuries. In the
Church we hear the living Word of God and learn how we must live if we
want to please God. Through the
→[[SACRAMENTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos309200) that Jesus
entrusted to his disciples, the Church builds us up, strengthens, and
consoles us. In the Church there is the blazing fire of the saints, by
which our hearts are kindled. In the Church the Holy
→[E[UCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475) is
celebrated, in which Christ's sacrifice and strength are renewed for us
in such a way that, united with him, we become his Body and live by his
strength. Despite all her human weaknesses, apart from the Church no one
can be a Christian. \[2030-2031, 2047\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[To love Christ is the same thing as to
love the Church.]{.small}

[BROTHER ROGER SCHUTZ]{.small}

[(1915-2005)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Even today the Church gives me Jesus.
That says it all. What would I know about him, what connection would
there be between him and me without the Church?]{.small}

[HENRI CARDINAL DE LUBAC, S.J.\
(1896-1961, French theologian)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Do you want to come to believe but do
not know the way? Learn from those who were tormented by doubts before
you. Imitate their way of acting, do everything that the faith requires,
as though you were already a believer. Attend Mass, use holy water, and
so on. That will no doubt make you simple and lead you to
faith.]{.small}

[BLAISE PASCAL\
(1588-1651)]{.small}

[344 ]{.bold1}[Why does the Church also make declarations about ethical
questions and about matters of personal conduct]{.italic}?

[Believing is a path. One learns how to stay on this path, in other
words, how to act rightly and to lead a good life, only by following the
instructions in the Gospel. The teaching authority
(→[M[AGISTERIUM]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos40776)) of the
Church must remind people also about the demands of the natural moral
law. \[2032-2040, 2049-2051\]]{.bold1}

There are not two truths. What is humanly right cannot be wrong from the
Christian perspective. And what is right according to Christianity
cannot be humanly wrong. That is why the Church must teach
comprehensively about moral issues.

[345]{.bold1} [What are the "Five Precepts of the Church"]{.italic}?

[(1) You shall attend Mass on Sunday and holy days of obligation and
abstain from work or activities that offend against the character of the
day. (2) You shall receive the sacrament of Penance at least once a
year. (3) You shall receive the
→[E[UCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475) at least
during the Easter season. (4) You shall observe the prescribed seasons
of fasting and days of abstinence (Ash Wednesday and Good Friday). (5)
You shall contribute to the material support of the Church.
\[2042-2043\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Nothing may have priority over the
liturgy.]{.small}

[ST. BENEDICT OF NURSIA\
(ca. 480-547)]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[HYPOCRISY]{.bold1}]{.small}

[Implies a double standard in the morality that one practices publicly
or implicitly. Externally a hypocritical person advocates goals and
values that he does not respect privately. "Little children, let us not
love in word or speech but in deed and in truth" (1 Jn 3:18).]{.small}

[346]{.bold1} [What is the purpose of the precepts of the Church, and
how binding are they]{.italic}?

[The "Five Precepts of the Church" with their minimum requirements are
supposed to remind us that one cannot be a Christian without making a
moral effort, without participating personally in the sacramental life
of the Church, and without union with her in solidarity. They are
obligatory for every Catholic Christian. \[2041, 2048\]]{.bold1}

[347]{.bold1} [Why is "not practicing what you preach" such a serious
deficiency in a Christian]{.italic}?

[Agreement between one's life and one's witness is the first requirement
for proclaiming the Gospel. Not practicing what you profess is therefore
→[[HYPOCRISY]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos575210), a betrayal
of the Christian duty to be "salt of the earth" and "light of the
world". \[2044-2046\]]{.bold1}

Paul was the one who reminded the Church in Corinth: "You show that you
are a letter from Christ . . . written not with ink but with the Spirit
of the living God, not on tablets of stone but on tablets of human
hearts" (2 Cor 3:3). Christians themselves, not the things they say, are
Christ's "letters of recommendation" (2 Cor 3:2) to the world. It is all
the more devastating, therefore, when there are even
→[[PRIESTS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_011.html_filepos257464) and religious
who abuse children. They not only commit unspeakable crimes against
their victims. They deprive many people of hope in God and extinguish
the light of faith in quite a few souls.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The world is full of people who preach
water and drink wine.]{.small}

[GIOVANNI GUARESCHI\
(1908-1968, Italian author of [The Little World of Don
Camillo]{.italic})]{.small}

[[SECTION TWO]{.bold1}]{.small}

The Ten Commandments

[348 ]{.bold1}["Teacher, what . . . must I do to have eternal life?" (Mt
19:16)]{.italic}

[Jesus says, "If you would enter life, keep the commandments" (Mt
19:17). Then he adds, "and come, follow me" (Mt 19:21). \[2052-2054,
2075-2076\]]{.bold1}

Christianity is more than a correct life and keeping the commandments.
Being a Christian is a living relationship to Jesus. A Christian unites
himself deeply and personally with the Lord and with him sets out on the
way that leads to true life.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Christ does not want admirers but
followers.]{.small}

[SØREN KIERKEGAARD\
(1813-1855)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Most people do not suspect what god
could make out of them if they would only place themselves at his
disposal.]{.small}

[ST. IGNATIUS LOYOLA]{.small}

[(1491-1556)]{.small}

[The Ten Commandments as printed nearby are not found in precisely that
form in Sacred Scripture; the text is based on two biblical sources: Ex
20:2-17 and Deut 5:6-21. Centuries ago the two sources were summarized
for instructional purposes, and the Ten Commandments were presented to
the faithful in the present form of the catechetical tradition.]{.small}

[349 ]{.bold1}[What are the Ten Commandments]{.italic}?

[1. I am the Lord your God: you shall not have strange Gods before
me.]{.bold1}

[2. You shall not take the name of the Lord your God in vain.]{.bold1}

[3. Remember to keep holy the Lord's day (→
[S[ABBATH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos604635)).]{.bold1}

[4. Honor your father and your mother.]{.bold1}

[5. You shall not kill.]{.bold1}

[6. You shall not commit adultery.]{.bold1}

[7. You shall not steal.]{.bold1}

[8. You shall not bear false witness against your neighbor.]{.bold1}

[9. You shall not covet your neighbor's wife.]{.bold1}

[10. You shall not covet your neighbor's goods.]{.bold1}

[350 ]{.bold1}[Are the Ten Commandments a random list]{.italic}?

[No. The Ten Commandments form a unity. One commandment refers to
another. You cannot arbitrarily toss out individual commandments.
Someone who breaks one commandment is violating the whole Law. \[2069,
2079\]]{.bold1}

What is remarkable about the Ten Commandments is that all of human life
is included within them. Indeed, we men are related at the same time to
God (Commandments 1-3) and to our fellow men (Commandments 4-10); we are
religious and social beings.

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[DECALOGUE]{.bold1}]{.small}

[("Ten Words", from Greek [deka]{.italic} = ten and [logos]{.italic} =
word). The Ten Commandments are the central summary of the basic rules
of human behavior in the Old Testament. Jews and Christians both look to
this fundamental text for orientation.]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[We love, because he first loved
us.]{.small}

[1 Jn 4:19]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Where is God? How can we love him? It
is not enough to say, "My God, I love you!" We love God in this world by
giving something up, by giving something away. Of course I can eat the
sugar myself, but I can also give it away.]{.small}

[BL. TERESA OF CALCUTTA]{.small}

[(1910-1997)]{.small}

[351 ]{.bold1}[Aren't the Ten Commandments outmoded]{.italic}?

[No, the Ten Commandments are by no means the product of a particular
time. They express man's fundamental obligations toward God and
neighbor, which are always and everywhere valid. \[2070-2072\]]{.bold1}

The Ten Commandments are commandments of reason just as they are also
part of the binding
→[[REVELATION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos28374) of God.
They are so fundamentally binding that no one can be dispensed from
keeping these commandments.

[[CHAPTER ONE]{.bold1}]{.small}

"You Shall Love the Lord Your God\
with All Your Heart, and with All Your Soul,\
and with All Your Mind"

[[THE FIRST COMMANDMENT:]{.bold1}]{.small}

[I am the Lord, your God. You shall not have strange Gods before
me.]{.small}

[352]{.bold1} [What is the meaning of the commandment, "I am the Lord,
your God" (Ex 20:2]{.italic})?

[Because the Almighty has revealed himself to us as our God and Lord, we
must not place anything above him or consider anything more important or
give any other thing or person priority over him. To know God and to
serve and worship him has absolute priority in our life. \[2083-2094,
2133-2134\]]{.bold1}

God expects us to give him our full [faith]{.italic}; we should place
all our [hope]{.italic} in him and direct all the strength of our
[love]{.italic} toward him. The commandment to love God is the most
important of all commandments and the key to all the others. That is why
it stands at the beginning of the Ten Commandments.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Where God is made great, men and women
are not made small: there too men and women become great and the world
is filled with light.]{.small}

[POPE BENEDICT XVI,\
September 11, 2006]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[By "praying" I do not mean the
recitation of prayers that have been \[earned by heart, but rather
simple worship, with or without words; remaining at God's feet for the
purpose and with the intention of worshipping him. Man cannot continue
without worshipping something.]{.small}

[FYODOR M. DOSTOYEVSKY\
(1821-1881)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Bread is important, freedom is more
important, but most important is unbroken fidelity and unadulterated
worship.]{.small}

[FR. ALFRED DELP, S.J.\
(1907-1945), before his execution by the Nazis]{.small}

![](images/00061.jpg){.calibre4}

[353]{.bold1} [Why do we worship God]{.italic}?

[We worship God because he exists and because reverence and worship are
the appropriate response to his revelation and his presence. "You shall
worship the Lord your God and him only shall you serve" (Mt
4:10).\[2095-2105, 2135-2136\]]{.bold1}

Worshipping God, however, is also beneficial to men, for it frees them
from servitude to the powers of this world. When God is no longer
worshipped and when he is no longer thought to be Lord over life and
death, others assume that position and put human dignity at risk.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[485](#YOUCAT_split_021.html_filepos791876)

[354]{.bold1} [Can people be forced to believe in God]{.italic}?

[No. No one may force others to believe, not even one's own children,
just as no one may be forced to be an unbeliever. A person can make the
decision to believe only in complete freedom. Christians, however, are
called to help other people, by word and example, to find the way to
faith. \[2104-2109, 2137\]]{.bold1}

Pope John Paul II said, "Proclaiming Christ and bearing witness to him,
when done in a way that respects consciences, does not violate freedom.
Faith demands a free adherence on the part of man, but at the same time
faith must also be offered to him" (Encyclical [Redemptoris
missio]{.italic}, 1990, no. 8).

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[We should fear God out of love and not
love him out of fear.]{.small}

[ST. FRANCIS DE SALES\
(1567-1622)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[We impose our faith on no one. Such
proselytism is contrary to Christianity. Faith can develop only in
freedom. But we do appeal to the freedom of men and women to open their
hearts to God, to seek him, to hear his voice.]{.small}

[POPE BENEDICT XVI,]{.small}

[September 10, 2006]{.small}

[355]{.bold1} "[You shall not have strange Gods before me." What does
that mean]{.italic}?

[This commandment forbids us:]{.bold1}

[• to adore other gods and pagan deities or to worship an earthly idol
or to devote oneself entirely to some earthly good (money, influence,
success, beauty, youth, and so on)]{.bold1}

[• to be superstitious, which means to adhere to esoteric, magic, or
occult or New Age practices or to get involved with fortune telling or
spiritualism, instead of believing in God's power, providence, and
→[[BLESSINGS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos306451)]{.bold1}

[• to provoke God by word or deed]{.bold1}

[• to commit a
→[[SACRILEGE]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos589873)]{.bold1}

[• to acquire spiritual power through corruption and to desecrate what
is holy through trafficking (simony). \[2110-2128, 2138-2140\]]{.bold1}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[PROSELYTISM]{.small}]{.bold1}

[(from Greek [proserchomai]{.italic} = to arrive): the exploitation of
the intellectual or physical poverty of others to draw them over to
one's own faith.]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[SACRILEGE]{.small}]{.bold1}

[(from Latin [sacrilegium]{.italic} = plundering of a temple): the
theft, profanation, or desecration of something sacred.]{.small}

[356]{.bold1} [Is esotericism as found, for example in New Age beliefs,
compatible with the Christian faith]{.italic}?

[No. →[E[SOTERICISM]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos593086)
ignores the reality of God. God is a personal Being; he is love and the
origin of life, not some cold cosmic energy. Man was willed and created
by God, but man himself is not divine; rather, he is a creature that is
wounded by sin, threatened by death, and in need of redemption. Whereas
most proponents of esotericism assume that man can redeem himself,
Christians believe that only Jesus Christ and God's grace redeem them.
Nor are nature and the cosmos God
(→[[PANTHEISM]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos594420)). Rather,
the Creator, even though he loves us immensely, is infinitely greater
and unlike anything he has created. \[2110-2128\]]{.bold1}

Many people today practice yoga for health reasons, enroll in a
→[[MEDITATION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_021.html_filepos822377) course so
as to become more calm and collected, or attend dance workshops so as to
experience their bodies in a new way. These techniques are not always
harmless. Often they are vehicles for doctrines that are foreign to
Christianity. No reasonable person should hold an irrational world view,
in which people can tap magical powers or harness mysterious spirits and
the "initiated" have a secret knowledge that is withheld from the
"ignorant". In ancient Israel, the surrounding peoples' beliefs in gods
and spirits were exposed as false. God alone is Lord; there is no god
besides him. Nor is there any (magical) technique by which one can
capture or charm "the divine", force one's wishes on the universe, or
redeem oneself. Much about these esoteric beliefs and practices is
→[[SUPERSTITION]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos592203) or
→[[OCCULTISM]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos595019).

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[SUPERSTITION]{.small}]{.bold1}

[The irrational assumption that certain sayings, actions, events, and
objects contain magic powers or produce magical effects.]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Praised be the Lord, who has redeemed
me from myself!]{.small}

[ST. TERESA OF AVILA]{.small}

[(1515-1582)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[No one denies God unless he has some
reason for wanting him not to exist.]{.small}

[ST. AUGUSTINE]{.small}

[(354-430)]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[ESOTERICISM]{.small}]{.bold1}

[(Greek [esoterikos]{.italic} = the inner circle, something into which
one must be initiated in order to understand it): since the nineteenth
century a common collective term for spiritual teachings and practices
in which man is led to a supposedly "true knowledge", which nonetheless
is already in him. On the other hand, a revelation in which God
manifests himself to man from outside is something foreign to esoteric
thinking.]{.small}

[357]{.bold1} [Is atheism always a sin against the First
Commandment]{.italic}?

[→[A[THEISM]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos596850) is not a sin
if a person has learned nothing about God or has examined the question
about God's existence conscientiously and cannot believe.
\[2127-2128\]]{.bold1}

The line between being unable to believe and being unwilling to believe
is not clear. The attitude that simply dismisses faith as unimportant,
without having examined it more closely, is often worse than
well-considered → [ATHEISM]{.small}.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[5](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos25573)

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[PANTHEISM]{.small}]{.bold1}

[(Greek [pan]{.italic} = all, [theos]{.italic} = God): the world view
that nothing exists except God; accordingly, everything that exists is
God, and God is everything that exists. This doctrine is incompatible
with the Christian faith.]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Unbelievers are the most credulous of
all.]{.small}

[BLAISE PASCAL\
(1588-1651)]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[OCCULTISM]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(Latin [occultus]{.italic} = hidden, secret): a collective term for
teachings and practices through which man supposedly acquires power over
his destiny, matter, and surroundings. Examples of occult practices are
Ouija boards, astrology, and clairvoyance.]{.small}

[358]{.bold1} [Why does the Old Testament forbid images of God, and why
do we Christians no longer keep that commandment]{.italic}?

[In order to protect the mystery of God and to set the people of Israel
apart from the idolatrous practices of the pagans, the First Commandment
said, "You shall not make for yourself a graven image" (Ex 20:4).
However, since God himself acquired a human face in Jesus Christ, the
prohibition against images was repealed in Christianity; in the Eastern
Church, →[[ICONS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos597815) are
even regarded as sacred. \[2129-2132, 2141\]]{.bold1}

The knowledge of the patriarchs of Israel that God surpasses everything
(→[[TRANSCENDENCE]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos597561)) and
is much greater than anything in the world lives on today in Judaism as
in Islam, where no image of God is or ever was allowed. In Christianity,
in light of Christ's life on earth, the prohibition against images was
mitigated from the fourth century on and was abolished at the Second
Council of Nicaea (787). By his Incarnation, God is no longer absolutely
unimaginable; after Jesus we can picture what he is like: "He who has
seen me has seen the Father" (Jn 14:9).
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[9](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos30435)

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[ATHEISM]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(Greek [theos]{.italic} = God): the view that God does not exist. A
general term for various forms of denying God's existence in theory or
in practice.]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[AGNOSTICISM]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(Greek [gnosis]{.italic} = knowledge): the view that God cannot be
known. A general term for a position that leaves open the question about
God's existence because it supposedly cannot be decided or because God
cannot be known with certainty.]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[TRANSCENDENT]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Latin [transcendere]{.italic} = to go beyond): surpassing sensory
experience; other-worldly.]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[ICON]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(from Greek [ikona]{.italic} = image): An icon is a sacred image in the
Eastern Church that is painted according to venerable patterns by an
artist who is praying and fasting; it is supposed to produce a mystical
union between the observer and what is depicted (Christ, angels,
saints).]{.small}

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Blessed be the name of the Lord from
this time forth and for evermore!]{.small}

[Ps 113:2]{.small}

![](images/00062.jpg){.calibre4}

[[THE SECOND COMMANDMENT:]{.bold1}]{.small}

You Shall Not Take the Name of the Lord Your God in Vain.

[359]{.bold1} [Why does God want us to "hallow" his name (that is, keep
it holy]{.italic})?

[To tell someone your name is a sign of trust. Since God has told us his
name, he makes himself recognizable and grants us access to him through
this name. God is absolute truth. Someone who calls Truth himself by his
name but uses it to testify to a lie sins seriously. \[2142-2155,
2160-2164\]]{.bold1}

One must not pronounce the name of God irreverently. For we know him
only because he has entrusted himself to us. The Holy Name, after all,
is the key to the heart of the Almighty. Therefore it is a terrible
offense to blaspheme God, to curse using God's name, or to make false
promises in his name. The Second Commandment is therefore also a
commandment that protects "holiness" in general. Places, things, names,
and people who have been touched by God are "holy". Sensitivity to what
is holy is called reverence.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[31](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos75435)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Reverence is the pole on which the
world turns.]{.small}

[JOHANN WOLFGANG VON GOETHE]{.small}

[(1749-1832, German poet)]{.small}

[360 ]{.bold1}[What is the meaning of the Sign of the Cross]{.italic}?

[Through the Sign of the Cross we place ourselves under the protection
of the Triune God. \[2157, 2166\]]{.bold1}

At the beginning of the day, at the beginning of a prayer, but also at
the beginning of important undertakings, a Christian makes the Sign of
the Cross over himself and thus starts his business "in the name of the
Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit". We are surrounded on all
sides by the Triune God; calling upon him by name sanctifies the things
we set out to do; it obtains
→[[BLESSINGS]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos306451) for us and
strengthens us in difficulties and temptations.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Let us not be ashamed to profess the
Crucified One; let us confidently seal our forehead with our fingers,
let us make the Sign of the Cross on everything, on the bread we eat and
over the cup we drink. Let us make this sign as we come and go, before
sleeping, when we lie down and when we arise, while traveling and while
resting.]{.small}

[ST. CYRIL OF JERUSALEM\
(ca. 313-386/387)]{.small}

[361 ]{.bold1}[What does it mean for a Christian to be baptized with a
particular name]{.italic}?

["In the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit", the
person is baptized with a name. The name and the face are ultimately
what make a person unique, even in God's sight. "Fear not, for I have
redeemed you; I have called you by name, you are mine" (Is 43:1).
\[2158\]]{.bold1}

Christians treat the name of a person reverently, because the name is
profoundly connected with that person's identity and dignity. From time
immemorial Christians have selected names for their children from the
list of the saints; they do so in the belief that the patron saint is an
example for them and will intercede with God for them in a special way.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[201](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos354300)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}["I will not blot his name out of the
book of life; I will confess his name before my Father and before his
angels."]{.small}

[Rev 3:5]{.small}

[[THE THIRD COMMANDMENT:]{.small}]{.bold1}

Remember to Keep Holy the Lord's Day.

[362]{.bold1} [Why do Jews celebrate the Sabbath]{.italic}?

[The →[[SABBATH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos604635) is for
the people of Israel the great sign commemorating God, the Creator and
Redeemer. \[2168-2172, 2189\]]{.bold1}

The →[[SABBATH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos604635) recalls
in the first place the seventh day of creation, when God "rested, and
was refreshed" (Ex 31:17), this, so to speak, authorizes all men to
interrupt their work and replenish their energies. Even slaves were
supposed to be allowed to observe the Sabbath. This recalls the second
great commemorative sign, the liberation of Israel from slavery in
Egypt: "You shall remember that you \[yourself\] were a servant in the
land of Egypt . . ." (Dt 5:15). The Sabbath is therefore a feast of
human freedom; on the Sabbath all breathe freely; on it the division of
the world into masters and slaves is abolished. In traditional Judaism
this day of freedom and rest is also a sort of foretaste of the world to
come.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[47](#YOUCAT_split_010.html_filepos103899)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Remember the sabbath day, to keep it
holy . . . In it you shall not do any work, you, or your son, or your
daughter, your manservant, or your maidservant, or your cattle, or the
sojourner who is within your gates.]{.small}

[Ex 20:8, 10]{.small}

![](images/00004.jpg){.calibre4}[[SABBATH]{.bold1}]{.small}

[(Hebrew, approximately "break for rest"): the Jewish day of rest
commemorating the seventh day of creation and the Exodus from Egypt. It
begins on Friday evening and ends on Saturday evening. In Orthodox
Judaism it is observed with a host of rules for maintaining the Sabbath
rest.]{.small}

[363]{.bold1} [How does Jesus deal with the Sabbath]{.italic}?

[Jesus observes the
→[S[ABBATH]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos604635), but at the
same time he deals with it very liberally, as one who has complete
command over it: "The sabbath was made for man, not man for the sabbath"
(Mk 2:27). \[2173\]]{.bold1}

The fact that Jesus claims the right to heal on the Sabbath and to
interpret the Sabbath laws mercifully poses a dilemma for his Jewish
contemporaries: Either Jesus is the Messiah sent by God, which makes him
"Lord even of the sabbath" (Mk 2:28), or else he is merely a man, in
which case his actions on the Sabbath are a sin against the Law.

[364 ]{.bold1}[Why do Christians replace the Sabbath with
Sunday]{.italic}?

[Christians replaced the celebration of the Sabbath with the celebration
of Sunday because Jesus Christ rose from the dead on a Sunday. The
"Lord's Day", however, does include elements of the Sabbath.
\[2174-2176, 2190-2191\]]{.bold1}

The Christian Sunday has three essential elements: (1) It recalls the
creation of the world and communicates the festive splendor of God's
goodness to the passage of time. (2) It recalls the "eighth day of
creation", when the world was made new in Christ (thus a prayer from the
Easter Vigil says: "You have wonderfully created man and even more
wonderfully restored him."). (3) It includes the theme of rest, not just
to sanctify the interruption of work, but to point even now toward man's
eternal rest in God.

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[If pagans call it the "day of the sun",
we willingly agree, for today the light of the world is raised, today is
revealed the sun of justice with healing in his rays.]{.small}

[ST. JEROME\
(347-419)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[That is the difference between animals
and man: the latter has a Sunday outfit, too.]{.small}

[MARTIN LUTHER\
(1483-1546)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[Without Sunday we cannot live.]{.small}

[The Christian MARTYRS OF ABITENE, before they were executed in 304 by
Emperor Diocletian because they had opposed his ban on the celebration
of Sunday.]{.small}

[365 ]{.bold1}[How do Christians make Sunday "the Lord's day"]{.italic}?

[A Catholic Christian attends Holy Mass on Sunday or on the vigil of
Sunday. On that day he refrains from all work that would prevent him
from worshipping God or disturb the festive, joyful, restful, and
restorative character of the day. \[2177-2186, 2192-2193\]]{.bold1}

Since Sunday is an Easter celebration that occurs each week, Christians
from the earliest times have gathered together on that day to celebrate
and thank their Redeemer and to reunite themselves with him and with
others who are redeemed. So it is a central duty of every conscientious
Catholic Christian to "keep holy" Sunday and the other holy days of the
Church. One is exempted from it only by urgent family duties and
important responsibilities in society. Because participation in the
Sunday →[E[UCHARIST]{.small}](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos366475) is
fundamental for a Christian life, the Church explicitly declares that it
is a serious sin to stay away from Sunday Mass without good reason.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[219](#YOUCAT_split_014.html_filepos394977),
[345](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos574273)

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[People used to say: Give your soul a
Sunday! Now they say: Give your Sunday a soul!]{.small}

[PETER ROSEGGER]{.small}

[(1843-1918, Austrian writer)]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[What does Sunday cost us? The very
question is already a decisive attack on Sunday. For Sunday is Sunday
precisely because it costs nothing and brings in nothing, in the
economic sense. The question of what preserving it as a work-free day
"costs" presupposes that we have already conceptually turned Sunday into
a workday.]{.small}

[ROBERT SPAEMANN]{.small}

[(b. 1927, German philosopher)]{.small}

[366]{.bold1} [Why is it important for the State to preserve
Sunday]{.italic}?

[Sunday is a genuine service to the good of society, because it is a
sign of opposition to the total absorption of man by the working world.
\[2188, 2192-2193\]]{.bold1}

Therefore in lands that have a Christian character, Christians not only
demand the governmental preservation of Sunday, they also do not ask
others to do work that they themselves do not want to do on Sunday.
Everyone in creation should take part in this "breather".

[[CHAPTER TWO]{.bold1}]{.small}

"You Shall Love Your Neighbor as Yourself"

[[THE FOURTH COMMANDMENT:]{.bold1}]{.small}

Honor your father and your mother.

[367]{.bold1} [To whom does the Fourth Commandment refer, and what does
it require of us]{.italic}?

[The Fourth Commandment refers in the first place to one's physical
parents, but also to the people to whom we owe our life, our well-being,
our security, and our faith. \[2196-2200, 2247-2248\]]{.bold1}

What we owe in the first place to our parents---namely love, gratitude,
and respect---should also govern our relations to people who guide us
and are there for us. There are many people who represent for us a
God-given, natural, and good authority: foster or step-parents, older
relatives and ancestors, educators, teachers, employers, superiors. In
the spirit of the Fourth Commandment we should do them justice. In the
broadest sense, this commandment applies even to our duties as citizens
to the State.
![](images/00005.jpg){.calibre4}[325](#YOUCAT_split_017.html_filepos544336)

![](images/00002.jpg){.calibre4}[Honor your father and your mother, that
your days may be long in the land which the Lord your God gives
you.]{.small}

[Ex 20:12]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The Life of the parents is the book
which the children read.]{.small}

[ST. AUGUSTINE\
(354-430)]{.small}

![](images/00063.jpg){.calibre4}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The family is a necessary good for
peoples, an indispensable foundation for society and a great and
lifelong treasure for couples. It is a unique good for children, who are
meant to be the fruit of the love, of the total and generous self-giving
of their parents.]{.small}

[POPE BENEDICT XVI,\
July 8, 2006]{.small}

![](images/00003.jpg){.calibre4}[The family that prays together, stays
together.]{.small}

[FATHER PATRICK PEYTON, C.S.C.\
Irish priest, promoter of the Rosary]{.small}

[368]{.bold1} [What place does the family have in God's plan of
creation]{.italic}?

[A man and a woman who are married to each other form, together with
their children, a family. God wills that the love of the spouses, if
possible, should produce children. These children, who are entrusted to
the protection and care of their parents, have the same dignity as their
parents. \[2201-2206, 2249\]]{.bold1}

God himself, in the depths of the Trinity, is communion. In the human
sphere, the family is the primordial image of communion. The family is
the unique school of living in relationships. Nowhere do children grow
up as well as in an intact family, in which they experience heartfelt
affection, mutual respect, and responsibility for one another. Finally,
faith grows in the family, too; the family is, the Church tells us, a
